\displaystyle>h^{2}\|b(x)\|^{2}L_{R}(2\frac{\sinh(\sqrt{-K_{\min}})}{\sqrt{-K_{\min}}}+4\frac{\cosh(\sqrt{-K_{\min}})-1}{-K_{\min}}L_{\nabla})

\displaystyle>h^{2}\|b(x)\|^{2}L_{R}(2\frac{\sinh(\sqrt{-K_{\min}})}{\sqrt{-K_{\min}}}+4\frac{\cosh(\sqrt{-K_{\min}})-1}{-K_{\min}}hL_{\nabla}).

If
h\leq\frac{1}{4L_{\nabla}}
, then
1-hL_{\nabla}\geq\frac{3}{4}
.
The value
h\leq\sqrt{\frac{3}{4\|b(x)\|^{2}L_{R}(2\frac{\sinh(\sqrt{-K_{\min}})}{\sqrt{-K_{\min}}}+4\frac{\cosh(\sqrt{-K_{\min}})-1}{-K_{\min}}L_{\nabla})}}

suffices.

When
K_{\min}=0
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\|Y(t)\|dt\leq

\displaystyle\|v^{\parallel}\|+\frac{1}{2}\|\omega^{\parallel}\|+\frac{1}{2}\|\omega^{\perp}\|+\|v^{\perp}\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|v\|\left(2+hL_{\nabla}\right)=:C_{=}.

Then we have

\displaystyle\|Y(1)-v-h\,\nabla_{v}b(x)\|\leq h^{2}\|b(x)\|^{2}L_{R}C_{=}.

By triangle inequality,

\displaystyle\|Y(1)\|

\displaystyle\geq\|v+h\nabla_{v}b(x)\|-\|Y(1)-v-h\nabla_{v}b(x)\|

\displaystyle\geq\|v\|-h\|\nabla_{v}b(x)\|-h^{2}\|b(x)\|^{2}L_{R}C_{=}

\displaystyle\geq\|v\|\left(1-hL_{\nabla}-h^{2}\|b(x)\|^{2}L_{R}(2+hL_{\nabla})\right).

Cleraly
h<1
is required, so we only need to find
h
s.t.

\displaystyle 1-hL_{\nabla}>h^{2}\|b(x)\|^{2}L_{R}(2+L_{\nabla})>h^{2}\|b(x)\|^{2}L_{R}(2+hL_{\nabla}).

If
h\leq\frac{1}{4L_{\nabla}}
, then
1-hL_{\nabla}\geq\frac{3}{4}
.
The value
h\leq\sqrt{\frac{3}{4\|b(x)\|^{2}L_{R}(2+hL_{\nabla})}}

suffices.
∎

B.5
Main Theorem

Now we prove our main theorem. We remark that the proof of Theorem
1

and
2
are essentially the same.

Proof.
[Proof of Theorem
1
]
By Lemma
5.1
, we have

\displaystyle\TV(\pi_{T},\hat{\pi}_{T})=\TV(\pi_{t_{N}},\hat{\pi}_{t_{N}})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\TV(\pi_{t_{0}},\hat{\pi}_{t_{0}})+\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{N}}\mathbb{E}[\langle\grad\log p(t,x),\tilde{v}(x,t)-v(x,t)\rangle]dt+\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{N}}\mathbb{E}[|\Div(\tilde{v}(x,t)-v(x,t))|]dt

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\TV(\pi_{t_{0}},\hat{\pi}_{t_{0}})+\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{N}}\mathbb{E}[\|\grad\log p(t,x)\|\cdot\|\tilde{v}(x,t)-v(x,t)\|]dt+\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{N}}\mathbb{E}[|\Div(\tilde{v}(x,t)-v(x,t))|]dt,

where
\pi_{t},\hat{\pi}_{t}
denote the law for
X_{t},Y_{t}
respectively.

Notice that

\displaystyle\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}(t-t_{k})dt=(\frac{1}{2}t^{2}-tt_{k})|_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}=\frac{1}{2}t_{k+1}^{2}-\frac{1}{2}t_{k}^{2}-t_{k}(t_{k+1}-t_{k})=\frac{1}{2}(t_{k+1}-t_{k})^{2}.

(11)

Hence using constant step size for discretization, we obtain

\displaystyle\TV(\pi_{T},\hat{\pi}_{T})=\TV(X_{t_{N}},Y_{t_{N}})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\TV(X_{t_{0}},Y_{t_{0}})+\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{N}}\mathbb{E}[\|\grad\log p(t,x)\|\cdot\|\tilde{v}(x,t)-v(x,t)\|]dt+\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{N}}\mathbb{E}[|\Div(\tilde{v}(x,t)-v(x,t))|]dt

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\TV(X_{t_{0}},Y_{t_{0}})+\frac{1}{2}Nh^{2}\Bigl(\sqrt{2L_{t}^{\text{score}}}(L_{t}^{\hat{v},x}L^{\hat{v}}+L^{v}L_{t}^{\hat{v},x}+L_{t}^{v,t}+L^{v}L_{t}^{v,x})

\displaystyle+L_{t}^{\Div,x}(L^{\hat{v}}+2L^{v})+L_{t}^{\Div,t}+L_{R}(L^{\hat{v}})^{2}d\Bigr)+Nh(\varepsilon\sqrt{2L_{t}^{\text{score}}}+\varepsilon)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\TV(X_{t_{0}},Y_{t_{0}})+h\Bigl(\sqrt{L_{t}^{\text{score}}}(L_{t}^{\hat{v},x}L^{\hat{v}}+L^{v}L_{t}^{\hat{v},x}+L_{t}^{v,t}+L^{v}L_{t}^{v,x})

\displaystyle+L_{t}^{\Div,x}(L^{\hat{v}}+2L^{v})+L_{t}^{\Div,t}+L_{R}(L^{\hat{v}})^{2}d\Bigr)+(\varepsilon\sqrt{2L_{t}^{\text{score}}}+\varepsilon).

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\TV(X_{t_{0}},Y_{t_{0}})+h\mathsf{C}_{\textsf{Lip}}+\varepsilon\mathsf{C}_{\textsf{eps}},

where note that
Nh<1
and we compute

\displaystyle\sqrt{L_{t}^{\text{score}}}((L_{t}^{v,x}+\varepsilon)(L^{v}+\varepsilon)+L^{v}(L_{t}^{v,x}+\varepsilon)+L_{t}^{v,t}+L^{v}L_{t}^{v,x})

\displaystyle+L_{t}^{\Div,x}(3L^{v}+\varepsilon)+L_{t}^{\Div,t}+L_{R}(L^{v}+\varepsilon)^{2}d

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{2}(\sqrt{L_{t}^{\text{score}}}+L_{R}d)+\varepsilon(2\sqrt{L_{t}^{\text{score}}}L^{v}+\sqrt{L_{t}^{\text{score}}}L_{t}^{v,x}+L_{t}^{\Div,x}+2L_{R}dL^{v})

\displaystyle+3\sqrt{L_{t}^{\text{score}}}L_{t}^{v,x}L^{v}+\sqrt{L_{t}^{\text{score}}}L_{t}^{v,t}+3L^{v}L_{t}^{\Div,x}+L_{t}^{\Div,t}+L_{R}(L^{v})^{2}d,

and denote

\displaystyle\mathsf{C}_{\textsf{Lip}}

\displaystyle\coloneqq 3\sqrt{L_{t}^{\text{score}}}L_{t}^{v,x}L^{v}+\sqrt{L_{t}^{\text{score}}}L_{t}^{v,t}+3L^{v}L_{t}^{\Div,x}+L_{t}^{\Div,t}+L_{R}(L^{v})^{2}d,

\displaystyle\mathsf{C}_{\textsf{eps}}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sqrt{2L_{t}^{\text{score}}}+1+2\sqrt{L_{t}^{\text{score}}}L^{v}+\sqrt{L_{t}^{\text{score}}}L_{t}^{v,x}+L_{t}^{\Div,x}+2L_{R}dL^{v}+\varepsilon(\sqrt{L_{t}^{\text{score}}}+L_{R}d).

∎

Proof.
[Proof of Theorem
2
]
We have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|\grad\log p(t,x)\|\cdot\|\tilde{v}(x,t)-v(x,t)\|]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\varepsilon\sqrt{3L_{t}^{\text{score}}}+(t-t_{k})\Bigl(6L_{t}^{\text{score}}\left((L_{t}^{\hat{v},x})^{2}(L_{t}^{\hat{v}})^{2}+(L_{t}^{\hat{v},x})^{2}(L_{t}^{v})^{2}+(L_{t}^{v,t})^{2}+(L_{t}^{v})^{2}(L_{t}^{v,x})^{2}\right)\Bigr)^{\frac{1}{2}},

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{p(t,x)}[\Div(\tilde{v}(x,t)-v(t,X_{t}))]\leq 3\varepsilon+(t-t_{k})\Bigl(L_{t}^{\Div\hat{v},x}(L_{t}^{\hat{v}}+L_{t}^{v})+L_{t}^{\Div,x}L_{t}^{v}+L_{t}^{\Div,t}+L_{R}(L_{t}^{\hat{v}})^{2}d\Bigr).

Also recall

\displaystyle\TV(\pi_{T},\hat{\pi}_{T})=\TV(\pi_{t_{N}},\hat{\pi}_{t_{N}})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\TV(\pi_{t_{0}},\hat{\pi}_{t_{0}})+\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{N}}\mathbb{E}[\langle\grad\log p(t,x),\tilde{v}(x,t)-v(x,t)\rangle]dt+\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{N}}\mathbb{E}[|\Div(\tilde{v}(x,t)-v(x,t))|]dt

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\TV(\pi_{t_{0}},\hat{\pi}_{t_{0}})+\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{N}}\mathbb{E}[\|\grad\log p(t,x)\|\cdot\|\tilde{v}(x,t)-v(x,t)\|]dt+\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{N}}\mathbb{E}[|\Div(\tilde{v}(x,t)-v(x,t))|]dt.

Notice that

\displaystyle\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}(t-t_{k})dt=(\frac{1}{2}t^{2}-tt_{k})|_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}=\frac{1}{2}t_{k+1}^{2}-\frac{1}{2}t_{k}^{2}-t_{k}(t_{k+1}-t_{k})=\frac{1}{2}(t_{k+1}-t_{k})^{2}.

(12)

Hence using constant step size for discretization, we obtain

\displaystyle\TV(\pi_{T},\hat{\pi}_{T})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\TV(X_{t_{0}},Y_{t_{0}})+\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{N}}\mathbb{E}[\|\grad\log p(t,x)\|\cdot\|\tilde{v}(x,t)-v(x,t)\|]dt+\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{N}}\mathbb{E}[|\Div(\tilde{v}(x,t)-v(x,t))|]dt

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\TV(X_{t_{0}},Y_{t_{0}})+\frac{1}{2}Nh^{2}\Bigl(\sqrt{6L_{t}^{\text{score}}}\left((L_{t}^{\hat{v},x})^{2}(L_{t}^{\hat{v}})^{2}+(L_{t}^{\hat{v},x})^{2}(L_{t}^{v})^{2}+(L_{t}^{v,t})^{2}+(L_{t}^{v})^{2}(L_{t}^{v,x})^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+\Bigl(L_{t}^{\Div\hat{v},x}(L_{t}^{\hat{v}}+L_{t}^{v})+L_{t}^{\Div,x}L_{t}^{v}+L_{t}^{\Div,t}+L_{R}(L_{t}^{\hat{v}})^{2}d\Bigr)\Bigr)+Nh(\varepsilon\sqrt{2L_{t}^{\text{score}}}+3\varepsilon)

(13)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\TV(X_{t_{0}},Y_{t_{0}})+h\mathsf{C}_{\textsf{Lip}}+\varepsilon\mathsf{C}_{\textsf{eps, 1}},

where note that
Nh<1
. Take
t_{0}=0
,
t_{N}=T<1
, we obtain

\displaystyle\TV(\pi_{T},\hat{\pi}_{T})\leq h\mathsf{C}_{\textsf{Lip}}+\varepsilon\mathsf{C}_{\textsf{eps, 1}}.

∎

B.6
Example: Hypersphere

For compact manifolds, under uniform estimation error (Assumption
3
, we can establish the regularity for
\hat{v}
.
In particular, under Assumption
3
and Assumption
2
,
since
\|\nabla\hat{v}(x)\|_{\op}=\|\nabla\hat{v}(x)-\nabla v(x)+\nabla v(x)\|_{\op}
, we have

1.

\|\hat{v}(t,x)\|\leq L^{\hat{v}}=L^{v}+\varepsilon
.

2.

\hat{v}(t,x)
is Lipschitz in
x
variable with
L_{t}^{\hat{v},x}=L_{t}^{v,x}+\varepsilon
.

Lemma B.5
.

On
\mathcal{S}^{d}
, Assumption
2
holds with the following constants.

1.

L_{t}^{v,x}=\frac{12\pi M_{1}(d-1)}{m_{1}(1-t)}
,
L_{t}^{\hat{v},x}=\varepsilon+\frac{12\pi M_{1}(d-1)}{m_{1}(1-t)}
.

2.

L_{t}^{v,t}:=\frac{8\pi^{2}d}{1-t}\frac{M_{1}}{m_{1}}
.

3.

L_{t}^{\Div,x}:=\frac{128\pi(d-1)^{2}}{(1-t)^{3}}\frac{M_{1}}{m_{1}}
.

4.

L_{t}^{\Div,t}:=\frac{128\pi^{2}(d-1)^{2}}{(1-t)^{3}}\frac{M_{1}}{m_{1}}
.

5.

L_{t}^{\text{score}}:=\frac{8(d-1)^{2}}{(1-t)^{2}}\frac{M_{1}}{m_{1}}
.

6.

L^{v}=\pi
.

7.

L_{R}=1
.

As the proof is technical, we defer it to Appendix
D
.

Now we prove Proposition
3.1
.

Proof.
[Proof of Proposition
3.1
]
We first recall the extra condition on
h
imposed by Lemma
5.2
:

\displaystyle h

\displaystyle<\min\{\frac{R}{\|b(x)\|},\frac{1}{4L_{\nabla}},\sqrt{\frac{3}{4\|b(x)\|^{2}L_{R}(2+2L_{\nabla}\max\{\frac{1}{\sqrt{K_{\min}}},1\})}}\}.

Since
\|\hat{v}(t,x)\|\leq L^{\hat{v}}=L^{v}+\varepsilon
, we know
\|b(x)\|
is of constant order, consequently
\frac{R}{\|b(x)\|}
is of constant order. Thus such an condition is dominated by the term
\frac{1}{L_{\nabla}}
, which in our case is exactly
\frac{1}{L_{t}^{\hat{v},x}}
. Plug in constants in Lemma
B.5
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|\grad\log p(t,x)\|\cdot\|\tilde{v}(x,t)-v(x,t)\|]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\varepsilon\sqrt{2\frac{8(d-1)^{2}}{(1-t)^{2}}\frac{M_{1}}{m_{1}}}+(t-t_{k})\Bigl(\frac{8(d-1)^{2}}{(1-t)^{2}}\frac{M_{1}}{m_{1}}(2((\varepsilon+\frac{12\pi M_{1}(d-1)}{m_{1}(1-t)})(\pi+\varepsilon)

\displaystyle\quad+\pi(\varepsilon+\frac{12\pi M_{1}(d-1)}{m_{1}(1-t)})+\frac{8\pi^{2}d}{1-t}\frac{M_{1}}{m_{1}}+\pi\frac{12\pi M_{1}(d-1)}{m_{1}(1-t)})^{2})\Bigr)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 4\varepsilon\frac{d-1}{1-t}\sqrt{\frac{M_{1}}{m_{1}}}+(t-t_{k})4\frac{d-1}{1-t}\sqrt{\frac{M_{1}}{m_{1}}}\bigl((\varepsilon+\frac{12\pi M_{1}(d-1)}{m_{1}(1-t)})(\pi+\varepsilon)

\displaystyle\quad+\pi(\varepsilon+\frac{12\pi M_{1}(d-1)}{m_{1}(1-t)})+\frac{8\pi^{2}d}{1-t}\frac{M_{1}}{m_{1}}+\pi\frac{12\pi M_{1}(d-1)}{m_{1}(1-t)}\bigr)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\varepsilon\frac{d-1}{1-t}\Bigl(\frac{M_{1}}{m_{1}}\Bigr)^{\frac{1}{2}}+(t-t_{k})\frac{(d-1)^{2}}{(1-t)^{2}}\Bigl(\frac{M_{1}}{m_{1}}\Bigr)^{\frac{3}{2}}.

And similarly,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{p(t,x)}[\Div(\tilde{v}(x,t)-v(t,X_{t}))]\leq\varepsilon+(t-t_{k})\Bigl(L_{t}^{\Div,x}(L^{\hat{v}}+2L^{v})+L_{t}^{\Div,t}+L_{R}(L^{\hat{v}})^{2}d\Bigr)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\varepsilon+(t-t_{k})\Bigl(\frac{128\pi(d-1)^{2}}{(1-t)^{3}}\frac{M_{1}}{m_{1}}(3\pi+\varepsilon)+\frac{128\pi^{2}(d-1)^{2}}{(1-t)^{3}}\frac{M_{1}}{m_{1}}+(\pi+\varepsilon)^{2}d\Bigr)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\varepsilon+(t-t_{k})\Bigl(\frac{(d-1)^{2}}{(1-t)^{3}}\frac{M_{1}}{m_{1}}+d\Bigr).

With early stopping, we terminate the sampling algorithm (Algorithm
1
) at time
T<1
(i.e.,
t_{0}=0,t_{N}=T
)

\displaystyle\TV(\pi_{T},\hat{\pi}_{T})=\TV(X_{t_{N}},Y_{t_{N}})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\TV(X_{t_{0}},Y_{t_{0}})+\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{N}}\mathbb{E}[\|\grad\log p(t,x)\|\cdot\|\tilde{v}(x,t)-v(x,t)\|]dt+\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{N}}\mathbb{E}[|\Div(\tilde{v}(x,t)-v(x,t))|]dt

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{N-1}\int_{t_{i}}^{t_{i+1}}\varepsilon\frac{d-1}{1-t}\Bigl(\frac{M_{1}}{m_{1}}\Bigr)^{\frac{1}{2}}+(t-t_{i})\frac{(d-1)^{2}}{(1-t)^{2}}\Bigl(\frac{M_{1}}{m_{1}}\Bigr)^{\frac{3}{2}}dt+\sum_{i=0}^{N-1}\int_{t_{i}}^{t_{i+1}}\varepsilon+(t-t_{i})\Bigl(\frac{(d-1)^{2}}{(1-t)^{3}}\frac{M_{1}}{m_{1}}+d\Bigr)dt

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\Bigl(\frac{M_{1}}{m_{1}}\Bigr)^{\frac{1}{2}}(d-1)\varepsilon\sum_{i=0}^{N-1}\int_{t_{i}}^{t_{i+1}}\frac{1}{1-t}dt+\Bigl(\frac{M_{1}}{m_{1}}\Bigr)^{\frac{3}{2}}(d-1)^{2}\sum_{i=0}^{N-1}\int_{t_{i}}^{t_{i+1}}\frac{t-t_{i}}{(1-t)^{3}}dt.

We first discuss the case of constant step size. Using

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{N-1}\int_{t_{i}}^{t_{i+1}}\frac{1}{1-t}dt=-\log(1-t_{N})

and (using
s:=1-t
so that
ds=-dt
)

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{N-1}\int_{t_{i}}^{t_{i+1}}\frac{t-t_{i}}{(1-t)^{3}}dt\leq h\sum_{i=0}^{N-1}\int_{t_{i}}^{t_{i+1}}\frac{1}{(1-t)^{3}}dt=h\sum_{i=0}^{N-1}\int_{1-t_{i+1}}^{1-t_{i}}\frac{1}{s^{3}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle h\sum_{i=0}^{N-1}-\frac{1}{(1-t_{i})^{2}}+\frac{1}{(1-t_{i+1})^{2}}\lesssim h\frac{1}{(1-T)^{2}},

we can finally bound the error as

\displaystyle\varepsilon\Bigl(\frac{M_{1}}{m_{1}}\Bigr)^{\frac{1}{2}}(d-1)\log(\frac{1}{1-T})+h\Bigl(\frac{M_{1}}{m_{1}}\Bigr)^{\frac{3}{2}}(d-1)^{2}\frac{1}{(1-T)^{2}}.

Now we have

\displaystyle\TV(X_{T},Y_{T})\lesssim\varepsilon\Bigl(\frac{M_{1}}{m_{1}}\Bigr)^{\frac{1}{2}}(d-1)\log(\frac{1}{1-T})+h\Bigl(\frac{M_{1}}{m_{1}}\Bigr)^{\frac{3}{2}}(d-1)^{2}\frac{1}{(1-T)^{2}}.

To obtain a sample up to
\varepsilon_{\text{target}}
accuracy, we assume
\varepsilon
is sufficiently small.
Then we need

\displaystyle h\Bigl(\frac{M_{1}}{m_{1}}\Bigr)^{\frac{3}{2}}(d-1)^{2}\frac{1}{(1-T)^{2}}=\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon_{\text{target}}),

which means

\displaystyle h=\frac{\varepsilon_{\text{target}}(1-T)^{2}}{(d-1)^{2}}\Bigl(\frac{m_{1}}{M_{1}}\Bigr)^{\frac{3}{2}}.

(14)

Next, we discuss a specific step size schedule that can improve the dependency on
\frac{1}{1-T}
. Denote
h_{k}=t_{k+1}-t_{k}
.
Then

\displaystyle\int_{t_{i}}^{t_{i+1}}\frac{1-t_{i}}{(1-t)^{3}}dt=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}(1-t_{i})(\frac{1}{(1-t_{i+1})^{2}}-\frac{1}{(1-t_{i})^{2}})=\frac{1}{2}(1-t_{i})(\frac{t_{i}^{2}-2t_{i}-t_{i+1}^{2}+2t_{i+1}}{(1-t_{i+1})^{2}(1-t_{i})^{2}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}(1-t_{i})\frac{(t_{i+1}-t_{i})(2-t_{i}-t_{i+1})}{(1-t_{i+1})^{2}(1-t_{i})^{2}}=\frac{1}{2}(1-t_{i})h_{i}\frac{2-t_{i}-t_{i+1}}{(1-t_{i+1})^{2}(1-t_{i})^{2}},

\displaystyle\int_{t_{i}}^{t_{i+1}}\frac{t-1}{(1-t)^{3}}dt=-\int_{t_{i}}^{t_{i+1}}\frac{1}{(1-t)^{2}}dt=\frac{1}{1-t_{i}}-\frac{1}{1-t_{i+1}}=-\frac{h_{i}}{(1-t_{i+1})(1-t_{i})}.

Together,

\displaystyle\int_{t_{i}}^{t_{i+1}}\frac{t-t_{i}}{(1-t)^{3}}dt=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}(1-t_{i})h_{i}\frac{2-t_{i}-t_{i+1}}{(1-t_{i+1})^{2}(1-t_{i})^{2}}-\frac{h_{i}}{(1-t_{i+1})(1-t_{i})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{h_{i}}{(1-t_{i+1})(1-t_{i})}(-1+\frac{1}{2}\frac{2-t_{i}-t_{i+1}}{(1-t_{i+1})})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{h_{i}^{2}}{2(1-t_{i+1})^{2}(1-t_{i})}\lesssim\frac{h_{i}^{2}}{(1-t_{i+1})^{3}}.

Now we want to control

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{N-1}\int_{t_{i}}^{t_{i+1}}\frac{t-t_{i}}{(1-t)^{3}}dt\lesssim\sum_{i=0}^{N-1}\frac{h_{i}^{2}}{(1-t_{i+1})^{3}}.

With
t_{i}=1-\frac{1}{(1+\eta i)^{2}}
.
Then
h_{i}=\frac{1}{(1+\eta i)^{2}}-\frac{1}{(1+\eta(i+1))^{2}}=\frac{\eta(2+\eta(2i+1))}{(1+\eta i)^{2}(1+\eta(i+1))^{2}}
.

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{N-1}\frac{h_{i}^{2}}{(1-t_{i+1})^{3}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=0}^{N-1}\frac{\eta^{2}(2+\eta(2i+1))^{2}}{(1+\eta i)^{4}(1+\eta(i+1))^{4}}(1+\eta(i+1))^{6}

\displaystyle=\eta^{2}\sum_{i=0}^{N-1}\frac{(2+\eta(2i+1))^{2}}{(1+\eta i)^{4}}(1+\eta(i+1))^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim 4N\eta^{2}.

Note that by construction of early stopping, it must hold that
1-\frac{1}{(1+\eta N)^{2}}=T
, which implies
\frac{\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-T}}-1}{\eta}=N
.
Hence

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{N-1}\frac{h_{i}^{2}}{(1-t_{i})^{3}}\lesssim 4N\eta^{2}\lesssim\eta\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-T}}.

We want to reach

\displaystyle\Bigl(\frac{M_{1}}{m_{1}}\Bigr)^{\frac{3}{2}}(d-1)^{2}\eta\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-T}}=\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon_{\text{target}}).

So we need

\displaystyle\eta\lesssim\Bigl(\frac{\varepsilon_{\text{target}}\sqrt{1-T}}{(d-1)^{2}}\Bigr).

and consequently

\displaystyle N=\frac{\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-T}}-1}{\eta}=\mathcal{O}(\frac{\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-T}}-1}{\frac{\varepsilon_{\text{target}}\sqrt{1-T}}{(d-1)^{2}}})=\mathcal{O}(\frac{d^{2}}{(1-T)\varepsilon_{\text{target}}}).

We remark that the step size schedule can be described by the discretized time points as follows:

\displaystyle t_{i}=1-\frac{1}{(1+\eta i)^{2}},\qquad\text{where}\qquad\eta=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\varepsilon_{\text{target}}\sqrt{1-T}}{(d-1)^{2}}\right).

(15)

∎

B.7
Example: SPD Manifold

We first state the following result proved later in Appendix
E
.

Proposition B.6
.

Let
M
be
\SPD(n)
. Assume Asumption
1
. We impose the following moment condition: there exists
M_{\lambda_{1}}
be such that

\displaystyle\max\big\{\mathbb{E}[d(X_{1},z)^{2}e^{\lambda_{1}d(X_{1},z)}],\mathbb{E}[e^{\lambda_{1}d(X_{1},z)}]\big\}\leq M_{\lambda_{1}},\qquad\text{where}\qquad\lambda_{1}=24\max\{1,\kappa\}.

We choose the prior distribution to be a Riemannian Gaussian distribution centered at some
z\in M
:
p_{0}(x)\propto\exp\big(-d\,d(x,z)^{2}\big)
.
We then have the following regularity results.

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|v(t,x)\|^{2}]\lesssim

\displaystyle d,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|\nabla v(t,x)\|]\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{d^{2+6\lambda}}{1-t}L_{R}M_{\lambda_{1}}^{\frac{1}{2}},\qquad

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|\nabla v(t,x)\|^{2}]

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{d^{3+12\lambda}}{(1-t)^{2}}L_{R}^{2}M_{\lambda_{1}}^{\frac{1}{2}},

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[|\frac{d}{dt}v(t,x)|]\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{d^{2+6\lambda}}{1-t}L_{R}M_{\lambda_{1}}^{\frac{1}{2}},\qquad

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[|\frac{d}{dt}v(t,x)|^{2}]

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{d^{3+12\lambda}}{(1-t)^{2}}L_{R}^{2}M_{\lambda_{1}}^{\frac{1}{2}},

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|\grad_{x}\Div v(t,x)\|]\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{d^{3+12\lambda}}{(1-t)^{2}}L_{R}^{3},\qquad

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|\grad_{x}\Div v(t,x)\|^{2}]

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{d^{5+24\lambda}}{(1-t)^{4}}L_{R}^{6}M_{\lambda_{1}},

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[|\frac{d}{dt}\Div v(t,x)|]\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{d^{3+12\lambda}}{(1-t)^{2}}L_{R}^{3}M_{\lambda_{1}}^{\frac{1}{2}},

where
\lambda=\max\{1,\kappa\}
.

Proof.
[Proof of Proposition
4.1
]
Using Proposition
B.6
, and following Theorem
2
, we have

\displaystyle\TV(\pi_{T},\hat{\pi}_{T})=\TV(X_{t_{N}},Y_{t_{N}})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\TV(X_{t_{0}},Y_{t_{0}})+\frac{1}{2}Nh^{2}\Bigl(\sqrt{6L_{t}^{\text{score}}}\left((L_{t}^{\hat{v},x})^{2}(L_{t}^{\hat{v}})^{2}+(L_{t}^{\hat{v},x})^{2}(L_{t}^{v})^{2}+(L_{t}^{v,t})^{2}+(L_{t}^{v})^{2}(L_{t}^{v,x})^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+\Bigl(L_{t}^{\Div\hat{v},x}(L_{t}^{\hat{v}}+L_{t}^{v})+L_{t}^{\Div,x}L_{t}^{v}+L_{t}^{\Div,t}+L_{R}(L_{t}^{\hat{v}})^{2}d\Bigr)\Bigr)+Nh(\varepsilon\sqrt{2L_{t}^{\text{score}}}+\varepsilon)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle Nh^{2}\Bigl(\frac{d^{5+12\lambda}}{(1-t)^{2}}L_{R}^{2}M_{\lambda_{1}}\sqrt{M\frac{d^{2}}{(1-t)^{2}}L_{R}^{2}d^{12\lambda}}\Bigr)+Nh(\varepsilon\sqrt{M\frac{d^{2}}{(1-t)^{2}}L_{R}^{2}d^{12\lambda}}+\varepsilon)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle h\Bigl(\frac{d^{6+18\lambda}}{(1-t)^{3}}L_{R}^{3}M_{\lambda_{1}}^{\frac{3}{2}}\Bigr)+(\frac{d^{6\lambda+1}}{1-t}L_{R})M_{\lambda_{1}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\varepsilon.

Thus to reach
\varepsilon_{\text{target}}
accuracy, we need

\displaystyle h=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\varepsilon_{\text{target}}(1-T)^{3}}{d^{6+18\lambda}L_{R}^{3}M_{\lambda_{1}}^{\frac{3}{2}}}\right).

Consequently, the iteration complexity would be

\displaystyle N=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{d^{6+18\lambda}L_{R}^{3}M_{\lambda_{1}}^{\frac{3}{2}}}{\varepsilon_{\text{target}}(1-T)^{3}}\right).

We remark that since
K_{\min}=-\frac{1}{2}

[
Criscitiello and Boumal 2023
]
, so
\lambda=\max\{1,\kappa\}=1
.
In the meanwhile, the upper bound on
h
required by Lemma
5.2
can be reduced to be of order

\displaystyle\min\{\frac{1}{L_{\nabla}},\sqrt{\frac{1}{\|b(x)\|^{2}L_{R}L_{\nabla}}}\}=\frac{1-T}{d^{8}L_{R}M_{\lambda_{1}}^{\frac{1}{2}}},

which is lower than our required order of
h
.

Therefore, we conclude the iteration complexity as

\displaystyle N=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{d^{6+18\lambda}L_{R}^{3}M_{\lambda_{1}}^{\frac{3}{2}}}{\varepsilon_{\text{target}}(1-T)^{3}}\right)=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{d^{24}L_{R}^{3}M_{\lambda_{1}}^{\frac{3}{2}}}{\varepsilon_{\text{target}}(1-T)^{3}}\right).

∎

Appendix C
Auxiliary Results for Proof of Main Theorems

C.1
Jacobi Equation

Lemma C.1

(Solution for Jacobi equation)
.

Let
M
be of constant sectional curvature
c
.
Let
J
be a normal Jacobi field along
\gamma
, with initial condition
J(0)=v^{\perp},J^{\prime}(0)=0
.
Then we have

\displaystyle J(t)=s_{c}^{(2)}(t)\|v^{\perp}\|E(t):=\begin{cases}\|v^{\perp}\|E(t),&\text{ if }c=0,\\
\|v^{\perp}\|\cos(\sqrt{c}t)E(t),&\text{ if }c>0,\\
\|v^{\perp}\|\cosh(\sqrt{-c}t)E(t),&\text{ if }c<0.\end{cases}

where
E
is a parallel normal unit vector field with
E(0)=\frac{v^{\perp}}{\|v^{\perp}\|}
.

Proof.
[Proof of Lemma
C.1
] Similar to the proof of
Lee 2018
, the solution is of the form
J(t)=f(t)E(t)
where
E(t)
is a parallel unit normal vector field along
\gamma
. Since the curvature is constant and
J
is a normal Jacobi field, the Jacobi equation reduces to
D_{t}^{2}J+cJ=0
, thus we only need to solve for
f^{\prime\prime}(t)+cf(t)=0
, where
f(t)\in\mathbb{R},\forall t
.

•

When
c=0
, we obtain
f^{\prime\prime}(t)=0
, hence (to satisfy initial condition)

f(t)=\|v^{\perp}\|
.

•

When
c>0
, we obtain
f(t)=\|v^{\perp}\|\cos(\sqrt{c}t)
.

•

When
c<0
, we obtain
f(t)=\|v^{\perp}\|\frac{e^{\sqrt{-c}t}+e^{-\sqrt{-c}t}}{2}=\|v^{\perp}\|\cosh(\sqrt{c}t)
.

Note that
E(0)=\frac{v^{\perp}}{\|v^{\perp}\|}
.
Therefore,

\displaystyle J(t)=\begin{cases}\|v^{\perp}\|E(t),&\text{ if }c=0,\\
\|v^{\perp}\|\cos(\sqrt{c}t)E(t),&\text{ if }c>0,\\
\|v^{\perp}\|\cosh(\sqrt{-c}t)E(t),&\text{ if }c<0.\end{cases}

∎

Lemma C.2
.

Let
J_{v}(t)
be a Jacobi field along
\gamma(t)=\Exp_{x}(thb(x))
,
with
J_{v}(0)=v
,
J_{v}^{\prime}(0)=\omega
.
Define
Y(t)=P_{\gamma(t)}^{\gamma(0)}J_{v}(t)
.
Up to the first conjugate point of
x
, we have

\displaystyle\|Y(t)\|\leq\|v^{\parallel}\|+\|\omega^{\parallel}\|t+s_{K_{\min}}(t)\|\omega^{\perp}\|+\frac{s_{K_{\min}}(t)}{s_{K_{\min}}(t_{0})}\|v^{\perp}\|.

Take
h<\frac{R}{\|b(x)\|}
,
we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\|Y(t)\|dt\leq\begin{cases}\|v^{\parallel}\|+\|\omega^{\parallel}\|+\frac{\|\omega^{\perp}\|}{\sqrt{K_{\min}}}+\|v^{\perp}\|,&\text{ if }K_{\min}>0,\\
\|v^{\parallel}\|+\|\omega^{\parallel}\|+\frac{\cosh(\sqrt{-K_{\min}})-1}{-K_{\min}}\|\omega^{\perp}\|+\frac{\sinh(\sqrt{-K_{\min}})}{\sqrt{-K_{\min}}}\|v^{\perp}\|,&\text{ if }K_{\min}<0,\\
\|v^{\parallel}\|+\frac{1}{2}\|\omega^{\parallel}\|+\frac{1}{2}\|\omega^{\perp}\|+\|v^{\perp}\|,&\text{ if }K_{\min}=0.\end{cases}

Proof.
[Proof of Lemma
C.2
]
Using isometric property of parallel transport,
\|Y(t)\|=\|J_{v}(t)\|
.
Decompose
v=v^{\parallel}+v^{\perp}
, where

v^{\parallel}
is the component in
\gamma^{\prime}(0)
direction, and

\langle v^{\perp},\gamma^{\prime}(0)\rangle=0
.
Similarly, we decompose

\omega=\omega^{\parallel}+\omega^{\perp}
.

Define
J_{v}=:J^{(0)}+J^{(1)}+J^{(2)}
, where

\displaystyle J^{(0)}(0)=v^{\parallel},

\displaystyle\quad D_{t}J^{(0)}(0)=\omega^{\parallel};

\displaystyle J^{(1)}(0)=0,

\displaystyle\quad D_{t}J^{(1)}(0)=\omega^{\perp};

\displaystyle J^{(2)}(0)=v^{\perp},

\displaystyle\quad D_{t}J^{(2)}(0)=0.

Note that both
J^{(1)}(0)
,
D_{t}J^{(1)}(0)
are orthogonla to
\gamma^{\prime}(0)
, so
J^{(1)}(t)
is a normal Jacobi field.

We know
J^{(0)}
is a tangential Jacobi field, so it has the form

\displaystyle J^{(0)}(t)=(a+bt)\gamma^{\prime}(t).

Plug in the initial value, we get (note that
D_{t}\gamma^{\prime}(0)=0
)

\displaystyle a\gamma^{\prime}(0)=v^{\parallel},\quad b\gamma^{\prime}(0)=\omega^{\parallel}.

Hence
|a|=\frac{\|v^{\parallel}\|}{\|\gamma^{\prime}(0)\|}
,
and
|b|=\frac{\|\omega^{\parallel}\|}{\|\gamma^{\prime}(0)\|}
.

By definition,
\|\gamma^{\prime}(0)\|=\|hb(x)\|=\|\gamma^{\prime}(t)\|
.
So we get

\displaystyle\|J^{(0)}(t)\|\leq\|v^{\parallel}\|+\|\omega^{\parallel}\|t.

If all sectional curvatures of
M
are bounded below by a constant
K_{\min}
,
Jacobi field comparison theorem yield

\displaystyle\|J^{(1)}(t)\|\leq s_{K}(t)\|D_{t}J^{(1)}(0)\|=s_{K_{\min}}(t)\|\omega^{\perp}\|.

Now consider the term
J^{2}(0)
. By
Cheeger et al. 1975
applied with Jacobi field formula given in Lemma
C.1
, we obtain
\|J^{(2)}(t)\|\leq\|\tilde{J}(t)\|
.
We remark that the focal point free condition is saying
J^{(2)}(t)\neq 0
.
As long as Cut locus is not reached, the geodesic is minimizing.
Hence it suffices to guarantee that the geodesic
\gamma
satisfies
\|\gamma^{\prime}(0)\|<\inj(M)
.

Finally, recall
\|Y(t)\|=\|J_{v}(t)\|
.

\displaystyle\|Y(t)\|=

\displaystyle\|J_{v}(t)\|=\|J^{(0)}(t)+J^{(1)}(t)+J^{(2)}(t)\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|v^{\parallel}\|+\|\omega^{\parallel}\|t+|s_{K_{\min}}(t)|\|\omega^{\perp}\|+|s_{K_{\min}}^{(2)}(t)|\|v^{\perp}\|.

We split into cases. The first case is
K_{\min}>0
.
In this case we denote
R=\inj(M)
and

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}|s_{K_{\min}}(t)|dt=\frac{1}{\sqrt{K_{\min}}}\int_{0}^{1}|\sin(t\sqrt{K_{\min}})|dt\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{K_{\min}}}.

Also,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}|s_{K_{\min}}^{(2)}(t)|dt=\int_{0}^{1}|\cos(\sqrt{K_{\min}}t)|dt\leq 1.

To summarize, when
h<\frac{R}{\|b(x)\|}
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\|Y(t)\|dt\leq

\displaystyle\|v^{\parallel}\|+\|\omega^{\parallel}\|+\frac{\|\omega^{\perp}\|}{\sqrt{K_{\min}}}+\|v^{\perp}\|.

The second case is
K_{\min}<0
.

Then
s_{K_{\min}}(t)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{-K_{\min}}}\sinh(\sqrt{-K_{\min}}t)
.
Consider
0<t\leq h<1
.

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}s_{K_{\min}}(t)dt=\int_{0}^{1}\frac{1}{\sqrt{-K_{\min}}}\sinh t\sqrt{-K_{\min}}dt=\frac{1}{-K_{\min}}(\cosh(\sqrt{-K_{\min}})-1).

For the second integral,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\cosh(\sqrt{-K_{\min}}t)dt=\frac{\sinh(\sqrt{-K_{\min}})}{\sqrt{-K_{\min}}}.

To summarize, when
h<\frac{R}{\|b(x)\|}
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\|Y(t)\|dt\leq

\displaystyle\|v^{\parallel}\|+\|\omega^{\parallel}\|+\frac{\cosh(\sqrt{-K_{\min}})-1}{-K_{\min}}\|\omega^{\perp}\|+\|v^{\perp}\|\frac{\sinh(\sqrt{-K_{\min}})}{\sqrt{-K_{\min}}}.

The third case is
K_{\min}=0
.
In this case
s_{K_{\min}}(t)=t
.

\displaystyle\|Y(t)\|=

\displaystyle\|J_{v}(t)\|=\|J^{(0)}(t)+J^{(1)}(t)+J^{(2)}(t)\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|v^{\parallel}\|+\|\omega^{\parallel}\|t+\|\omega^{\perp}\|t+\|v^{\perp}\|.

Then we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\|Y(t)\|dt\leq

\displaystyle\|v^{\parallel}\|+\frac{1}{2}\|\omega^{\parallel}\|+\frac{1}{2}\|\omega^{\perp}\|+\|v^{\perp}\|.

∎

C.2
Divergence Term

Lemma C.3
.

Let
c
denote the geodesic with
c(0)=z
,
c(h)=x
.
Define a variation of geodesics
\Lambda(s,t)
as

\Lambda(s,t)=\Exp_{c(t)}(sP_{c(0)}^{c(t)}E_{i}(t))

where
E_{i}(t)
is basis vector field along
c
.
Let
V
be a vector field.
For every
z
, we can obtain a new vector field by parallel transport,
denote as
P_{c(0)}^{c(t)}V(z)
.
Then

\displaystyle D_{s}P_{c(0)}^{c(h)}V(z)=P_{c(0)}^{c(h)}D_{s}V(z)-P_{c(0)}^{c(h)}\int_{0}^{h}P_{c(\tau)}^{c(t_{k})}R(\partial_{s}\Lambda(0,\tau),\partial_{t}\Lambda(0,\tau))P_{c(t_{k})}^{c(\tau)}V(z)d\tau,

where
D_{s}
denote the covariant derivative along
E_{i}(t)
.

Note that the
s
direction is actually arbitrary. The goal is to compute the divergence at a point,
so we can enumerate over all possible
s
direction, in all basis vectors.
Each
s
direction, roughly speaking, defines a
D_{s}
.

Proof.
[Proof of Lemma
C.3
] By construction, it holds that

\displaystyle\Lambda(0,0)=z,\Lambda(0,h)=x.

and the parallel transport
P_{z}^{x}=P_{\Lambda(0,0)}^{\Lambda(0,h)}
is along
c
.
Define
W(s,t)=P_{\Lambda(s,0)}^{\Lambda(s,t)}V(\Lambda(s,0))
,
where we perform parallel transport along curve
t\mapsto\Lambda(s,t)
which might not be a geodesic.
By definition of parallel transport,
D_{t}W(s,t)=0,\forall s
.

We know

\displaystyle-D_{t}D_{s}W(s,t)=D_{s}D_{t}W(s,t)-D_{t}D_{s}W(s,t)=R(\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,t),\partial_{t}\Lambda(s,t))W(s,t).

Hence evaluating at
s=0
, we obtain

\displaystyle D_{t}D_{s}W(0,t)=-R(\partial_{s}\Lambda(0,t),\partial_{t}\Lambda(0,t))W(0,t).

We perform parallel transport
P_{c(t)}^{c(0)}
on both sides of the equation,
and by Lemma
C.5
we have

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}P_{c(t)}^{c(0)}D_{s}W(0,t)=P_{c(t)}^{c(0)}D_{t}D_{s}W(0,t)=-P_{c(t)}^{c(0)}R(\partial_{s}\Lambda(0,t),\partial_{t}\Lambda(0,t))W(0,t).

Observe that both side of the equation is a time dependent vector field in
T_{c(0)}M=T_{z}M
.
Hence we can perform integration

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{h}\frac{d}{d\tau}P_{c(\tau)}^{c(t_{k})}D_{s}W(0,\tau)d\tau=-\int_{0}^{h}P_{c(\tau)}^{c(t_{k})}R(\partial_{s}\Lambda(0,\tau),\partial_{t}\Lambda(0,\tau))W(0,\tau)d\tau.

Hence

\displaystyle P_{c(h)}^{c(0)}D_{s}W(0,h)=D_{s}W(0,0)-\int_{0}^{h}P_{c(\tau)}^{c(t_{k})}R(\partial_{s}\Lambda(0,\tau),\partial_{t}\Lambda(0,\tau))W(0,\tau)d\tau.

Note that
D_{s}W(0,0)=D_{s}V(\Lambda(0,0))
.
Perform parallel transport
P_{c(0)}^{c(h)}
on both sides of the equation, we have

\displaystyle D_{s}W(0,h)=P_{c(0)}^{c(h)}D_{s}V(\Lambda(0,0))-P_{c(0)}^{c(h)}\int_{0}^{h}P_{c(\tau)}^{c(t_{k})}R(\partial_{s}\Lambda(0,\tau),\partial_{t}\Lambda(0,\tau))W(0,\tau)d\tau.

Recall
W(s,t)=P_{\Lambda(s,0)}^{\Lambda(s,t)}V(\Lambda(s,0))
.

\displaystyle D_{s}P_{c(0)}^{c(h)}V(\Lambda(0,0))=P_{c(0)}^{c(h)}D_{s}V(\Lambda(0,0))-P_{c(0)}^{c(h)}\int_{0}^{h}P_{c(\tau)}^{c(t_{k})}R(\partial_{s}\Lambda(0,\tau),\partial_{t}\Lambda(0,\tau))P_{c(t_{k})}^{c(\tau)}V(z)d\tau.

∎

Notice that
\tilde{v}
is defined through
\hat{v}
and the inverse of
F
. We need to control
\Div\tilde{v}(x,t)
in our analysis, by writing it as some expression involving

\Div\hat{v}
.

Lemma C.4
.

Under Assumption
1
, we have

\displaystyle|\Div(\tilde{v}(x,t)-v(x,t))|\leq\left|\Div\hat{v}(t_{k},z)-\Div v(x,t)\right|+L_{R}(t-t_{k})d\|\hat{v}(t_{k},z)\|^{2}.

where
\Exp_{z}((t-t_{k})\hat{v}(t_{k},z))=x
.

Proof.
[Proof of Lemma
C.4
]
We follow the setting in Lemma
C.3
with
V
replaced by
\hat{v}
.
We use
\{E_{i}(t)\}_{i=1}^{d}
to denote an orthonormal basis vector field along geodesic
c
.
Note that a slight difference is the time shift, where in Lemma
C.3

we have the curve is from time
0
to
h
, but here we are from
t_{k}
to
t
(so we have
h=t-t_{k}
).
We denote our time variable as
\tau
. In most cases we mean the variable
\tau
is in
[t_{k},t]
.

By definition,
\Exp_{z}((t-t_{k})\hat{v}(t_{k},z))=x
.
So we know
\Lambda(0,\tau)=\Exp_{z}((\tau-t_{k})\hat{v}(t_{k},z))
. By construction,

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\Lambda(0,\tau)=P_{c(t_{k})}^{c(\tau)}\hat{v}(t_{k},z),\quad\partial_{s}\Lambda(0,\tau)=E_{i}(\tau).

By Lemma
C.3
with
c(\tau)=\Exp_{z}((\tau-t_{k})\hat{v}(t_{k},z))
,

\displaystyle D_{s}P_{c(t_{k})}^{c(t)}\hat{v}(t_{k},z)=P_{c(t_{k})}^{c(t)}D_{s}\hat{v}(t_{k},z)-P_{c(t_{k})}^{c(t)}\int_{t_{k}}^{t}P_{c(\tau)}^{c(t_{k})}R(E_{i}(\tau),P_{c(t_{k})}^{c(\tau)}\hat{v}(t_{k},z))P_{c(t_{k})}^{c(\tau)}\hat{v}(t_{k},z)d\tau.

By definition of Riemannian divergence,

\displaystyle\Div\tilde{v}(t,x)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{d}\langle\nabla_{E_{i}(t)}\tilde{v}(t,x),E_{i}(t)\rangle=\sum_{i=1}^{d}\langle\nabla_{E_{i}(t)}P_{F_{t_{k},t-t_{k}}^{-1}(x)}^{x}\hat{v}(t_{k},F_{t_{k},t-t_{k}}^{-1}(x)),E_{i}(t)\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{d}\langle D_{s}^{(i)}P_{c(t_{k})}^{c(t)}\hat{v}(t_{k},z),E_{i}(t)\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{d}\langle P_{c(t_{k})}^{c(t)}D_{s}^{(i)}\hat{v}(t_{k},z)-P_{c(t_{k})}^{c(t)}\int_{t_{k}}^{t}P_{c(\tau)}^{c(t_{k})}R(E_{i}(\tau),P_{c(t_{k})}^{c(\tau)}\hat{v}(t_{k},z))P_{c(t_{k})}^{c(\tau)}\hat{v}(t_{k},z)d\tau,E_{i}(t)\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{d}\langle P_{c(t_{k})}^{c(t)}\nabla_{E_{i}(t_{k})}\hat{v}(t_{k},z)-P_{c(t_{k})}^{c(t)}\int_{t_{k}}^{t}P_{c(\tau)}^{c(t_{k})}R(E_{i}(\tau),P_{c(t_{k})}^{c(\tau)}\hat{v}(t_{k},z))P_{c(t_{k})}^{c(\tau)}\hat{v}(t_{k},z)d\tau,E_{i}(t)\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{d}\langle P_{c(t_{k})}^{c(t)}\nabla_{E_{i}(t_{k})}\hat{v}(t_{k},z),E_{i}(t)\rangle

\displaystyle\quad-\sum_{i=1}^{d}\langle P_{c(t_{k})}^{c(t)}\int_{t_{k}}^{t}P_{c(\tau)}^{c(t_{k})}R(E_{i}(\tau),P_{c(t_{k})}^{c(\tau)}\hat{v}(t_{k},z))P_{c(t_{k})}^{c(\tau)}\hat{v}(t_{k},z)d\tau,E_{i}(t)\rangle

\displaystyle=\Div\hat{v}(t_{k},z)-\sum_{i=1}^{d}\langle P_{c(t_{k})}^{c(t)}\int_{t_{k}}^{t}P_{c(\tau)}^{c(t_{k})}R(E_{i}(\tau),P_{c(t_{k})}^{c(\tau)}\hat{v}(t_{k},z))P_{c(t_{k})}^{c(\tau)}\hat{v}(t_{k},z)d\tau,E_{i}(t)\rangle,

where
D_{s}^{(i)}
represent the covariant derivative corresponds to
E_{i}(\tau)
.

We have

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{d}\langle P_{c(t_{k})}^{c(t)}\int_{t_{k}}^{t}P_{c(\tau)}^{c(t_{k})}R(E_{i}(\tau),P_{c(t_{k})}^{c(\tau)}\hat{v}(t_{k},z))P_{c(t_{k})}^{c(\tau)}\hat{v}(t_{k},z)d\tau,E_{i}(t)\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{d}\int_{t_{k}}^{t}\langle P_{c(\tau)}^{c(t_{k})}R(E_{i}(\tau),P_{c(t_{k})}^{c(\tau)}\hat{v}(t_{k},z))P_{c(t_{k})}^{c(\tau)}\hat{v}(t_{k},z),E_{i}(t_{k})\rangle d\tau

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{d}\int_{t_{k}}^{t}\langle R(E_{i}(\tau),P_{c(t_{k})}^{c(\tau)}\hat{v}(t_{k},z))P_{c(t_{k})}^{c(\tau)}\hat{v}(t_{k},z),E_{i}(\tau)\rangle d\tau

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{d}\int_{t_{k}}^{t}L_{R}\|\hat{v}(t_{k},z)\|^{2}d\tau\leq(t-t_{k})dL_{R}\|\hat{v}(t_{k},z)\|^{2}.

It follows that

\displaystyle|\Div(\tilde{v}(x,t)-v(x,t))|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|\Div\hat{v}(t_{k},z)-\sum_{i=1}^{d}\langle P_{c(t_{k})}^{c(t)}\int_{t_{k}}^{t}P_{c(\tau)}^{c(t_{k})}R(E_{i}(\tau),P_{c(t_{k})}^{c(\tau)}\hat{v}(t_{k},z))P_{c(t_{k})}^{c(\tau)}\hat{v}(t_{k},z)d\tau,E_{i}(t)\rangle-\Div v(x,t)|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left|\Div\hat{v}(t_{k},z)-\Div v(x,t)\right|+L_{R}(t-t_{k})d\|\hat{v}(t_{k},z)\|^{2}.

∎

Lemma C.5
.

We have that

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}P_{c(t)}^{c(0)}Y(t)=P_{c(t)}^{c(0)}D_{t}Y(t).

Proof.
[Proof of Lemma
C.5
]
Denote the geodesic along
t
direction as
c(t)
.
Let
v\in T_{c(0)}M
be arbitrary, and define
Z(t)=P_{c(0)}^{c(t)}v
.

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\langle P_{c(t)}^{c(0)}Y(t),v\rangle=\frac{d}{dt}\langle Y(t),P_{c(0)}^{c(t)}v\rangle=\langle D_{t}Y(t),P_{c(0)}^{c(t)}v\rangle+0=\langle D_{t}Y(t),P_{c(0)}^{c(t)}v\rangle=\langle P_{c(t)}^{c(0)}D_{t}Y(t),v\rangle.

On the other hand,

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\langle P_{c(t)}^{c(0)}Y(t),v\rangle=\langle\frac{d}{dt}P_{c(t)}^{c(0)}Y(t),v\rangle.

Since the above holds for any
v
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}P_{c(t)}^{c(0)}Y(t)=P_{c(t)}^{c(0)}D_{t}Y(t).

∎

Appendix D
Hypersphere Regularity Results

Recall that

\displaystyle v(t,x)=\frac{1}{1-t}\int_{M}\Log_{x}(x_{1})\,p_{t}(x_{1}|x)\,dV_{g}(x_{1}).

To establish regularity of
v
, it is natural to study the formula for conditional density,
p_{t}(x_{1}|x)
.
In a Euclidean space, with Gaussian distribution as prior
p_{0}
, we have that
p_{t}(x_{1}\mid x_{t}=x)\propto p_{1}(x_{1})\exp(-\frac{\|tx_{1}-x\|^{2}}{2(1-t)^{2}})
. This is a standard result for flow matching, see for example
Guan et al. 2026
and
Zhou and Liu 2025
. But on a Riemannian manifold, the curvature would introduce an extra term due to the change of variable formula, and the existence of cut points would introduce an extra indicator function.
We provide the formula for conditional density on the hypersphere
\mathcal{S}^{d}
with uniform distribution as prior, and geodesic interpolation. See Lemma
D.1
below.

Lemma D.1

(Conditional density on
S^{d}
for geodesic interpolation)
.

Let
S^{d}
be the unit sphere with round metric,
d\geq 2
.
Let
X_{1}\sim p_{1}
be the data distribution with smooth
densities
p_{1}>0
w.r.t.
dV_{g}
, and
X_{0}\sim p_{0}
being uniform distribution independent of
X_{1}
.
Consider geodesic interpolation with minimizing geodesic.
Fix
t\in[0,1)
and
x,x_{1}\in S^{d}
.
Write
r=d(x,x_{1})
.
Denote

\displaystyle J_{t}(x\mid x_{1})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1-t}\Bigl(\frac{\sin\bigl(r/(1-t)\bigr)}{\sin r}\Bigr)^{d-1}\mathbf{1}_{\{d(x,x_{1})<(1-t)\pi\}}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{1-t}\Bigl(\frac{\sin\bigl(r/(1-t)\bigr)}{\sin r}\Bigr)^{d-1},&\text{ if }r<(1-t)\pi,\\
0,&\text{ if }r\geq(1-t)\pi.\end{cases}

Then the conditional density of
X_{1}
given
X_{t}=x
is

\displaystyle p_{t}(x_{1}\mid x)

\displaystyle=\frac{p_{1}(x_{1})\,J_{t}(x\mid x_{1})}{\displaystyle\int_{S^{d}}p_{1}(z)\,J_{t}(x\mid z)dV_{g}(z)}.

Proof.
The proof strategy is as follows.
We first write out the joint distribution of
X_{0},X_{1}
,
and then use the change of variable formula to obtain joint distribution of

X_{t},X_{1}
.

We apply the change of variable formula. We need a diffeomorphism between
(X_{0},X_{1})
and
(X_{t},X_{1})
.
Fix
t\in(0,1)
and
x_{1}\in S^{d}
.
Use polar coordinates at
x_{1}
:
every
y\in S^{d}\setminus\{-x_{1}\}
can be written uniquely as

y=\Exp_{x_{1}}(r\omega)
for some
r\in(0,\pi),\omega\in S^{d-1}
.
And we know the Riemannian volume element is

\displaystyle dV_{g}(y)=(\sin r)^{d-1}dr\,d\omega.

In particular, for any
x_{0}
, we set
r_{0}=d(x_{0},x_{1})\in(0,\pi)

and
\omega=\frac{\Log_{x_{1}}(x_{0})}{\|\Log_{x_{1}}(x_{0})\|}
.
Then we can write

\displaystyle x_{0}=\Exp_{x_{1}}(r_{0}\omega),\qquad x_{1}=\Exp_{x_{1}}(0\cdot\omega).

and

\displaystyle X_{t}=\Exp_{x_{0}}\bigl(t\Log_{x_{0}}(x_{1})\bigr)=\Exp_{x_{1}}\bigl((1-t)r_{0}\,\omega\bigr).

Thus we can define the desired diffeomorphism as
F_{t}:(r_{0},\omega)\mapsto(r,\omega)
satisfying

\displaystyle F_{t,x_{1}}(r_{0},\omega)=((1-t)r_{0},\omega),\qquad F_{t,x_{1}}^{-1}(r,\omega)=(\frac{r}{1-t},\omega).

But however, note that we have to restrict
r<(1-t)\pi
, otherwise
\frac{r}{1-t}\notin(0,\pi)
,
consequently

F_{t,x_{1}}^{-1}(r,\omega)
is no longer under the polar coordinate.

Now recall the change of variable formula.
We should have

\displaystyle\int_{S^{d}}p_{0}(F_{t,x_{1}}^{-1}(x))|\det dF_{t,x_{1}}^{-1}|dV_{g}(x)=\int_{S^{d}}p_{0}(x_{0})dV_{g}(x_{0}).

Written in polar coordinates:
the volume element at
x_{0}
and
x
are

\displaystyle dV_{g}(x_{0})=(\sin r_{0})^{d-1}dr_{0}\,d\omega,\qquad dV_{g}(x)=(\sin r)^{d-1}dr\,d\omega.

Define a function
J_{t}(x\mid x_{1})
to satisfy

\displaystyle dV_{g}(x_{0})=J_{t}(x\mid x_{1})\,dV_{g}(x).

Using
r_{0}=r/(1-t)
(hence
dr_{0}=dr/(1-t)
), we get

\displaystyle(\sin r_{0})^{d-1}dr_{0}\,d\omega

\displaystyle=J_{t}(x\mid x_{1})(\sin r)^{d-1}dr\,d\omega

\displaystyle(\sin(r/(1-t)))^{d-1}\frac{dr}{1-t}d\omega

\displaystyle=J_{t}(x\mid x_{1})(\sin r)^{d-1}dr\,d\omega,

Now we derive the density. Note that by our construction of geodesic interpolation, given any
x_{0}
and
t
,
the resulting
x
must satisfy
d(x_{1},x)=(1-t)d(x_{1},x_{0})\leq(1-t)\pi
.
Hence

\displaystyle J_{t}(x\mid x_{1})=\frac{1}{1-t}\Bigl(\frac{\sin\bigl(r/(1-t)\bigr)}{\sin r}\Bigr)^{d-1},\qquad r=d(x,x_{1}),

for all
r<(1-t)\pi
, and
J_{t}(x\mid x_{1})=0
otherwise. Thus, we can equivalently write

\displaystyle J_{t}(x\mid x_{1})=\frac{1}{1-t}\Bigl(\frac{\sin\bigl(r/(1-t)\bigr)}{\sin r}\Bigr)^{d-1}\mathbf{1}_{\{d(x,x_{1})<(1-t)\pi\}}.

The joint density of
(X_{0},X_{1})
is

\displaystyle p(x_{0},x_{1})=p_{0}(x_{0})p_{1}(x_{1})

with respect to
dV_{g}(x_{0})\,dV_{g}(x_{1})
.
By the change of variable formula,

\displaystyle p_{t}(x,x_{1})

\displaystyle=p_{0}(F_{t,x_{1}}^{-1}(x))p_{1}(x_{1})J_{t}(x\mid x_{1}).

Finally, since
p_{t}(x,x_{1})=p_{t}(x_{1}\mid x)p_{t}(x)=p_{t}(x_{1}\mid x)\int_{S^{d}}p_{t}(x,z)\,dV_{g}(z)
,
the conditional density of
X_{1}
given
X_{t}=x
is

\displaystyle p_{t}(x_{1}\mid x)=\frac{p_{t}(x,x_{1})}{\int_{S^{d}}p_{t}(x,z)\,dV_{g}(z)},

which gives the formula stated in the lemma.

∎

Following Lemma
D.1
, we can derive the following formula for interpolated density.

Lemma D.2
.

We can write the interpolated density as

\displaystyle p_{t}(x)=\int_{S^{d}}p_{t}(x,x_{1})\,dV_{g}(x_{1})=\frac{1}{\Vol(S^{d})}\int_{S^{d}}p_{1}(x_{1})J_{t}(x\mid x_{1})dV_{g}(x_{1}).

Proof.

Following Lemma
D.1
, we have

\displaystyle p_{t}(x,x_{1})

\displaystyle=p_{0}(F_{t,x_{1}}^{-1}(x))p_{1}(x_{1})J_{t}(x\mid x_{1}).

Consequently, we have (note that
p_{0}
is uniform distribution)

\displaystyle p_{t}(x)=\int_{S^{d}}p_{t}(x,x_{1})\,dV_{g}(x_{1})=\frac{1}{\Vol(S^{d})}\int_{S^{d}}p_{1}(x_{1})J_{t}(x\mid x_{1})dV_{g}(x_{1}).

∎

Before justifying the regularity of
v
, we first need to show that its smoothness is not destroyed by the indicator function, which corresponds to the cut point. In the following Lemma, we show that on a Hypersphere, the
\sin
function (that appeared in the conditional density function) would smooth out the indicator function, resulting in a smooth vector field.

Lemma D.3
.

Let
\mathcal{S}^{d}
be the unit hypersphere with round metric,
d\geq 2
.
Let
x_{1}\in\mathcal{S}^{d}
and define, for
t\in[0,1)
and
x\in\mathcal{S}^{d}
. Denote
r(x)=d(x,x_{1})
as the radial distance function.
Then for integer
m
with
0\leq m\leq d-2
, the function
J(t,x\mid x_{1})
viewed as a function on
[0,1)\times\mathcal{S}^{d}
is
C^{m}
.

Consequently,
v(t,x)
as a conditional expectation is
C^{2}
in
(t,x)
for
t\in[0,1)
.
As a result, the solution for flow matching ODE exists and unique.

Proof.

Denote
\mathcal{U}:=\{(t,x)\in[0,1)\times\mathcal{S}^{d}:\ r(x)<(1-t)\pi\}
.
We first show the smoothness on
\mathcal{U}
.
Fix
(t_{0},x_{0})\in\mathcal{U}
where
x_{0}\neq x_{1}
, and write
r_{0}:=r(x_{0})>0
.
Since
r_{0}<(1-t_{0})\pi<\pi
, we have
x_{0}\neq-x_{1}
.
Moreover, by continuity of
r(\cdot)
there exists a neighborhood

\mathcal{V}
of
(t_{0},x_{0})
such that for all
(t,x)\in\mathcal{V}
,

\mathbf{1}_{\{r(x)<(1-t)\pi\}}\equiv 1
, and consequently

\displaystyle J(t,x\mid x_{1})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1-t}\Bigl(\frac{\sin\bigl(r(x)/(1-t)\bigr)}{\sin r(x)}\Bigr)^{d-1}.

Since
r=d(x,x_{1})
is smooth when
x\notin\{x_{1},-x_{1}\}
,

J
is smooth as a composition of smooth functions.

It remains to check the smoothness of
J(t,x\mid x_{1})
at point
x=x_{1}
(
r=0
).
Introduce normal coordinates at
x_{1}
:
for
x
near
x_{1}
write

\displaystyle v:=\Log_{x_{1}}(x)\in T_{x_{1}}\mathcal{S}^{d},\qquad r(x)=d(x,x_{1})=\|v\|.

It suffices to show that under normal coordinates,
J
viewed as a function of
v
, is smooth.
Notice that
\|v\|
is not differentiable at
v=0
, hence functions such as

v\mapsto\sin\|v\|
are not
C^{1}
at
0
.
But notice that in
J
, the “non-smooth” part on
\|v\|
cancels.
By checking smoothness of

\displaystyle\frac{\sin(\|v\|/(1-t))}{\sin\|v\|},

we conclude the smoothness of
J
on
U
.

We extend the smoothness result by checking the boundary
r=(1-t)\pi
.
To prove
C^{m}
, fix
(t_{0},x_{0})
with
r(x_{0})=(1-t_{0})\pi
.
Since
r(x_{0})\in(0,\pi)
, we have
x\mapsto r(x)
is smooth near
x_{0}
.
Set
s(t,x)
as follows, describing how far will
x
reach the boundary:

\displaystyle s(t,x):=(1-t)\pi-r(x).

On the side
s>0
we have
u:=r/(1-t)\in(0,\pi)
and

\displaystyle\sin\Bigl(\frac{r}{1-t}\Bigr)=\sin\Bigl(\pi-\frac{s}{1-t}\Bigr)=\sin\Bigl(\frac{s}{1-t}\Bigr).

Therefore, for
s>0
,

\displaystyle J(t,x\mid x_{1})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1-t}\Bigl(\frac{\sin\bigl(s(t,x)/(1-t)\bigr)}{\sin r(x)}\Bigr)^{d-1}=s(t,x)^{d-1}(1-t)^{-d}\left(\frac{\frac{\sin\bigl(s(t,x)/(1-t)\bigr)}{s(t,x)/(1-t)}}{\sin r(x)}\right)^{d-1}.

On the whole manifold
\mathcal{S}^{d}
, we have

\displaystyle J(t,x\mid x_{1})=\bigl(s(t,x)\bigr)_{+}^{\,d-1}\,(1-t)^{-d}\left(\frac{\frac{\sin\bigl(s(t,x)/(1-t)\bigr)}{s(t,x)/(1-t)}}{\sin r(x)}\right)^{d-1},\qquad(s)_{+}:=\max\{s,0\}.

Since
s\mapsto(s)_{+}^{\,d-1}
is
C^{m}
as a one-dimensional function for every
m\leq d-2
,
and since
s(t,x)
is smooth in
(t,x)
, the composition

\bigl(s(t,x)\bigr)_{+}^{\,d-1}
is
C^{m}
in
(t,x)
for all
m\leq d-2
.
It follows that
J
is
C^{m}
, as a product of a smooth function and
\bigl(s(t,x)\bigr)_{+}^{\,d-1}
.
∎

Remark 2
.

The fact that
v
being
C^{2}
in
(t,x)
for
t\in[0,1)
follows from
smoothness of
J
, and the following properties of
\Log
: (1)
\Log
being uniformly bounded, and (2) singularity of derivatives of
\Log_{x}(x_{1})
as
x_{1}\to-x
is well controlled under polar coordinates.

The existence and uniqueness of flow matching ODE follows from classical theory on time-dependent flow,
see for example
[
Marsden et al. 2002
, Section 4.1]
and
[
Lee 2012
, Theorem 9.48]
.

D.1
Auxiliary Lemmas

The following result studies the derivative of a function that only depends on the radial distance. In other words, if a function
\phi(x)
on
M
only depends on
x
through
r(x)=d(x,x_{1})
for some fixed
x_{1}
, then we can control its derivative through derivative of
r
.

Lemma D.4
.

Fix
x_{1}\in\mathcal{S}^{d}
and define
r(x):=d(x,x_{1})
. Assume
x\notin\Cut(x_{1})
,
so that
r
is smooth in a neighborhood of
x
and
\|\grad_{x}r(x)\|=1
.
Let
F\colon(0,\pi)\to\mathbb{R}
be
C^{2}
and set
\phi(x):=F(r(x))
.
Then

\displaystyle\grad_{x}\phi(x)=F^{\prime}(r(x))\,\grad_{x}r(x),\qquad\|\grad_{x}\phi(x)\|=|F^{\prime}(r(x))|.

Moreover,

\displaystyle\|\nabla\grad\phi(x)\|_{\op}\leq|F^{\prime}(r(x))|\,\|\nabla\grad r(x)\|_{\op}+|F^{\prime\prime}(r(x))|.

Proof.

We apply the chain rule for the Riemannian gradient. For any
u\in T_{x}S^{d}
, the differential satisfies

\displaystyle d\phi(x)[u]=d(F\circ r)(x)[u]=F^{\prime}(r(x))\,dr(x)[u].

By the definition of the Riemannian gradient,

\displaystyle\langle\grad_{x}\phi,u\rangle=F^{\prime}(r(x))\,\langle\grad_{x}r,u\rangle,\qquad\forall u\in T_{x}S^{d},

hence

\displaystyle\grad_{x}\phi(x)=F^{\prime}(r(x))\,\grad_{x}r(x).

Taking norms gives

\displaystyle\|\grad_{x}\phi(x)\|=|F^{\prime}(r(x))|\,\|\grad_{x}r(x)\|.

Since
x\notin\Cut(x_{1})
, the distance function satisfies
\|\grad_{x}r(x)\|=1
,
and therefore

\displaystyle\|\grad_{x}\phi(x)\|=|F^{\prime}(r(x))|.

Next, for any
u\in T_{x}S^{d}
,

\displaystyle\nabla_{u}\grad\phi(x)

\displaystyle=\nabla_{u}\bigl(F^{\prime}(r(x))\,\grad r(x)\bigr)

\displaystyle=F^{\prime}(r(x))\,\nabla_{u}\grad r(x)+u\left(F^{\prime}(r(x))\right)\grad r(x)

\displaystyle=F^{\prime}(r(x))\,\nabla_{u}\grad r(x)+F^{\prime\prime}(r(x))\,u(r(x))\,\grad r(x)

\displaystyle=F^{\prime}(r(x))\,\nabla_{u}\grad r(x)+F^{\prime\prime}(r(x))\,\langle\grad r(x),u\rangle\,\grad r(x).

Hence, for
\|u\|=1
,

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{u}\grad\phi(x)\|

\displaystyle\leq|F^{\prime}(r(x))|\,\|\nabla_{u}\grad r(x)\|+|F^{\prime\prime}(r(x))|\,|\langle\grad r(x),u\rangle|\,\|\grad r(x)\|

\displaystyle\leq|F^{\prime}(r(x))|\,\|\nabla_{u}\grad r(x)\|+|F^{\prime\prime}(r(x))|,

using
\|\grad r(x)\|=1
and
|\langle\grad r(x),u\rangle|\leq\|u\|=1
.
Taking the supremum over
\|u\|=1
yields

\displaystyle\|\nabla\grad\phi(x)\|_{\op}\leq|F^{\prime}(r(x))|\,\|\nabla\grad r(x)\|_{\op}+|F^{\prime\prime}(r(x))|.

∎

The following result controls the conditional expectation of some certain “functions with singularity”, which will be used to establish regularity.

Lemma D.5

(Expectation of
\frac{1}{\sin^{a}u}
.)
.

Consider
d\geq 3
and
a=1,2
. Let
r=d(x,x_{1})
and set
u=r/(1-t)\in(0,\pi)
. We have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\frac{1}{\sin^{a}u}\mid X_{t}=x]\leq 2\frac{M_{1}}{m_{1}},

where
0<m_{1}\leq p_{1}\leq M_{1}
.

Proof.

We have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigl[\frac{1}{\sin^{a}u}\mid X_{t}=x\bigr]

\displaystyle=\int_{S^{d}}\frac{1}{\sin^{a}u}\,p_{t}(x_{1}\mid x)\,dV_{g}(x_{1})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{M_{1}}{m_{1}\Vol(S^{d})}\int_{S^{d}}\frac{1}{\sin^{a}u}\,J_{t}(x\mid x_{1})\,dV_{g}(x_{1}).

Use
r=(1-t)u
so that
\frac{1}{1-t}dr=du
,

\displaystyle\int_{S^{d}}\frac{1}{\sin^{a}u}\,J_{t}(x\mid x_{1})\,dV_{g}(x_{1})

\displaystyle\qquad=\int_{S^{d-1}}\int_{0}^{\pi}\frac{1}{\sin^{a}u}\ J_{t}(x\mid x_{1})\,(\sin r)^{d-1}drd\omega

\displaystyle\qquad=\Vol(S^{d-1})\int_{0}^{\pi}\frac{1}{\sin^{a}u}\sin(u)^{d-1}\,du

\displaystyle\qquad=\Vol(S^{d-1})\int_{0}^{\pi}\sin(u)^{d-1-a}\,du.

Therefore

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigl[\frac{1}{\sin^{a}u}\mid X_{t}=x\bigr]

\displaystyle=\int_{S^{d}}\frac{1}{\sin^{a}u}\,p_{t}(x_{1}\mid x)\,dV_{g}(x_{1})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{M_{1}\Vol(S^{d-1})}{m_{1}\Vol(S^{d})}\int_{0}^{\pi}\sin(u)^{d-1-a}\,du.

\displaystyle=\frac{M_{1}\Vol(S^{d-1})}{m_{1}\Vol(S^{d})\Vol(S^{d-1-a})}\Vol(S^{d-1-a})\int_{0}^{\pi}\sin(u)^{d-1-a}\,du

\displaystyle=\frac{M_{1}\Vol(S^{d-1})}{m_{1}\Vol(S^{d})\Vol(S^{d-1-a})}\Vol(S^{d-a}).

Recall

\displaystyle\Vol(S^{n})=\frac{2\pi^{(n+1)/2}}{\Gamma((n+1)/2)}.

Hence

\displaystyle\frac{\Vol(S^{d-1})}{\Vol(S^{d})}

\displaystyle=\frac{\frac{2\pi^{d/2}}{\Gamma(d/2)}}{\frac{2\pi^{(d+1)/2}}{\Gamma((d+1)/2)}}=\pi^{-1/2}\frac{\Gamma((d+1)/2)}{\Gamma(d/2)},

\displaystyle\frac{\Vol(S^{d-a})}{\Vol(S^{d-1-a})}

\displaystyle=\frac{\frac{2\pi^{(d-a+1)/2}}{\Gamma((d-a+1)/2)}}{\frac{2\pi^{(d-a)/2}}{\Gamma((d-a)/2)}}=\pi^{1/2}\frac{\Gamma((d-a)/2)}{\Gamma((d-a+1)/2)}.

By Kershaw’s inequality, for
d\geq 3
,

\displaystyle\frac{\Gamma((d+1)/2)}{\Gamma(d/2)}\leq(d/2-\frac{1}{2}+\sqrt{\frac{3}{4}})^{\frac{1}{2}},

and

\displaystyle\frac{\Gamma(d/2)}{\Gamma((d-1)/2)}\geq(\frac{d}{2}-1+\frac{1}{2})^{\frac{1}{2}},

\displaystyle\frac{\Gamma((d-a+1)/2)}{\Gamma((d-a)/2)}\geq(\frac{d-a-1}{2}+\frac{1}{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Together,

\displaystyle\frac{\Vol(S^{d-1})\Vol(S^{d-a})}{\Vol(S^{d})\Vol(S^{d-1-a})}

\displaystyle=\frac{\Gamma((d+1)/2)\Gamma((d-a)/2)}{\Gamma(d/2)\Gamma((d-a+1)/2)}\leq(\frac{d/2-\frac{1}{2}+\sqrt{\frac{3}{4}}}{\frac{d-a-1}{2}+\frac{1}{2}})^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle=(\frac{d-a+a-1+2\sqrt{\frac{3}{4}}}{d-a})^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq(1+\frac{a-1+2\sqrt{\frac{3}{4}}}{d-a})^{\frac{1}{2}}.

For
a=1,2
, we have
(1+\frac{a-1+2\sqrt{\frac{3}{4}}}{d-a})^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq 2
.
∎

The following results (Lemmas
D.6
,
D.7
,
D.8
, and
D.9
) provide bounds on the building blocks that appear in the derivative formulas for
v(t,x)
. For all the results below, we assume
0<m_{1}\leq p_{1}(x)\leq M_{1}
.

Lemma D.6

(Moment bounds for
\grad_{x}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x)
.)
.

We have

\displaystyle\|\grad_{x}\log J_{t}(x\mid x_{1})\|\leq\frac{2(d-1)}{(1-t)\sin u}.

Furthermore,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|\grad_{x}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x)\|\mid X_{t}=x]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{8(d-1)}{(1-t)}\frac{M_{1}}{m_{1}},

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|\grad_{x}\log J_{t}(x\mid X_{1})\|^{2}\mid X_{t}=x]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{8(d-1)^{2}}{(1-t)^{2}}\frac{M_{1}}{m_{1}},

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|\grad_{x}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x)\|^{2}\mid X_{t}=x]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{32(d-1)^{2}}{(1-t)^{2}}\frac{M_{1}}{m_{1}}.

Proof.

We first bound
\|\grad_{x}\log J_{t}(x\mid x_{1})\|
.
Let
r=d(x,x_{1})
and set
u=r/(1-t)\in(0,\pi)
. On the set
r<(1-t)\pi
,

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial r}\log J_{t}(r)=(d-1)\Bigl(\frac{1}{1-t}\cot\frac{r}{1-t}-\cot r\Bigr)=(d-1)\Bigl(\frac{1}{1-t}\cot u-\cot((1-t)u)\Bigr).

By the Lemma
D.4
,

\displaystyle\|\grad_{x}\log J_{t}(x\mid x_{1})\|=\Bigl|\frac{\partial}{\partial r}\log J_{t}(r)\Bigr|=(d-1)\Bigl|\frac{1}{1-t}\cot u-\cot((1-t)u)\Bigr|.

For
u\in(0,\pi)
, we have
|\cot u|\leq 1/\sin u
.
Since
\sin((1-t)u)\geq(1-t)\sin u
, we have

\displaystyle|\cot((1-t)u)|\leq\frac{1}{\sin((1-t)u)}\leq\frac{1}{(1-t)\sin u}.

Therefore for all
u\in(0,\pi)
, we can bound

\displaystyle\Bigl|\frac{1}{1-t}\cot u-\cot((1-t)u)\Bigr|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{1-t}|\cot u|+|\cot((1-t)u)|\leq\frac{1}{(1-t)\sin u}+\frac{1}{(1-t)\sin u}

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{(1-t)\sin u}.

So

\displaystyle\|\grad_{x}\log J_{t}(x\mid x_{1})\|\leq\frac{2(d-1)}{(1-t)\sin u}.

Now we bound
\mathbb{E}\|\grad_{x}\log p_{t}\|
by
\mathbb{E}\|\grad_{x}\log J_{t}\|
.
Let

\displaystyle Z_{t}(x):=\int_{S^{d}}p_{1}(z)J_{t}(x\mid z)\,dV_{g}(z),

so

\displaystyle\log p_{t}(x_{1}\mid x)=\log p_{1}(x_{1})+\log J_{t}(x\mid x_{1})-\log Z_{t}(x).

Since
p_{1}
does not depend on
x
,

\displaystyle\grad_{x}\log p_{t}(x_{1}\mid x)=\grad_{x}\log J_{t}(x\mid x_{1})-\grad_{x}\log Z_{t}(x).

Also,

\displaystyle\grad_{x}Z_{t}(x)=\int_{S^{d}}p_{1}(z)\grad_{x}J_{t}(x\mid z)\,dV_{g}(z)=\int_{S^{d}}p_{1}(z)J_{t}(x\mid z)\grad_{x}\log J_{t}(x\mid z)\,dV_{g}(z),

hence

\displaystyle\grad_{x}\log Z_{t}(x)=\int_{S^{d}}\grad_{x}\log J_{t}(x\mid z)\,p_{t}(z\mid x)\,dV_{g}(z)=\mathbb{E}\bigl[\grad_{x}\log J_{t}(x\mid X_{1})\mid X_{t}=x\bigr].

Therefore

\displaystyle\grad_{x}\log p_{t}(x_{1}\mid x)=\grad_{x}\log J_{t}(x\mid x_{1})-\mathbb{E}\bigl[\grad_{x}\log J_{t}(x\mid X_{1})\mid X_{t}=x\bigr],

and by triangle inequality,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigl[\|\grad_{x}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x)\|\mid X_{t}=x\bigr]\leq 2\,\mathbb{E}\bigl[\|\grad_{x}\log J_{t}(x\mid X_{1})\|\mid X_{t}=x\bigr]\leq\frac{8(d-1)}{(1-t)}\frac{M_{1}}{m_{1}},

where we used Lemma
D.5
.

For second moment, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|\grad_{x}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x)\|^{2}\mid X_{t}=x]\leq 4\,\mathbb{E}[\|\grad_{x}\log J_{t}(x\mid X_{1})\|^{2}\mid X_{t}=x].

Recall
\|\grad_{x}\log J_{t}(x\mid x_{1})\|\leq\frac{2(d-1)}{(1-t)\sin u}
, using Lemma
D.5
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|\grad_{x}\log J_{t}(x\mid X_{1})\|^{2}\mid X_{t}=x]\leq\frac{4(d-1)^{2}}{(1-t)^{2}}\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\frac{1}{\sin^{2}u}\mid X_{t}=x\Bigr]\leq\frac{8(d-1)^{2}}{(1-t)^{2}}\frac{M_{1}}{m_{1}}.

Hence

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|\grad_{x}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x)\|^{2}\mid X_{t}=x]\leq\frac{32(d-1)^{2}}{(1-t)^{2}}\frac{M_{1}}{m_{1}}.

∎

Lemma D.7

(Moment bounds for
\partial_{t}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x)
.)
.

We have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\Bigl|\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x)\Bigr|\mid X_{t}=x\Bigr]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{1-t}+4\pi\frac{d-1}{1-t}\,\frac{M_{1}}{m_{1}},

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\Bigl|\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x)\Bigr|^{2}\mid X_{t}=x\Bigr]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{8}{(1-t)^{2}}+\frac{16\pi^{2}(d-1)^{2}}{(1-t)^{2}}\,\frac{M_{1}}{m_{1}}.

Proof.
Let

\displaystyle Z_{t}(x)=\int_{S^{d}}p_{1}(z)J_{t}(x\mid z)\,dV_{g}(z),

so we have
\log p_{t}(x_{1}\mid x)=\log p_{1}(x_{1})+\log J_{t}(x\mid x_{1})-\log Z_{t}(x)
.
Since
p_{1}
does not depend on
t
,

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\log p_{t}(x_{1}\mid x)=\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\log J_{t}(x\mid x_{1})-\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\log Z_{t}(x).

Moreover, for
d\geq 2
the function
\partial_{t}J_{t}(x\mid z)
is integrable
against
p_{1}(z)dV_{g}(z)
, and the map
t\mapsto Z_{t}(x)
is
C^{1}
with

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial t}Z_{t}(x)=\int_{S^{d}}p_{1}(z)\frac{\partial}{\partial t}J_{t}(x\mid z)\,dV_{g}(z)=\int_{S^{d}}p_{1}(z)J_{t}(x\mid z)\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\log J_{t}(x\mid z)\,dV_{g}(z).

Dividing by
Z_{t}(x)
yields

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\log Z_{t}(x)=\int_{S^{d}}\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\log J_{t}(x\mid z)\,p_{t}(z\mid x)\,dV_{g}(z)=\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\log J_{t}(x\mid X_{1})\mid X_{t}=x\Bigr].

Therefore

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\log p_{t}(x_{1}\mid x)=\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\log J_{t}(x\mid x_{1})-\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\log J_{t}(x\mid X_{1})\mid X_{t}=x\Bigr],

and by triangle inequality,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\Bigl|\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x)\Bigr|\mid X_{t}=x\Bigr]

\displaystyle\leq 2\,\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\Bigl|\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\log J_{t}(x\mid X_{1})\Bigr|\mid X_{t}=x\Bigr],

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\Bigl|\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x)\Bigr|^{2}\mid X_{t}=x\Bigr]

\displaystyle\leq 4\,\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\Bigl|\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\log J_{t}(x\mid X_{1})\Bigr|^{2}\mid X_{t}=x\Bigr].

We compute
\partial_{t}\log J_{t}
explicitly and bound its conditional expectation.
For
r=d(x,x_{1})<(1-t)\pi
,

\displaystyle\log J_{t}(x\mid x_{1})=-\log(1-t)+(d-1)\Bigl(\log\sin(r/(1-t))-\log\sin r\Bigr).

Holding
r
fixed and differentiating in
t
gives

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\log J_{t}(x\mid x_{1})=\frac{1}{1-t}+(d-1)\frac{r}{(1-t)^{2}}\cot(r/(1-t)).

Write
u=r/(1-t)\in(0,\pi)
. Then
r=(1-t)u
and

\displaystyle\Bigl|\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\log J_{t}(x\mid x_{1})\Bigr|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{1-t}+\frac{d-1}{1-t}\,|u\cot u|,

\displaystyle\Bigl|\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\log J_{t}(x\mid x_{1})\Bigr|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\frac{1}{(1-t)^{2}}+2\frac{(d-1)^{2}}{(1-t)^{2}}\,|u\cot u|^{2}.

Hence

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\Bigl|\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\log J_{t}(x\mid X_{1})\Bigr|\mid X_{t}=x\Bigr]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{1-t}+\pi\frac{d-1}{1-t}\,\frac{2M_{1}}{m_{1}},

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\Bigl|\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\log J_{t}(x\mid X_{1})\Bigr|^{2}\mid X_{t}=x\Bigr]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{(1-t)^{2}}+\frac{2\pi^{2}(d-1)^{2}}{(1-t)^{2}}\,\frac{2M_{1}}{m_{1}}.

where we used
|u\cot u|\leq\frac{\pi}{\sin u}
.
Consequently,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\Bigl|\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x)\Bigr|\mid X_{t}=x\Bigr]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{1-t}+4\pi\frac{d-1}{1-t}\,\frac{M_{1}}{m_{1}},

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\Bigl|\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x)\Bigr|^{2}\mid X_{t}=x\Bigr]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{8}{(1-t)^{2}}+\frac{16\pi^{2}(d-1)^{2}}{(1-t)^{2}}\,\frac{M_{1}}{m_{1}}.

∎

Lemma D.8
.

We have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigl[\|\nabla_{x}^{2}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x)\|_{\op}\mid X_{t}=x\bigr]\leq\frac{64(d-1)^{2}}{(1-t)^{2}}\frac{M_{1}}{m_{1}},

Proof.

Recall that

\displaystyle\grad_{x}\log p_{t}(x_{1}\mid x)=\grad_{x}\log J_{t}(x\mid x_{1})-\grad_{x}\log Z_{t}(x),

Take derivative again, we obtain

\displaystyle\nabla\grad_{x}\log p_{t}(x_{1}\mid x)=\nabla\grad_{x}\log J_{t}(x\mid x_{1})-\nabla\grad_{x}\log Z_{t}(x),

Recall

\displaystyle\grad_{x}\log Z_{t}(x)=\mathbb{E}\bigl[\grad_{x}\log J_{t}(x\mid X_{1})\mid X_{t}=x\bigr].

Hence

\displaystyle\nabla_{u}\grad_{x}\log Z_{t}(x)=\nabla_{u}\mathbb{E}\bigl[\grad_{x}\log J_{t}(x\mid X_{1})\mid X_{t}=x\bigr]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigl[\nabla_{u}\grad_{x}\log J_{t}(x\mid X_{1})\mid X_{t}=x\bigr]+\mathbb{E}\bigl[\grad_{x}\log J_{t}(x\mid X_{1})\nabla_{u}\log p_{t}(x_{1}\mid x)\mid X_{t}=x\bigr],

and we can bound

\displaystyle\|\nabla\grad_{x}\log Z_{t}(x)\|_{\op}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\bigl[\|\nabla^{2}\log J_{t}(x\mid X_{1})\|_{\op}\mid X_{t}=x\bigr]

\displaystyle\qquad+\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\bigl[\|\grad_{x}\log J_{t}(x\mid X_{1})\|^{2}\mid X_{t}=x\bigr]\mathbb{E}\bigl[\|\nabla\log p_{t}(x_{1}\mid x)\|^{2}\mid X_{t}=x\bigr]}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\bigl[\|\nabla^{2}\log J_{t}(x\mid X_{1})\|_{\op}\mid X_{t}=x\bigr]+\frac{16(d-1)^{2}M_{1}}{(1-t)^{2}m_{1}}.

To control
\|\nabla^{2}\log J_{t}(x\mid X_{1})\|_{\op}
, we consider

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial r^{2}}\log J_{t}(x\mid X_{1})

\displaystyle=\frac{\partial}{\partial r}(d-1)(\frac{1}{1-t}\cot\frac{r}{1-t}-\cot r)=(d-1)(-\frac{1}{(1-t)^{2}}\frac{1}{\sin^{2}\frac{r}{1-t}}+\frac{1}{\sin^{2}r})

\displaystyle=(d-1)(\frac{1}{\sin^{2}(1-t)u}-\frac{1}{(1-t)^{2}}\frac{1}{\sin^{2}u}).

Applying Lemma
D.4
with
\phi=\log J_{t}(r)
, we have

\displaystyle\|\nabla\grad\log J_{t}(r)\|_{\op}

\displaystyle\leq|\log J_{t}^{\prime}(r(x))|\|\nabla\grad r(x)\|_{\op}+|\log J_{t}^{\prime\prime}(r(x))|

\displaystyle\leq|\log J_{t}^{\prime}(r(x))|\cot r+|\log J_{t}^{\prime\prime}(r(x))|,

where
\|\nabla\grad r(x)\|_{\op}\leq|\cot r|
by
Lee 2018

Now using

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial r}\log J_{t}(x\mid X_{1})

\displaystyle=(d-1)(\frac{1}{1-t}\cot\frac{r}{1-t}-\cot r),

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial r^{2}}\log J_{t}(x\mid X_{1})

\displaystyle=(d-1)(\frac{1}{\sin^{2}(1-t)u}-\frac{1}{(1-t)^{2}}\frac{1}{\sin^{2}u}),

we obtain

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{2}\log J_{t}(x\mid X_{1})\|_{\op}\leq\frac{4(d-1)}{(1-t)^{2}\sin^{2}u},

hence

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|\nabla^{2}\log J_{t}(x\mid X_{1})\|\mid X_{t}=x]\leq\frac{16(d-1)}{(1-t)^{2}}\frac{M_{1}}{m_{1}}.

Now we bound
\mathbb{E}[\|\nabla_{x}^{2}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x)\|_{\op}\mid X_{t}=x]
.
Recall

\displaystyle\nabla\grad_{x}\log p_{t}(x_{1}\mid x)

\displaystyle=\nabla\grad_{x}\log J_{t}(x\mid x_{1})-\nabla\grad_{x}\log Z_{t}(x),

\displaystyle\|\nabla\grad_{x}\log Z_{t}(x)\|_{\op}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\bigl[\|\nabla^{2}\log J_{t}(x\mid X_{1})\|_{\op}\mid X_{t}=x\bigr]+\frac{16(d-1)^{2}M_{1}}{(1-t)^{2}m_{1}}.

By triangle inequality, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigl[\|\nabla_{x}^{2}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x)\|_{\op}\mid X_{t}=x\bigr]\leq 2\,\mathbb{E}\bigl[\|\nabla_{x}^{2}\log J_{t}(x\mid X_{1})\|_{\op}\mid X_{t}=x\bigr]+\frac{16(d-1)^{2}}{(1-t)^{2}}\frac{M_{1}}{m_{1}}.

Finally, using the bound

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigl[\|\nabla_{x}^{2}\log J_{t}(x\mid X_{1})\|_{\op}\mid X_{t}=x\bigr]\leq\frac{16(d-1)}{(1-t)^{2}}\frac{M_{1}}{m_{1}},

we conclude

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigl[\|\nabla_{x}^{2}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x)\|_{\op}\mid X_{t}=x\bigr]\leq\frac{32(d-1)}{(1-t)^{2}}\frac{M_{1}}{m_{1}}+\frac{16(d-1)^{2}}{(1-t)^{2}}\frac{M_{1}}{m_{1}}<\frac{64(d-1)^{2}}{(1-t)^{2}}\frac{M_{1}}{m_{1}}.

∎

Lemma D.9
.

We have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|\partial_{t}\grad_{x}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x)\|\mid X_{t}=x]\leq\frac{16\pi^{2}(d-1)^{2}}{(1-t)^{2}}\frac{M_{1}}{m_{1}}.

Proof.

We have

\displaystyle\grad_{x}\log p_{t}(x_{1}\mid x)

\displaystyle=\grad_{x}\log J_{t}(x\mid x_{1})-\grad_{x}\log Z_{t}(x)

\displaystyle=\grad_{x}\log J_{t}(x\mid x_{1})-\mathbb{E}\bigl[\grad_{x}\log J_{t}(x\mid X_{1})\mid X_{t}=x\bigr],

so we have

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}\grad_{x}\log p_{t}(x_{1}\mid x)\|

\displaystyle\leq\|\partial_{t}\grad_{x}\log J_{t}(x\mid x_{1})\|+\mathbb{E}\bigl[\|\partial_{t}\grad_{x}\log J_{t}(x\mid X_{1})\|\mid X_{t}=x\bigr]

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}[\|\grad_{x}\log J_{t}(x\mid X_{1})\|\,|\partial_{t}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x)|\mid X_{t}=x].

A direct differentiation at fixed
r
gives

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}\grad_{x}\log J_{t}(x\mid x_{1})\|

\displaystyle=\|\partial_{t}\frac{\partial}{\partial r}\log J_{t}(r)\grad r(x)\|=(d-1)\Bigl\|\partial_{t}\frac{1}{1-t}\cot u-\cot((1-t)u)\Bigr\|

\displaystyle=(d-1)\Bigl\|\frac{1}{(1-t)^{2}}\cot u-\frac{u}{\sin^{2}(1-t)u}\Bigr\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{d-1}{(1-t)^{2}}\frac{2\pi}{\sin^{2}u}.

Taking expectation and using
\mathbb{E}[1/\sin^{a}u]\leq 2M_{1}/m_{1}
for
a=1,2
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|\partial_{t}\grad_{x}\log J_{t}(x\mid X_{1})\|\mid X_{t}=x]\leq\frac{4\pi(d-1)}{(1-t)^{2}}\frac{M_{1}}{m_{1}},

Recall that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\Bigl|\partial_{t}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x)\Bigr|^{2}\mid X_{t}=x\Bigr]

\displaystyle\leq 4\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\Bigl|\partial_{t}\log J_{t}\Bigr|^{2}\mid X_{t}=x\Bigr]

\displaystyle\leq 4\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\frac{(d-1)^{2}\pi^{2}}{(1-t)^{2}}\frac{1}{\sin^{2}u}\mid X_{t}=x\Bigr]\leq\frac{8\pi^{2}(d-1)^{2}}{(1-t)^{2}}\frac{M_{1}}{m_{1}}.

Together with
\|\grad_{x}\log J_{t}(x\mid x_{1})\|\leq\frac{2(d-1)}{(1-t)\sin u}
, by Cauchy-Schwarz,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|\grad_{x}\log J_{t}(x\mid X_{1})\|\,|\partial_{t}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x)|\mid X_{t}=x]

\displaystyle\leq\Bigl(\mathbb{E}[\|\grad_{x}\log J_{t}(x\mid X_{1})\|^{2}\mid X_{t}=x]\Bigr)^{1/2}\Bigl(\mathbb{E}[|\partial_{t}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x)|^{2}\mid X_{t}=x]\Bigr)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{8\pi(d-1)^{2}}{(1-t)^{2}}\frac{M_{1}}{m_{1}}\frac{8\pi^{2}(d-1)^{2}}{(1-t)^{2}}\frac{M_{1}}{m_{1}}}\leq\frac{8\pi^{2}(d-1)^{2}}{(1-t)^{2}}\frac{M_{1}}{m_{1}}.

Thus

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|\partial_{t}\grad_{x}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x)\|\mid X_{t}=x]\leq\frac{16\pi^{2}(d-1)^{2}}{(1-t)^{2}}\frac{M_{1}}{m_{1}}.

∎

D.2
Regularity for Flow Matching Vector Field

In this section, we show that both the spatial derivative (Lemma
D.10
) and time derivative (Lemma
D.11
) of
v(t,x)
are uniformly bounded.

Lemma D.10
.

Assume
d\geq 3
and
t\in(0,1)
. Let
p_{1}
be a smooth density on
S^{d}
such that

\displaystyle 0<m_{1}\leq p_{1}(z)\leq M_{1}<\infty.

For all unit tangent vector
w
, we have

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{w}v(t,x)\|\leq\frac{12\pi M_{1}(d-1)}{m_{1}(1-t)}.

Hence
v(t,x)
is
L
-Lipschitz with
L=\frac{12\pi M_{1}(d-1)}{m_{1}(1-t)}
.

Proof.

Fix
t<1
and
x\in M
, and let
w\in T_{x}M
be a unit tangent vector.
We consider the covariant derivative of
v
in the direction
w
:

\displaystyle\nabla_{w}v(t,x)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1-t}\nabla_{w}\int_{M}\Log_{x}(x_{1})\,p_{t}(x_{1}|x)\,dV_{g}(x_{1})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1-t}\int_{M}\nabla_{w}\big(\Log_{x}(x_{1})\big)\,p_{t}(x_{1}|x)\,dV_{g}(x_{1})

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{1-t}\int_{M}\Log_{x}(x_{1})\,\langle\grad_{x}p_{t}(x_{1}|x),w\rangle\,dV_{g}(x_{1}).

Define

\displaystyle P(t,x)

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{1-t}\int_{S^{d}}\Log_{x}(x_{1})\langle\grad_{x}p_{t}(x_{1}\mid x),w\rangle\,dV_{g}(x_{1}),

\displaystyle G(t,x)

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{1-t}\int_{S^{d}}\|\nabla_{w}\Log_{x}(x_{1})\|p_{t}(x_{1}\mid x)\,dV_{g}(x_{1}).

We show that
\|P(t,x)\|\leq\frac{8\pi M_{1}(d-1)}{m_{1}(1-t)},\forall\|w\|=1
. We first write
\|P(t,x)\|
as a conditional moment of
\|\grad_{x}\log p_{t}\|
.
Using
\grad_{x}p_{t}=p_{t}\grad_{x}\log p_{t}
and
\|\Log_{x}(x_{1})\|=d(x,x_{1})
,

\displaystyle\|P(t,x)\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{1-t}\int_{S^{d}}\|\Log_{x}(x_{1})\|\,\|\grad_{x}p_{t}(x_{1}\mid x)\|\,dV_{g}(x_{1})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1-t}\int_{S^{d}}\|\Log_{x}(x_{1})\|\,p_{t}(x_{1}\mid x)\,\|\grad_{x}\log p_{t}(x_{1}\mid x)\|\,dV_{g}(x_{1})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1-t}\mathbb{E}\bigl[\|\Log_{x}(X_{1})\|\,\|\grad_{x}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x)\|\mid X_{t}=x\bigr].

On the support of
p_{t}(\cdot\mid x)
we have
d(x,X_{1})<(1-t)\pi
, hence

\displaystyle\|\Log_{x}(X_{1})\|\leq(1-t)\pi,

so

\displaystyle\|P(t,x)\|\leq\pi\,\mathbb{E}\bigl[\|\grad_{x}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x)\|\mid X_{t}=x\bigr].

Using Lemma
D.6
,

\displaystyle\|P(t,x)\|\leq\frac{8\pi M_{1}(d-1)}{m_{1}(1-t)}.

Next, we show that
G(t,x)\leq 4\pi\frac{M_{1}}{m_{1}(1-t)}
.

Notice that
\nabla_{w}\Log_{x}(x_{1})
can be related to a Hessian.
Denote
r=d(x,x_{1})\in(0,\pi)
, it is easy to show
(see, for example,
Alimisis et al. 2020
)

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{w}\Log_{x}(x_{1})\|\leq 1+|r\cot(r)|.

Now we bound the
\cot
term.
On the support of
p_{t}(\cdot\mid x)
we have
r<(1-t)\pi
. Let
u=r/(1-t)\in(0,\pi)
, so
r=(1-t)u
.
Using
|\cot r|\leq 1/\sin r
and the concavity bound
\sin((1-t)u)\geq(1-t)\sin u
,

\displaystyle|r\cot r|\leq\frac{r}{\sin r}=\frac{(1-t)u}{\sin((1-t)u)}\leq\frac{(1-t)u}{(1-t)\sin u}=\frac{u}{\sin u}\leq\frac{\pi}{\sin u}.

Therefore for
r<(1-t)\pi
,

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{w}\Log_{x}(x_{1})\|\leq 1+\frac{\pi}{\sin u}<\frac{2\pi}{\sin u}.

Using the same technique as before,

\displaystyle\int_{S^{d}}\|\nabla_{w}\Log_{x}(x_{1})\|\,p_{t}(x_{1}\mid x)\,dV_{g}(x_{1})\leq 4\pi\frac{M_{1}}{m_{1}}.

We conclude that
Multiplying by
(1-t)^{-1}
yields

\displaystyle G(t,x)\leq 4\pi\frac{M_{1}}{m_{1}(1-t)}.

∎

Lemma D.11
.

Fix
d\geq 3
and
t\in(0,1)
. Let
p_{1}
be a smooth density on
S^{d}

satisfying

\displaystyle 0<m_{1}\leq p_{1}(z)\leq M_{1}<\infty.

For

\displaystyle v(t,x)=\frac{1}{1-t}\int_{S^{d}}\Log_{x}(x_{1})\,p_{t}(x_{1}\mid x)\,dV_{g}(x_{1}),

we have for every
x\in S^{d}
,

\displaystyle\Bigl\|\frac{d}{dt}v(t,x)\Bigr\|\leq\frac{3\pi}{1-t}+\frac{4\pi^{2}d}{1-t}\frac{M_{1}}{m_{1}}\leq\frac{8\pi^{2}d}{1-t}\frac{M_{1}}{m_{1}}.

Proof.
Observe that on the support of
p_{t}(\cdot\mid x)
we have
d(x,x_{1})<(1-t)\pi
, hence

\displaystyle\|\Log_{x}(x_{1})\|=d(x,x_{1})\leq(1-t)\pi.

Therefore

\displaystyle\|v(t,x)\|\leq\frac{1}{1-t}\int_{S^{d}}\|\Log_{x}(x_{1})\|\,p_{t}(x_{1}\mid x)\,dV_{g}(x_{1})\leq\frac{1}{1-t}(1-t)\pi=\pi.

We differentiate
v(t,x)
in
t
.
Since
\Log_{x}(x_{1})
does not depend on
t
,

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}v(t,x)

\displaystyle=\frac{d}{dt}\Bigl(\frac{1}{1-t}\Bigr)\int_{S^{d}}\Log_{x}(x_{1})\,p_{t}(x_{1}\mid x)\,dV_{g}(x_{1})+\frac{1}{1-t}\int_{S^{d}}\Log_{x}(x_{1})\,\frac{\partial}{\partial t}p_{t}(x_{1}\mid x)\,dV_{g}(x_{1})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(1-t)^{2}}\int_{S^{d}}\Log_{x}(x_{1})\,p_{t}(x_{1}\mid x)\,dV_{g}(x_{1})+\frac{1}{1-t}\int_{S^{d}}\Log_{x}(x_{1})\,\frac{\partial}{\partial t}p_{t}(x_{1}\mid x)\,dV_{g}(x_{1}).

Using

\displaystyle\int_{S^{d}}\Log_{x}(x_{1})\,p_{t}(x_{1}\mid x)\,dV_{g}(x_{1})=(1-t)\,v(t,x),

we have

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}v(t,x)=\frac{1}{1-t}v(t,x)+\frac{1}{1-t}\int_{S^{d}}\Log_{x}(x_{1})\,\frac{\partial}{\partial t}p_{t}(x_{1}\mid x)\,dV_{g}(x_{1}).

Since
\int_{S^{d}}p_{t}(x_{1}\mid x)\,dV_{g}(x_{1})=1
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{S^{d}}\frac{\partial}{\partial t}p_{t}(x_{1}\mid x)\,dV_{g}(x_{1})=0.

Also, wherever
p_{t}(x_{1}\mid x)>0
,

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial t}p_{t}(x_{1}\mid x)=p_{t}(x_{1}\mid x)\,\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\log p_{t}(x_{1}\mid x).

Hence

\displaystyle\Bigl\|\int_{S^{d}}\Log_{x}(x_{1})\,\frac{\partial}{\partial t}p_{t}(x_{1}\mid x)\,dV_{g}(x_{1})\Bigr\|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{S^{d}}\|\Log_{x}(x_{1})\|\,p_{t}(x_{1}\mid x)\,\Bigl|\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\log p_{t}(x_{1}\mid x)\Bigr|\,dV_{g}(x_{1})

\displaystyle\leq(1-t)\pi\,\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\Bigl|\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x)\Bigr|\mid X_{t}=x\Bigr].

Therefore

\displaystyle\Bigl\|\frac{d}{dt}v(t,x)\Bigr\|\leq\frac{\pi}{1-t}+\pi\,\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\Bigl|\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x)\Bigr|\mid X_{t}=x\Bigr].

Using Lemma
D.7
,

\displaystyle\Bigl\|\frac{d}{dt}v(t,x)\Bigr\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\pi}{1-t}+\pi\,\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\Bigl|\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x)\Bigr|\mid X_{t}=x\Bigr]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\pi}{1-t}+2\pi(\frac{1}{1-t}+\frac{d-1}{1-t}\frac{2\pi M_{1}}{m_{1}})\leq\frac{1}{1-t}(3\pi+4\pi^{2}\frac{dM_{1}}{m_{1}}).

∎

D.3
Regularity for Divergence

In this section, we show that both the gradient (Lemma
D.12
) and the time derivative (Lemma
D.13
) of

\Div v(t,x)
are uniformly bounded.

Lemma D.12
.

Assume
d\geq 3
and
t\in(0,1)
. Let
p_{1}
be a smooth density on
S^{d}
such that

\displaystyle 0<m_{1}\leq p_{1}(z)\leq M_{1}<\infty.

Then for every
x\in S^{d}
we have

\displaystyle\|\grad_{x}\Div v(t,x)\|\leq\frac{128\pi(d-1)^{2}}{(1-t)^{3}}\frac{M_{1}}{m_{1}}.

Proof.
Write

\displaystyle v(t,x)=\frac{1}{1-t}\int_{S^{d}}\Log_{x}(x_{1})\,p_{t}(x_{1}\mid x)\,dV_{g}(x_{1}).

Using
\Div(fW)=\langle\grad f,W\rangle+f\Div W
with
f=p_{t}(\cdot\mid x)
and
W=\Log_{x}(\cdot)
,

\displaystyle\Div v(t,x)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1-t}\int_{S^{d}}\Div_{x}\Log_{x}(x_{1})\,p_{t}(x_{1}\mid x)\,dV_{g}(x_{1})

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{1-t}\int_{S^{d}}\langle\grad_{x}p_{t}(x_{1}\mid x),\Log_{x}(x_{1})\rangle\,dV_{g}(x_{1})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1-t}\mathbb{E}[\Div_{x}\Log_{x}(X_{1})\mid X_{t}=x]

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{1-t}\mathbb{E}[\langle\grad_{x}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x),\Log_{x}(X_{1})\rangle\mid X_{t}=x].

Differentiate this identity in
x
along a unit vector
\xi\in T_{x}S^{d}
and take norms.
We take gradient, and obtain (for a smooth integrand
F(x,x_{1})
),

\displaystyle\grad_{x}\mathbb{E}[F(x,X_{1})\mid X_{t}=x]=\mathbb{E}[\grad_{x}F(x,X_{1})\mid X_{t}=x]+\mathbb{E}[F(x,X_{1})\grad_{x}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x)\mid X_{t}=x].

Applying this formula and using triangle inequality yields

\displaystyle\|\grad_{x}\Div v(t,x)\|\leq\frac{1}{1-t}\Bigl(T_{1}+T_{2}+T_{3}+T_{4}\Bigr),

where

\displaystyle T_{1}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[\|\grad_{x}\Div_{x}\Log_{x}(X_{1})\|\mid X_{t}=x],

\displaystyle T_{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[|\Div_{x}\Log_{x}(X_{1})|\,\|\grad_{x}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x)\|\mid X_{t}=x],

\displaystyle T_{3}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[\|\grad_{x}\langle\grad_{x}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x),\Log_{x}(X_{1})\rangle\|\mid X_{t}=x],

\displaystyle T_{4}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[|\langle\grad_{x}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x),\Log_{x}(X_{1})\rangle|\,\|\grad_{x}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x)\|\mid X_{t}=x].

Now we compute
\Div_{x}\Log_{x}(x_{1})
and its
x
-gradient.
Let
r=d(x,x_{1})\in(0,\pi)
. Notice that

\displaystyle\Log_{x}(x_{1})=-\grad_{x}\frac{1}{2}r^{2},

hence

\displaystyle\Div_{x}\Log_{x}(x_{1})=-\Div_{x}(r\grad r)=-r\Delta r-\langle\grad r,\grad r\rangle=-(d-1)r\cot r-1,

where we used
\Delta r=(d-1)\cot r
(see for example
[
Lee 2018
, Theorem 11.11]
)
and
\|\grad r\|^{2}=1
.

Differentiate in
x
using that
r
is a radial function and
\|\grad r\|=1
:

\displaystyle\grad_{x}\Div_{x}\Log_{x}(x_{1})=-(d-1)\bigl(\cot r-r\csc^{2}r\bigr)\grad_{x}r,

which implies

\displaystyle\|\grad_{x}\Div_{x}\Log_{x}(x_{1})\|=(d-1)\bigl|\cot r-r\csc^{2}r\bigr|=(d-1)\bigl|\cot r-r\frac{1}{\sin^{2}r}\bigr|.

We bound
T_{1}=\mathbb{E}[\|\grad_{x}\Div_{x}\Log_{x}(X_{1})\|\mid X_{t}=x]
. Using
r=(1-t)u
and
\sin((1-t)u)\geq(1-t)\sin u
(concavity of
\sin
on
[0,\pi]
),

\displaystyle|\cot r|\leq\frac{1}{\sin r}\leq\frac{1}{(1-t)\sin u},\qquad r\frac{1}{\sin^{2}r}\leq\frac{(1-t)\pi}{\sin^{2}r}\leq\frac{\pi}{(1-t)\sin^{2}u}.

Hence

\displaystyle\|\grad_{x}\Div_{x}\Log_{x}(x_{1})\|\leq(d-1)\Bigl(\frac{1}{(1-t)\sin u}+\frac{\pi}{(1-t)\sin^{2}u}\Bigr).

Therefore

\displaystyle T_{1}\leq(d-1)\frac{1}{1-t}\frac{M_{1}}{m_{1}}(2\pi+2).

We bound
T_{2}=\mathbb{E}[|\Div_{x}\Log_{x}(X_{1})|\,\|\grad_{x}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x)\|\mid X_{t}=x]
. Recall
|\Div_{x}\Log_{x}(x_{1})|\leq 1+(d-1)r|\cot r|
, so we have

\displaystyle|\Div_{x}\Log_{x}(x_{1})|\leq 1+(d-1)\pi\frac{1}{\sin r}\leq 1+(d-1)\pi\frac{1}{(1-t)\sin u}.

Thus by Cauchy-Schwarz,

\displaystyle T_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\Bigl(\mathbb{E}[|\Div_{x}\Log_{x}(X_{1})|^{2}\mid X_{t}=x]\Bigr)^{1/2}\Bigl(\mathbb{E}[\|\grad_{x}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x)\|^{2}\mid X_{t}=x]\Bigr)^{1/2}.

Using
(a+b)^{2}\leq 2a^{2}+2b^{2}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[|\Div_{x}\Log_{x}(X_{1})|^{2}\mid X_{t}=x]

\displaystyle\leq 2+2(d-1)^{2}\pi^{2}\frac{1}{(1-t)^{2}}\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\frac{1}{\sin^{2}u}\mid X_{t}=x\Bigr]

\displaystyle\leq 2+2(d-1)^{2}\pi^{2}\frac{1}{(1-t)^{2}}\frac{2M_{1}}{m_{1}}.

Also recall

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|\grad_{x}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x)\|^{2}\mid X_{t}=x]\leq\frac{32(d-1)^{2}}{(1-t)^{2}}\frac{M_{1}}{m_{1}}.

Hence we obtain

\displaystyle T_{2}

\displaystyle\leq(2+2(d-1)^{2}\pi^{2}\frac{1}{(1-t)^{2}}\frac{2M_{1}}{m_{1}})^{\frac{1}{2}}(\frac{32(d-1)^{2}}{(1-t)^{2}}\frac{M_{1}}{m_{1}})^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{16\pi M_{1}}{m_{1}}\frac{(d-1)^{2}}{(1-t)^{2}}.

We bound
T_{3}=\mathbb{E}[\|\grad_{x}\langle\grad_{x}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x),\Log_{x}(X_{1})\rangle\|\mid X_{t}=x]
.
We use the product rule: denote
g(x)=\langle g_{1}(x),g_{2}(x)\rangle
.
For
g_{1},g_{2}
, we have (viewing as directional derivative along
u
and use compatiblity)

\displaystyle\langle\grad_{x}\langle g_{1}(x),g_{2}(x)\rangle,u\rangle=\nabla_{u}\langle g_{1}(x),g_{2}(x)\rangle=\langle\nabla_{u}g_{1}(x),g_{2}(x)\rangle+\langle g_{1}(x),\nabla_{u}g_{2}(x)\rangle.

Hence we can write

\displaystyle\|\grad_{x}\langle\grad_{x}\log p_{t}(x_{1}\mid x),\Log_{x}(x_{1})\rangle\|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|\nabla\grad_{x}\log p_{t}(x_{1}\mid x)\|_{\op}\|\Log_{x}(x_{1})\|+\|\nabla\Log_{x}(x_{1})\|_{\op}\|\grad_{x}\log p_{t}(x_{1}\mid x)\|,

so

\displaystyle T_{3}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}[\|\nabla\Log_{x}(X_{1})\|\,\|\grad_{x}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x)\|\mid X_{t}=x]

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathbb{E}[\|\nabla^{2}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x)\|\,\|\Log_{x}(X_{1})\|\mid X_{t}=x].

On the support of
p_{t}(\cdot\mid x)
,
\|\Log_{x}(X_{1})\|\leq(1-t)\pi
, hence

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|\nabla^{2}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x)\|\,\|\Log_{x}(X_{1})\|\mid X_{t}=x]\leq(1-t)\pi\,\mathbb{E}[\|\nabla^{2}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x)\|\mid X_{t}=x].

By Lemma
D.8
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|\nabla^{2}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x)\|\,\|\Log_{x}(X_{1})\|\mid X_{t}=x]\leq\frac{64\pi(d-1)^{2}}{1-t}\frac{M_{1}}{m_{1}}.

Also, on
S^{d}
,

\displaystyle\|\nabla\Log_{x}(x_{1})\|\leq 1+r|\cot r|<\pi\frac{2}{(1-t)\sin u},

so by Cauchy-Schwarz inequality,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|\nabla\Log_{x}(X_{1})\|\,\|\grad_{x}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x)\|\mid X_{t}=x]

\displaystyle\leq\Bigl(\frac{32(d-1)^{2}}{(1-t)^{2}}\frac{M_{1}}{m_{1}}\frac{8\pi^{2}}{(1-t)^{2}}\frac{M_{1}}{m_{1}}\Bigr)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{16\pi M_{1}}{m_{1}}\frac{(d-1)}{(1-t)^{2}}.

Hence we get

\displaystyle T_{3}\leq\frac{16\pi M_{1}}{m_{1}}\frac{(d-1)}{(1-t)^{2}}+\frac{64\pi(d-1)^{2}}{1-t}\frac{M_{1}}{m_{1}}\leq\frac{64\pi M_{1}}{m_{1}}\frac{(d-1)^{2}}{(1-t)^{2}}.

We bound
T_{4}=\mathbb{E}[|\langle\grad_{x}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x),\Log_{x}(X_{1})\rangle|\,\|\grad_{x}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x)\|\mid X_{t}=x]
. We have

\displaystyle T_{4}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}[\|\Log_{x}(X_{1})\|\,\|\grad_{x}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x)\|^{2}\mid X_{t}=x]

\displaystyle\leq(1-t)\pi\,\mathbb{E}[\|\grad_{x}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x)\|^{2}\mid X_{t}=x]

\displaystyle\leq(1-t)\pi\cdot\frac{32(d-1)^{2}}{(1-t)^{2}}\frac{M_{1}}{m_{1}}=\frac{32\pi(d-1)^{2}}{1-t}\frac{M_{1}}{m_{1}}.

Together,

\displaystyle\|\grad_{x}\Div v(t,x)\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1-t}\Bigl(T_{1}+T_{2}+T_{3}+T_{4}\Bigr)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(1-t)}\frac{M_{1}}{m_{1}}\Bigl((d-1)\frac{1}{1-t}(2\pi+2)+16\pi\frac{(d-1)^{2}}{(1-t)^{2}}+64\pi\frac{(d-1)^{2}}{(1-t)^{2}}+\frac{32\pi(d-1)^{2}}{1-t}\Bigr)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{128\pi(d-1)^{2}}{(1-t)^{3}}\frac{M_{1}}{m_{1}}.

∎

Lemma D.13
.

Assume
d\geq 3
and
t\in(0,1)
. Let
p_{1}
be a smooth density on
S^{d}
such that

\displaystyle 0<m_{1}\leq p_{1}(z)\leq M_{1}<\infty.

Then for every
x\in S^{d}
,

\displaystyle\Bigl|\frac{d}{dt}\Div v(t,x)\Bigr|\leq\frac{128\pi^{2}(d-1)^{2}}{(1-t)^{3}}\frac{M_{1}}{m_{1}}.

Proof.
We start by computing the time derivative of divergence. Recall

\displaystyle\Div v(t,x)=\frac{1}{1-t}\mathbb{E}[\Div_{x}\Log_{x}(X_{1})\mid X_{t}=x]+\frac{1}{1-t}\mathbb{E}[\langle\grad_{x}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x),\Log_{x}(X_{1})\rangle\mid X_{t}=x].

Let
F(t,x_{1})
be smooth in
t
and integrable under
p_{t}(\cdot\mid x)
. Then

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\mathbb{E}[F(t,X_{1})\mid X_{t}=x]=\mathbb{E}[\partial_{t}F(t,X_{1})\mid X_{t}=x]+\mathbb{E}[F(t,X_{1})\,\partial_{t}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x)\mid X_{t}=x].

Applying this with
F(t,x_{1})=\Div_{x}\Log_{x}(x_{1})
and
F(t,x_{1})=\langle\grad_{x}\log p_{t}(x_{1}\mid x),\Log_{x}(x_{1})\rangle
respectively,

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\mathbb{E}[\Div_{x}\Log_{x}(X_{1})\mid X_{t}=x]=\mathbb{E}[\Div_{x}\Log_{x}(X_{1})\,\partial_{t}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x)\mid X_{t}=x],

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\mathbb{E}[\langle\grad_{x}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x),\Log_{x}(X_{1})\rangle\mid X_{t}=x]=\mathbb{E}[\partial_{t}\langle\grad_{x}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x),\Log_{x}(X_{1})\rangle\mid X_{t}=x]

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathbb{E}[\langle\grad_{x}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x),\Log_{x}(X_{1})\rangle\,\partial_{t}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x)\mid X_{t}=x].

Therefore, for fixed
x
,

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\Div v(t,x)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(1-t)^{2}}\mathbb{E}\bigl[\Div_{x}\Log_{x}(X_{1})+\langle\grad_{x}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x),\Log_{x}(X_{1})\rangle\mid X_{t}=x\bigr]

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{1-t}\mathbb{E}[\Div_{x}\Log_{x}(X_{1})\,\partial_{t}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x)\mid X_{t}=x]

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{1-t}\mathbb{E}[\partial_{t}\langle\grad_{x}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x),\Log_{x}(X_{1})\rangle\mid X_{t}=x]

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{1-t}\mathbb{E}[\langle\grad_{x}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x),\Log_{x}(X_{1})\rangle\,\partial_{t}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x)\mid X_{t}=x].

Define

\displaystyle T_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(1-t)^{2}}\mathbb{E}\bigl[|\Div_{x}\Log_{x}(X_{1})+\langle\grad_{x}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x),\Log_{x}(X_{1})\rangle|\mid X_{t}=x\bigr],

\displaystyle T_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1-t}\mathbb{E}[|\Div_{x}\Log_{x}(X_{1})\,\partial_{t}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x)|\mid X_{t}=x],

\displaystyle T_{3}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1-t}\mathbb{E}[|\partial_{t}\langle\grad_{x}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x),\Log_{x}(X_{1})\rangle|\mid X_{t}=x],

\displaystyle T_{4}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1-t}\mathbb{E}[|\langle\grad_{x}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x),\Log_{x}(X_{1})\rangle\,\partial_{t}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x)|\mid X_{t}=x].

For
T_{1}
, recall

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(1-t)^{2}}\mathbb{E}\bigl[\Div_{x}\Log_{x}(X_{1})\mid X_{t}=x\bigr]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{(1-t)^{2}}\mathbb{E}\bigl[1+(d-1)\pi\frac{1}{(1-t)\sin u}\mid X_{t}=x\bigr]

\displaystyle\leq\pi\frac{(d-1)}{(1-t)^{3}}\frac{2M_{1}}{m_{1}},

and

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(1-t)^{2}}\mathbb{E}\bigl[\langle\grad_{x}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x),\Log_{x}(X_{1})\rangle\mid X_{t}=x\bigr]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(1-t)^{2}}(1-t)\pi\mathbb{E}\bigl[\|\grad_{x}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x)\|\mid X_{t}=x\bigr]\leq\frac{8\pi(d-1)}{(1-t)^{2}}\frac{M_{1}}{m_{1}}.

Hence

\displaystyle T_{1}\leq\pi\frac{(d-1)}{(1-t)^{3}}\frac{2M_{1}}{m_{1}}+\frac{8\pi(d-1)}{(1-t)^{2}}\frac{M_{1}}{m_{1}}\leq\frac{16\pi(d-1)}{(1-t)^{3}}\frac{M_{1}}{m_{1}}.

For
T_{2}
, recall

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[|\Div_{x}\Log_{x}(X_{1})|^{2}\mid X_{t}=x]\leq 2+2(d-1)^{2}\pi^{2}\frac{1}{(1-t)^{2}}\frac{2M_{1}}{m_{1}}\leq\frac{(d-1)^{2}}{(1-t)^{2}}\frac{4\pi^{2}M_{1}}{m_{1}}.

Hence we have

\displaystyle T_{2}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1-t}\mathbb{E}[|\Div_{x}\Log_{x}(X_{1})\,\partial_{t}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x)|\mid X_{t}=x]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1-t}\Bigl(\mathbb{E}[|\Div_{x}\Log_{x}(X_{1})|^{2}\mid X_{t}=x]\mathbb{E}[|\partial_{t}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x)|^{2}\mid X_{t}=x]\Bigr)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1-t}\Bigl(\frac{(d-1)^{2}}{(1-t)^{2}}\frac{4\pi^{2}M_{1}}{m_{1}}\frac{32\pi^{2}(d-1)^{2}}{(1-t)^{2}}\,\frac{M_{1}}{m_{1}}\Bigr)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\frac{16\pi^{2}(d-1)^{2}}{(1-t)^{3}}\frac{M_{1}}{m_{1}},

where by Lemma
D.7
,
