\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\Bigl|\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x)\Bigr|^{2}\mid X_{t}=x\Bigr]\leq\frac{8}{(1-t)^{2}}+\frac{16\pi^{2}(d-1)^{2}}{(1-t)^{2}}\,\frac{M_{1}}{m_{1}}\leq\frac{32\pi^{2}(d-1)^{2}}{(1-t)^{2}}\,\frac{M_{1}}{m_{1}}.

For
T_{3}
, since
\Log_{x}(x_{1})
does not depend on
t
,

\displaystyle|\partial_{t}\langle\grad_{x}\log p_{t}(x_{1}\mid x),\Log_{x}(x_{1})\rangle|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}\grad_{x}\log p_{t}(x_{1}\mid x)\|\,\|\Log_{x}(x_{1})\|\leq(1-t)\pi\,\|\partial_{t}\grad_{x}\log p_{t}(x_{1}\mid x)\|.

Using Lemma
D.9
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|\partial_{t}\grad_{x}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x)\|\mid X_{t}=x]\leq\frac{16\pi^{2}(d-1)^{2}}{(1-t)^{2}}\frac{M_{1}}{m_{1}}.

Hence

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[|\partial_{t}\langle\grad_{x}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x),\Log_{x}(X_{1})\rangle|\mid X_{t}=x]

\displaystyle\leq(1-t)\pi\cdot\frac{16\pi^{2}(d-1)^{2}}{(1-t)^{2}}\frac{M_{1}}{m_{1}}

\displaystyle=\frac{16\pi^{3}(d-1)^{2}}{(1-t)}\frac{M_{1}}{m_{1}}.

Last, we bound
T_{4}
. Similarly, by Cauchy-Schwarz and the bound we did for
T_{3}
,

\displaystyle\Bigl|\mathbb{E}[\langle\grad_{x}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x),\Log_{x}(X_{1})\rangle\,\partial_{t}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x)\mid X_{t}=x]\Bigr|

\displaystyle\leq\Bigl(\mathbb{E}[|\langle\grad_{x}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x),\Log_{x}(X_{1})\rangle|^{2}\mid X_{t}=x]\Bigr)^{1/2}\Bigl(\mathbb{E}[|\partial_{t}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x)|^{2}\mid X_{t}=x]\Bigr)^{1/2}.

We bound the first factor by

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[|\langle\grad_{x}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x),\Log_{x}(X_{1})\rangle|^{2}\mid X_{t}=x]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(1-t)^{2}\pi^{2}\mathbb{E}[\|\grad_{x}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x)\|^{2}\mid X_{t}=x]\leq 32\pi^{2}(d-1)^{2}\frac{M_{1}}{m_{1}}.

Therefore

\displaystyle T_{4}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1-t}\Bigl|\mathbb{E}[\langle\grad_{x}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x),\Log_{x}(X_{1})\rangle\,\partial_{t}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x)\mid X_{t}=x]\Bigr|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 32\pi^{2}\frac{(d-1)^{2}}{(1-t)^{2}}\frac{M_{1}}{m_{1}}.

Together,

\displaystyle|\frac{d}{dt}\Div v(t,x)|\leq

\displaystyle T_{1}+T_{2}+T_{3}+T_{4}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{16\pi(d-1)}{(1-t)^{3}}\frac{M_{1}}{m_{1}}+\frac{16\pi^{2}(d-1)^{2}}{(1-t)^{3}}\frac{M_{1}}{m_{1}}+\frac{16\pi^{3}(d-1)^{2}}{(1-t)^{2}}\frac{M_{1}}{m_{1}}+32\pi^{2}\frac{(d-1)^{2}}{(1-t)^{2}}\frac{M_{1}}{m_{1}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{128\pi^{2}(d-1)^{2}}{(1-t)^{3}}\frac{M_{1}}{m_{1}}.

∎

D.4
Regularity of
v(t,x)
and
\log p_{t}

Finally, we bound
\|v(t,x)\|
and
\mathbb{E}[\|\grad\log p_{t}(X_{t})\|^{2}]
in Lemma
D.14
and
D.15
.

Lemma D.14
.

Assume
d\geq 2
and
t\in(0,1)
. Let
p_{1}
be a smooth density on
S^{d}
such that

\displaystyle 0<m_{1}\leq p_{1}(z)\leq M_{1}<\infty.

For every
x\in S^{d}
,

\displaystyle\|v(t,x)\|\leq\pi.

Proof.

Fix
x\in S^{d}
. By Jensen and the definition of
v
,

\displaystyle\|v(t,x)\|

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1-t}\Bigl\|\int_{S^{d}}\Log_{x}(x_{1})\,p_{t}(x_{1}\mid x)\,dV_{g}(x_{1})\Bigr\|\leq\frac{1}{1-t}\int_{S^{d}}\|\Log_{x}(x_{1})\|\,p_{t}(x_{1}\mid x)\,dV_{g}(x_{1}).

On the support of
p_{t}(\cdot\mid x)
we have
d(x,x_{1})<(1-t)\pi
, hence

\displaystyle\|\Log_{x}(x_{1})\|=d(x,x_{1})\leq(1-t)\pi.

Therefore

\displaystyle\|v(t,x)\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{1-t}\int_{S^{d}}(1-t)\pi\,p_{t}(x_{1}\mid x)\,dV_{g}(x_{1})=\pi\int_{S^{d}}p_{t}(x_{1}\mid x)\,dV_{g}(x_{1})=\pi,

since
p_{t}(\cdot\mid x)
is a probability density.
∎

Lemma D.15
.

Assume
d\geq 3
and
t\in(0,1)
. Let
p_{0}\equiv\Vol(S^{d})^{-1}
be the uniform prior on
S^{d}
,
and let
p_{1}
be a smooth density on
S^{d}
such that

\displaystyle 0<m_{1}\leq p_{1}(z)\leq M_{1}<\infty.

Let
X_{0}\sim p_{0}
and
X_{1}\sim p_{1}
be independent, and define the geodesic interpolation

\displaystyle X_{t}=\Exp_{X_{0}}(t\Log_{X_{0}}(X_{1})).

Let
p_{t}
denote the marginal density of
X_{t}
with respect to
dV_{g}
.
Then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigl[\|\grad\log p_{t}(X_{t})\|^{2}\bigr]\leq\frac{8(d-1)^{2}}{(1-t)^{2}}\frac{M_{1}}{m_{1}}.

Proof.
Using Lemma
D.2
, notice that

\log p_{t}(x)=\log Z_{t}(x)-\log\Vol(S^{d})
, we have

\displaystyle\grad\log p_{t}(x)=\grad\log Z_{t}(x).

Take gradient, we obtain

\displaystyle\grad Z_{t}(x)=\int_{S^{d}}p_{1}(x_{1})\,\grad_{x}J_{t}(x\mid x_{1})\,\,dV_{g}(x_{1}).

Using
\grad_{x}J_{t}=J_{t}\grad_{x}\log J_{t}
and the definition of the conditional density

\displaystyle p_{t}(x_{1}\mid x)=\frac{p_{1}(x_{1})J_{t}(x\mid x_{1})}{Z_{t}(x)},

we obtain

\displaystyle\grad\log Z_{t}(x)

\displaystyle=\frac{\grad Z_{t}(x)}{Z_{t}(x)}=\int_{S^{d}}\grad_{x}\log J_{t}(x\mid x_{1})\,p_{t}(x_{1}\mid x)\,dV_{g}(x_{1})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigl[\grad_{x}\log J_{t}(x\mid X_{1})\mid X_{t}=x\bigr].

Now we have

\displaystyle\grad\log p_{t}(X_{t})=\mathbb{E}[\grad_{x}\log J_{t}(X_{t}\mid X_{1})\mid X_{t}],

which implies

\displaystyle\|\grad\log p_{t}(X_{t})\|^{2}\leq\mathbb{E}[\|\grad_{x}\log J_{t}(X_{t}\mid X_{1})\|^{2}\mid X_{t}].

Taking expectation again gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|\grad\log p_{t}(X_{t})\|^{2}]\leq\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\mathbb{E}[\|\grad_{x}\log J_{t}(X_{t}\mid X_{1})\|^{2}\mid X_{t}]\Bigr]\leq\frac{8(d-1)^{2}}{(1-t)^{2}}\frac{M_{1}}{m_{1}}.

where the last inequality follows from Lemma
D.6
.

∎

D.5
Finiteness of Score Regularity: Proof of Proposition
5.3

Proof.
[Proof of Proposition
5.3
]
Notice that

\mathbb{E}\bigl[\|\grad\log p_{t}(X_{t})\|^{2}\bigr]=\int_{M}\|\grad\log p_{t}(x)\|^{2}\,p_{t}(x)\,dV_{g}(x)=:I(p_{t}),

i.e., it is exactly the (Riemannian) Fisher information of
p_{t}
.
Consider the continuity equation
\partial_{t}p_{t}+\Div(p_{t}\,v(t,\cdot))=0
. Let
s_{t}:=\grad\log p_{t}
denote the score.

We first compute time derivative of
I
.
Differentiate and use the ordinary product rule:

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}I(p_{t})

\displaystyle=\int_{M}\partial_{t}\big(\langle s_{t},s_{t}\rangle\big)\,p_{t}\,dV_{g}+\int_{M}\langle s_{t},s_{t}\rangle\,\partial_{t}p_{t}\,dV_{g}

\displaystyle=2\int_{M}\big\langle\partial_{t}s_{t},\,s_{t}\big\rangle\,p_{t}\,dV_{g}+\int_{M}\|s_{t}\|^{2}\,\partial_{t}p_{t}\,dV_{g}.

Observe that

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\log p_{t}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{p_{t}}\Div(p_{t}v)=-\Div v-\frac{1}{p_{t}}\langle\grad p_{t},v\rangle

\displaystyle=-\Div v-\langle\grad\log p_{t},v\rangle=-\Div v-\langle s_{t},v\rangle.

Taking the gradient yields

\displaystyle\partial_{t}s_{t}=\grad(\partial_{t}\log p_{t})=-\grad(\Div v)\;-\;\grad\big(\langle s_{t},v\rangle\big).

We compute the terms in the time derivative of
I(p_{t})
separately. For the first term,

\displaystyle 2\int_{M}\big\langle\partial_{t}s_{t},\,s_{t}\big\rangle\,p_{t}\,dV_{g}

\displaystyle=2\int_{M}\big\langle-\grad(\Div v)\;-\;\grad\big(\langle s_{t},v\rangle\big),\,s_{t}\big\rangle\,p_{t}\,dV_{g}

\displaystyle=-2\int_{M}\big\langle\grad(\Div v),\,s_{t}\big\rangle\,p_{t}\,dV_{g}-2\int_{M}\big\langle\;\grad\big(\langle s_{t},v\rangle\big),\,s_{t}\big\rangle\,p_{t}\,dV_{g}

\displaystyle=-2\int_{M}\big\langle\grad(\Div v),\,s_{t}\big\rangle\,p_{t}\,dV_{g}-2\int_{M}\langle\nabla_{s_{t}}s_{t},v\rangle p_{t}+\langle\nabla_{s_{t}}v,s_{t}\rangle p_{t}\,dV_{g}.

For the second term,

\displaystyle\int_{M}\|s_{t}\|^{2}\,\partial_{t}p_{t}\,dV_{g}

\displaystyle=-\int_{M}\|s_{t}\|^{2}\,\Div(p_{t}v)\,dV_{g}=\int_{M}\big\langle\grad\|s_{t}\|^{2},\,v\big\rangle\,p_{t}\,dV_{g}

\displaystyle=2\int_{M}\big\langle\nabla_{v}s_{t},s_{t}\big\rangle\,p_{t}\,dV_{g}.

Since
\Hess
is symmetric, we have

\displaystyle\big\langle\nabla_{v}s_{t},s_{t}\big\rangle=\langle\nabla_{v}\grad\log p_{t},s_{t}\rangle=\bigl(\Hess\log p_{t}\bigr)(v,s_{t})=\langle\nabla_{s_{t}}\grad\log p_{t},v\rangle=\big\langle\nabla_{s_{t}}s_{t},v\big\rangle.

Together,

\frac{d}{dt}I(p_{t})=-2\int_{M}\langle s_{t},\nabla_{s_{t}}v\rangle\,p_{t}\,dV_{g}-2\int_{M}\langle\grad(\Div v),s_{t}\rangle\,p_{t}\,dV_{g}.

Now we derive an ODE that helps to bound
I
.
Using Cauchy–Schwarz and the pointwise bounds

\|\nabla v(t,x)\|_{\op}\leq L_{t}^{v,x}
and
\|\grad\Div v(t,x)\|\leq L_{t}^{\Div,x}

(from Assumption
2
), we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}I(p_{t})

\displaystyle\leq 2L_{t}^{v,x}\,I(p_{t})\;+\;2L_{t}^{\Div,x}\,\int_{M}\|s_{t}(x)\|\,p_{t}(x)\,dV_{g}(x)

\displaystyle\leq 2L_{t}^{v,x}\,I(p_{t})\;+\;2L_{t}^{\Div,x}\,\sqrt{I(p_{t})}.

(16)

Let
y(t):=\sqrt{I(p_{t})}
. Since
I^{\prime}=2yy^{\prime}
, (
16
) implies

y^{\prime}(t)\leq L_{t}^{v,x}\,y(t)+L_{t}^{\Div,x}.

It remains to solve the ODE and obtain a finite upper bound.
Define

A(t):=\int_{0}^{t}L_{s}^{v,x}\,ds,\qquad\mu(t):=e^{-A(t)}.

Then
\mu
is absolutely continuous and

\mu^{\prime}(t)=-L_{t}^{v,x}\,e^{-A(t)}=-L_{t}^{v,x}\,\mu(t)\quad\text{for a.e. }t.

Multiply by
\mu(t)
:

\mu(t)\,y^{\prime}(t)\leq\mu(t)\,L_{t}^{v,x}\,y(t)+\mu(t)\,L_{t}^{\Div,x}.

Using the product rule and the identity for
\mu^{\prime}(t)
,

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\big(\mu(t)\,y(t)\big)

\displaystyle=\mu^{\prime}(t)\,y(t)+\mu(t)\,y^{\prime}(t)

\displaystyle=-L_{t}^{v,x}\,\mu(t)\,y(t)+\mu(t)\,y^{\prime}(t)

\displaystyle\leq-L_{t}^{v,x}\,\mu(t)\,y(t)+\mu(t)\,L_{t}^{v,x}\,y(t)+\mu(t)\,L_{t}^{\Div,x}

\displaystyle=\mu(t)\,L_{t}^{\Div,x}.

Hence, for a.e.
t
,

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\big(\mu(t)\,y(t)\big)\leq\mu(t)\,L_{t}^{\Div,x}.

Integrating over
[0,t]
yields

\mu(t)\,y(t)-\mu(0)\,y(0)\leq\int_{0}^{t}\mu(s)\,L_{s}^{\Div,x}\,ds.

Since
\mu(0)=e^{-A(0)}=1
, we obtain

\mu(t)\,y(t)\leq y(0)+\int_{0}^{t}e^{-A(s)}\,L_{s}^{\Div,x}\,ds.

Multiply both sides by
e^{A(t)}
:

\displaystyle y(t)

\displaystyle\leq e^{A(t)}\left(y(0)+\int_{0}^{t}e^{-A(s)}\,L_{s}^{\Div,x}\,ds\right)

\displaystyle=\exp\!\Bigl(\int_{0}^{t}L_{s}^{v,x}\,ds\Bigr)\left(y(0)+\int_{0}^{t}L_{s}^{\Div,x}\,\exp\!\Bigl(-\int_{0}^{s}L_{r}^{v,x}\,dr\Bigr)\,ds\right).

Therefore, for all
t<1
,

\displaystyle\sqrt{I(p_{t})}\leq\exp\!\Bigl(\int_{0}^{t}L_{s}^{v,x}\,ds\Bigr)\left(\sqrt{I(p_{0})}+\int_{0}^{t}L_{s}^{\Div,x}\,\exp\!\Bigl(-\int_{0}^{s}L_{r}^{v,x}\,dr\Bigr)\,ds\right),

and hence

\mathbb{E}\bigl[\|\grad\log p_{t}(X_{t})\|^{2}\bigr]=I(p_{t})\leq L_{t}^{\mathrm{score}}

for some finite number
L_{t}^{\mathrm{score}}
.
∎

Appendix E
SPD Manifold Regularity Results

In this section, we work on the SPD manifold with affine invariant metric, denoted as
\SPD(n)
.

We briefly discuss the similarity and difference between the regularity analysis in this section and that of the hypersphere.

•

On a hypersphere, there exist cut points, resulting indicator function in the conditional density. We provide the formula for the conditional density function, on a general Hadamard manifold, see Lemma
E.1
. We see that with non-positive curvature, there would be no cut points. Consequently, there would be no indicator function in the expression of the conditional density, guaranteeing better smoothness.

•

The hypersphere is a compact manifold, but
\SPD(n)
is non-compact. On a compact manifold, the vector field
v(t,x)
itself is uniformly bounded. But on a non-compact manifold,
\|v(t,x)\|
could possibly blow up. Therefore, on
\SPD(n)
, it’s unlikely that regularity will hold pointwise, but we can still expect its norm to be bounded, in expectation.

•

Moreover, from a high-level idea, the procedure for providing upper bounds on derivatives of
v(t,x)
remains the same. For example, in Lemma
D.12
, we expanded the gradient of
\Div v(t,x)
as terms involving
\mathbb{E}[\|\grad_{x}\Div_{x}\Log_{x}(X_{1})\|\mid X_{t}=x]
,
\mathbb{E}[\|\grad_{x}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x)\|^{2}\mid X_{t}=x]
, just to name a few. To establish regularity on
\SPD(n)
, we still have roughly the same expansion, involving the same collection of terms. The difference is that, instead of obtain uniform upper bounds (as in Lemma
D.6
,
D.7
,
D.8
, and
D.9
), our bounds for
\SPD(n)
will depend on radial distance function
r(x)=d(x,x_{0})
for some
x_{0}
.

Throughout this section, we use the following notation:
\kappa:=\sqrt{-K_{\min}}>0
and the model function is defined as

s_{K_{\min}}(r):=\frac{1}{\kappa}\sinh(\kappa r),\qquad r\geq 0.

Define the
radial contraction map
and its inverse by

\displaystyle\Phi_{t,x_{1}}(x)

\displaystyle:=\Exp_{x_{1}}\big((1-t)\Log_{x_{1}}(x)\big),

\displaystyle\Psi_{t,x_{1}}(x)

\displaystyle:=\Exp_{x_{1}}\Big(\frac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)\Big).

and note that under our geodesic interpolation,
x_{t}=\Phi_{t,x_{1}}(x_{0})
, and
x_{0}=\Psi_{t,x_{1}}(x)
.

We first provide the density formula.

Lemma E.1

(Density of
p_{t}(x_{1}\mid x)
on a Hadamard manifold)
.

Let
(M,g)
be a complete, simply-connected,
d
-dimensional Riemannian manifold with non-positive curvature.
Let
p_{0},p_{1}
be the prior and target distributions, assuming independence.
Then the conditional density of
X_{1}
given
X_{t}=x
is

\displaystyle p_{t}(x_{1}\mid x)

\displaystyle=\frac{p_{1}(x_{1})\,p_{0}\big(\Psi_{t,x_{1}}(x)\big)\,J_{t}(x\mid x_{1})}{\displaystyle\int_{M}p_{1}(z)\,p_{0}\big(\Psi_{t,z}(x)\big)\,J_{t}(x\mid z)\,dV_{g}(z)},

\displaystyle\quad\text{where}\quad J_{t}(x\mid x_{1})=(1-t)^{-d}\,\frac{\big|\det(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\frac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\big|}{\big|\det(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\big|}.

Furthermore, we have

\displaystyle p_{t}(x)=\int_{M}p_{1}(z)\,p_{0}\big(\Psi_{t,z}(x)\big)\,J_{t}(x\mid z)\,dV_{g}(z).

Proof.

Notice that
\Phi_{t,x_{1}}
is a global diffeomorphism, and
\Psi_{t,x_{1}}
is its inverse.
For fixed
x_{1}
and
x
, the change-of-variables formula gives

\displaystyle dV_{g}(\Psi_{t,x_{1}}(x))=\big|\det(d\Psi_{t,x_{1}})_{x}\big|\,dV_{g}(x),

which implies

\displaystyle p(\Psi_{t,x_{1}}(x),x_{1})\,dV_{g}(\Psi_{t,x_{1}}(x))\,dV_{g}(x_{1})

\displaystyle=p_{0}\big(\Psi_{t,x_{1}}(x)\big)\,p_{1}(x_{1})\,\big|\det(d\Psi_{t,x_{1}})_{x}\big|\,dV_{g}(x)\,dV_{g}(x_{1}).

We define

\displaystyle J_{t}(x\mid x_{1}):=\big|\det(d\Psi_{t,x_{1}})_{x}\big|.

Equivalently, the joint measure of
(X_{t},X_{1})
can be written as

\displaystyle p_{t}(x,x_{1})\,dV_{g}(x)\,dV_{g}(x_{1})

\displaystyle=p_{0}\big(\Psi_{t,x_{1}}(x)\big)\,p_{1}(x_{1})\,J_{t}(x\mid x_{1})\,dV_{g}(x)\,dV_{g}(x_{1}),

Integrating the joint density over
x_{1}
yields the marginal

p_{t}(x)=\int_{M}p_{1}(z)\,p_{0}\big(\Psi_{t,z}(x)\big)\,J_{t}(x\mid z)\,dV_{g}(z).

Therefore, by Bayes’ rule,

\displaystyle p_{t}(x_{1}\mid x)=\frac{p_{t}(x,x_{1})}{p_{t}(x)}=\frac{p_{1}(x_{1})\,p_{0}\big(\Psi_{t,x_{1}}(x)\big)\,J_{t}(x\mid x_{1})}{\displaystyle\int_{M}p_{1}(z)\,p_{0}\big(\Psi_{t,z}(x)\big)\,J_{t}(x\mid z)\,dV_{g}(z)}.

It remains to compute
J_{t}(x\mid x_{1})
. Recall that
\Psi_{t,x_{1}}(x)=\Exp_{x_{1}}\Big(\frac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)\Big)
.
Using chain rule,

\displaystyle(d\Psi_{t,x_{1}})_{x}

\displaystyle=(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{v}\circ\big((1-t)^{-1}Id\big)\circ(d\Log_{x_{1}})_{x}.

Using
(d\Log_{x_{1}})_{x}=\big((d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\big)^{-1}
, we have

\displaystyle(d\Psi_{t,x_{1}})_{x}

\displaystyle=(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\frac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\circ\big((1-t)^{-1}Id\big)\circ\big((d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\big)^{-1}.

Taking determinants,

\displaystyle J_{t}(x\mid x_{1})=(d\Psi_{t,x_{1}})_{x}=(1-t)^{-d}\,\frac{\big|\det(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\frac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\big|}{\big|\det(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\big|}.

∎

Now we prove Proposition
B.6
, which verifies Assumption
4
.

Proof.
[Proof of Proposition
B.6
]
The desired result is directly implied by Lemma
E.4
and Lemma
E.6
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|v(t,x)\|^{2}]\lesssim

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[d(x_{0},x_{1})^{2}]\lesssim d,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|\nabla v(t,x)\|]\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{d}{1-t}L_{R}\mathbb{E}[d(x_{0},x_{1})^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}[s_{K_{\min}}(d(x_{0},x_{1}))^{6}]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{d^{2+6\lambda}}{1-t}L_{R}M_{\lambda_{1}}^{\frac{1}{2}},

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[|\frac{d}{dt}v(t,x)|]\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{d}{1-t}L_{R}\mathbb{E}[d(x_{0},x_{1})^{2}s_{K_{\min}}(d(x_{0},x_{1}))^{6}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}[d(x_{0},x_{1})^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{d^{2+6\lambda}}{1-t}L_{R}M_{\lambda_{1}}^{\frac{1}{2}},

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|\grad_{x}\Div v(t,x)\|]\lesssim

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[d(x_{0},x_{1})s_{K_{\min}}(d(x_{0},x_{1}))^{6}]\frac{d^{2}}{(1-t)^{2}}L_{R}^{3}\lesssim\frac{d^{3+12\lambda}}{(1-t)^{2}}L_{R}^{3},

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[|\frac{d}{dt}\Div v(t,x)|]\lesssim

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[d(x_{0},x_{1})^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{d^{2}}{(1-t)^{2}}L_{R}^{3}\mathbb{E}[d(x_{1},x_{0})^{2}s_{K_{\min}}(d(x_{0},x_{1}))^{12}]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{d^{3+12\lambda}}{(1-t)^{2}}L_{R}^{3}M_{\lambda_{1}}^{\frac{1}{2}},

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|\nabla v(t,x)\|^{2}]\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{d^{2}}{(1-t)^{2}}L_{R}^{2}\mathbb{E}[d(x_{0},x_{1})^{4}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}[s_{K_{\min}}(d(x_{0},x_{1}))^{12}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim\frac{d^{3+12\lambda}}{(1-t)^{2}}L_{R}^{2}M_{\lambda_{1}}^{\frac{1}{2}},

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[|\frac{d}{dt}v(t,x)|^{2}]\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{d^{2}}{(1-t)^{2}}L_{R}^{2}\mathbb{E}[d(x_{0},x_{1})^{4}s_{K_{\min}}(d(x_{0},x_{1}))^{12}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}[d(x_{0},x_{1})^{4}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim\frac{d^{3+12\lambda}}{(1-t)^{2}}L_{R}^{2}M_{\lambda_{1}}^{\frac{1}{2}},

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|\grad_{x}\Div v(t,x)\|^{2}]\lesssim

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[d(x_{0},x_{1})^{2}s_{K_{\min}}(d(x_{0},x_{1}))^{12}]\frac{d^{4}}{(1-t)^{4}}L_{R}^{6}\lesssim\frac{d^{5+24\lambda}}{(1-t)^{4}}L_{R}^{6}M_{\lambda_{1}}.

∎

E.1
Proof of Proposition
5.4

We start to prove the score regularity result. In the following Lemma, we decompose
\mathbb{E}\big[\|\grad\log p_{t}(X_{t})\|^{2}\big]
as the sum of two terms.

Lemma E.2
.

For
X_{t}\sim p_{t}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\|\grad\log p_{t}(X_{t})\|^{2}\big]\leq 2\,\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\|\grad_{x}\log p_{0}(\Psi_{t,X_{1}}(X_{t}))\big\|^{2}\Big]+2\,\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\|\grad_{x}\log J_{t}(X_{t}\mid X_{1})\big\|^{2}\Big].

Proof.

By Lemma
E.1
,

\displaystyle p_{t}(x)=\int_{M}p_{1}(z)\,p_{0}\big(\Psi_{t,z}(x)\big)\,J_{t}(x\mid z)\,dV_{g}(z).

Taking the Riemannian gradient with respect to
x
and differentiating under the integral sign,

\displaystyle\grad p_{t}(x)

\displaystyle=\int_{M}p_{1}(z)\,\grad_{x}\Big(p_{0}(\Psi_{t,z}(x))\,J_{t}(x\mid z)\Big)\,dV_{g}(z).

(17)

Using the identity
\grad(\phi)=\phi\,\grad\log\phi
, we have

\grad_{x}\Big(p_{0}(\Psi_{t,z}(x))\,J_{t}(x\mid z)\Big)=p_{0}(\Psi_{t,z}(x))\,J_{t}(x\mid z)\,\grad_{x}\log\Big(p_{0}(\Psi_{t,z}(x))\,J_{t}(x\mid z)\Big).

Plugging into (
17
) and dividing by
p_{t}(x)
yields

\displaystyle\grad\log p_{t}(x)

\displaystyle=\frac{\grad p_{t}(x)}{p_{t}(x)}=\int_{M}\grad_{x}\log\Big(p_{0}(\Psi_{t,z}(x))\,J_{t}(x\mid z)\Big)\,\frac{p_{1}(z)\,p_{0}(\Psi_{t,z}(x))\,J_{t}(x\mid z)}{p_{t}(x)}\,dV_{g}(z).

Observe that

\displaystyle\frac{p_{1}(z)\,p_{0}(\Psi_{t,z}(x))\,J_{t}(x\mid z)}{p_{t}(x)}=\frac{p_{1}(z)\,p_{0}(\Psi_{t,z}(x))\,J_{t}(x\mid z)}{\int_{M}p_{1}(z)\,p_{0}\big(\Psi_{t,z}(x)\big)\,J_{t}(x\mid z)\,dV_{g}(z)}=p_{t}(z\mid x).

Hence we have

\displaystyle\grad\log p_{t}(x)=\grad\log p_{t}(x)

\displaystyle=\int_{M}\grad_{x}\log\Big(p_{0}(\Psi_{t,z}(x))\,J_{t}(x\mid z)\Big)\,p_{t}(z\mid x)\,dV_{g}(z)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big[\grad_{x}\log\big(p_{0}(\Psi_{t,X_{1}}(x))J_{t}(x\mid X_{1})\big)\mid X_{t}=x\Big].

Next, apply Jensen’s inequality,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|\grad\log p_{t}(x)\|^{2}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big[\|\mathbb{E}[\grad_{x}\log\big(p_{0}(\Psi_{t,X_{1}}(x))J_{t}(x\mid X_{1})\big)\mid X_{t}=x]\|^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbb{E}[\|\grad_{x}\log\big(p_{0}(\Psi_{t,X_{1}}(x))J_{t}(x\mid X_{1})\big)\|^{2}\mid X_{t}=x]\Big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big[\|\grad_{x}\log\big(p_{0}(\Psi_{t,X_{1}}(x))J_{t}(x\mid X_{1})\big)\|^{2}\Big].

The result follows from

\grad_{x}\log\big(p_{0}(\Psi)\,J_{t}\big)=\grad_{x}\log p_{0}(\Psi)+\grad_{x}\log J_{t},\qquad\|a+b\|^{2}\leq 2\|a\|^{2}+2\|b\|^{2}.

∎

Now we prove Proposition
5.4
.

Proof.
[Proof of Proposition
5.4
]
From Lemma
E.2
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\|\grad\log p_{t}(X_{t})\|^{2}\big]\leq 2\,\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\|\grad_{x}\log p_{0}(\Psi_{t,X_{1}}(X_{t}))\big\|^{2}\Big]+2\,\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\|\grad_{x}\log J_{t}(X_{t}\mid X_{1})\big\|^{2}\Big].

We show the following in Appendix
E.3
(Lemma
E.7
and Lemma
E.8
):

\displaystyle\|\grad_{x}\log J_{t}(x\mid x_{1})\|\leq

\displaystyle d\,\|(d\Log_{x_{1}})_{x}\|\Bigg(\frac{1}{1-t}\,\|\nabla(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\frac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\|+\|\nabla(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\|\Bigg),

\displaystyle\Big\|\grad_{x}\log p_{0}(\Psi_{t,X_{1}}(X_{t}))\Big\|\leq

\displaystyle 2dd(x_{0},z)\Big\|(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\tfrac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\Big\|_{\op}\;\frac{1}{1-t}\;\big\|(d\Log_{x_{1}})_{x}\big\|_{\op}.

We also prove the following in Lemma
E.10
and Lemma
E.12
:

\displaystyle\|(d\Log_{x_{1}})_{x}\|

\displaystyle\leq 1,

\displaystyle\Big\|(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\tfrac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\Big\|_{\op}

\displaystyle\leq s_{K_{\min}}(\|\tfrac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)\|)/\|\tfrac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)\|=s_{K_{\min}}(d(x_{0},x_{1}))/d(x_{0},x_{1}),

\displaystyle\|\nabla(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\frac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{16}{3}s_{K_{\min}}(d(x_{0},x_{1})/2)^{2}L_{R}s_{K_{\min}}(d(x_{0},x_{1})).

So together,

\displaystyle\|\grad_{x}\log J_{t}(x\mid x_{1})\|\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{d}{1-t}L_{R}s_{K_{\min}}(d(x_{0},x_{1}))^{3},

\displaystyle\Big\|\grad_{x}\log p_{0}(\Psi_{t,X_{1}}(X_{t}))\Big\|\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{d}{1-t}s_{K_{\min}}(d(x_{0},x_{1}))d(x_{0},z).

Using Lemma
E.4
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\|\grad\log p_{t}(X_{t})\|^{2}\big]\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{d^{2}}{(1-t)^{2}}L_{R}^{2}\mathbb{E}[s_{K_{\min}}(d(x_{0},x_{1}))^{6}]\lesssim\frac{d^{2}}{(1-t)^{2}}L_{R}^{2}\mathbb{E}[s_{K_{\min}}(d(x_{0},x_{1}))^{6}]

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{d^{2+12\lambda}}{(1-t)^{2}}L_{R}^{2}M,

where
\lambda=\max\{1,\kappa\}
.

∎

E.2
Auxiliary Results for Regularity for Hadamard and SPD manifolds

E.2.1
Expectation Control

Due to the non-compact nature of a Hadamard manifold as well as curvature distortion, bounding expectations of the form

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[d(X_{0},X_{1})^{2}\,s_{K_{\min}}\big(d(X_{0},X_{1})\big)^{a}],\quad a\in\mathbb{N},

is a key step to establish regularity. We show that by choosing
X_{0}
following a Riemannian Gaussian distribution, the above expectation can be controlled for a class of data distribution
X_{1}
satisfying certain moment condition (
7
).

Lemma E.3
.

Let
(M,g)
be a complete, simply connected
d
-dimensional Riemannian manifold (Hadamard) and assume

K_{\min}\ \leq\ \sec\ \leq\ 0,\qquad\text{where }K_{\min}<0\text{ is independent of }d.

Fix a basepoint
z\in M
and set
r(x):=d(x,z)
.
Define

\displaystyle p_{0}(x)=\frac{1}{Z}e^{(-\beta\,r(x)^{m})},\qquad Z:=\int_{M}e^{-\beta\,r(x)^{m}}\,dV_{g}(x),\qquad\text{where}\qquad\beta=d,m=2.

Let
\lambda\geq 0
be independent of
d
.
Then for
b\in\{0,1,2,3,4\}
,

\mathbb{E}_{p_{0}}\big[r(x)^{b}e^{\lambda r(x)}\big]\ =\mathcal{O}(d^{2\lambda}(\log d)^{b}).

Proof.
We first write the expectation as an integral over tangent space.
Since
M
is Hadamard,
\Exp_{z}:T_{z}M\to M
is a global
C^{\infty}
diffeomorphism.
We equip the vector space
T_{z}M
with the inner product
g
, and denote by
dv
the corresponding Lebesgue measure. For any measurable
F:M\to[0,\infty]
, the change-of-variables formula gives

\displaystyle\int_{M}F(x)\,dV_{g}(x)=\int_{T_{z}M}F(\Exp_{z}(v))\;\big|\det\big(d(\Exp_{z})_{v}\big)\big|\;dv.

We now apply this formula with
F_{1}(x)=e^{-d\,r(x)^{2}}
,
F_{2}(x)=r(x)^{b}e^{\lambda r(x)}e^{-d\,r(x)^{2}}
. Since
r(\Exp_{z}(v))=\|v\|
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{p_{0}}[r^{b}e^{\lambda r}]=\frac{\int_{T_{z}M}\|v\|^{b}e^{\lambda\|v\|}e^{-d\|v\|^{2}}\,\big|\det(d(\Exp_{z})_{v})\big|\,dv}{\int_{T_{z}M}e^{-d\|v\|^{2}}\,\big|\det(d(\Exp_{z})_{v})\big|\,dv}.

(18)

Recall that using comparison theorem (note that the
d-1
comes from
\det
) we have

1\ \leq\ \big|\det(d(\Exp_{z})_{r\theta})\big|\ \leq\ \Big(\frac{s_{K_{\min}}(r)}{r}\Big)^{d-1},\qquad\forall r>0.

(19)

Since
\frac{s_{K_{\min}}(r)}{r}=\frac{\sinh(\kappa r)}{\kappa r}\leq\,e^{\kappa r}
, we have

\big|\det(d(\Exp_{z})_{r\theta})\big|\ \leq\,e^{\kappa(d-1)r}.

(20)

We now integrate in
T_{z}M
using Euclidean polar coordinates:

v=r\theta
with
r\in[0,\infty)
,
\theta\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
, and

dv=r^{d-1}\,dr\,d\theta.

We split at
R=2\log d
and bound the expectation. Consider the decomposition below

\mathbb{E}_{p_{0}}[r^{b}e^{\lambda r}]=\mathbb{E}_{p_{0}}[r^{b}e^{\lambda r}\mathbf{1}_{\{r\leq R\}}]+\mathbb{E}_{p_{0}}[r^{b}e^{\lambda r}\mathbf{1}_{\{r>R\}}].

We first consider the central part. On
\{r\leq R\}
,

r^{b}e^{\lambda r}\leq R^{b}e^{\lambda R}
, hence

\mathbb{E}_{p_{0}}[r^{b}e^{\lambda r}\mathbf{1}_{\{r\leq R\}}]\leq R^{b}e^{\lambda R}=(2\log d)^{b}\,d^{2\lambda}.

(21)

We next consider the tail part. Write the tail contribution as
N_{\mathrm{tail}}/Z
.
Using change of variable formula and the upper bound (
20
), we obtain

\displaystyle N_{\mathrm{tail}}

\displaystyle:=\int_{\{x:\,r(x)>R\}}r(x)^{b}e^{\lambda r(x)}e^{-dr(x)^{2}}\,dV_{g}(x)

\displaystyle=\int_{\{v:\,\|v\|>R\}}\|v\|^{b}e^{\lambda\|v\|}e^{-d\|v\|^{2}}\big|\det(d(\Exp_{z})_{v})\big|\,dv

\displaystyle\leq|\mathbb{S}^{d-1}|\int_{R}^{\infty}r^{d-1+b}e^{(-dr^{2}+(\lambda+\kappa(d-1))r)}\,dr.

(22)

Define

\Psi_{d}(r):=-dr^{2}+(\lambda+\kappa(d-1))r+(d-1+b)\log r.

We justify that this
\Psi_{d}(r)
is dominated by the
-dr^{2}
term.
For
r\geq R=2\log d
, we have
(d-1+b)\log r\leq\frac{d}{4}r^{2},\forall d\geq 4
.
Also, for
r\geq R
and
d
large,
\kappa(d-1)r\leq\frac{d}{4}r^{2}
because this is equivalent to

r\geq 4\kappa(1-1/d)
, which holds once
R\geq 4\kappa
(i.e.
d\geq e^{2\kappa}
).
Finally,
\lambda r\leq\frac{d}{8}r^{2}
holds for all
r\geq R
once
d\geq 8\lambda
.
Summing these inequalities yields, there exists some constant
d_{1}
s.t. for all
d\geq d_{1}
and all
r\geq R
,

(\lambda+\kappa(d-1))r+(d-1+b)\log r\leq\frac{d}{2}r^{2},\quad\text{hence}\quad\Psi_{d}(r)\leq-\frac{d}{2}r^{2}.

Therefore, for
d\geq d_{1}
,

\displaystyle\int_{R}^{\infty}e^{\Psi_{d}(r)}\,dr

\displaystyle\leq\int_{R}^{\infty}e^{-(d/2)r^{2}}\,dr=\int_{R}^{\infty}\frac{1}{rd}(-\frac{d}{dr}e^{-(d/2)r^{2}})\,dr\leq\frac{1}{Rd}\int_{R}^{\infty}(-\frac{d}{dr}e^{-(d/2)r^{2}})\,dr

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{dR}\exp\Big(-\frac{d}{2}R^{2}\Big)=\frac{1}{dR}\exp\big(-2d(\log d)^{2}\big).

Plugging into (
22
) gives

N_{\mathrm{tail}}\leq|\mathbb{S}^{d-1}|\cdot\frac{1}{dR}\exp\big(-2d(\log d)^{2}\big).

(23)

For the denominator
Z
, recall that

\big|\det(d(\Exp_{z})_{v})\big|\geq 1
, so we get

\displaystyle\int_{T_{z}M}e^{-d\|v\|^{2}}\big|\det(d(\Exp_{z})_{v})\big|\,dv

\displaystyle\geq\int_{T_{z}M}e^{-d\|v\|^{2}}\,dv=\Big(\frac{\pi}{d}\Big)^{d/2}.

Hence, using (
23
),

\mathbb{E}_{p_{0}}[r^{b}e^{\lambda r}\mathbf{1}_{\{r>R\}}]=\frac{N_{\mathrm{tail}}}{Z}\leq\frac{|\mathbb{S}^{d-1}|}{dR}\exp\big(-2d(\log d)^{2}\big)\cdot\Big(\frac{d}{\pi}\Big)^{d/2}=\frac{|\mathbb{S}^{d-1}|}{2d\log d}d^{-2d\log d}\cdot\Big(\frac{d}{\pi}\Big)^{d/2}\lesssim 1.

Together, we conclude that for all
d\geq d_{1}
, combining (
21
) with the tail bound gives

\mathbb{E}_{p_{0}}[r^{b}e^{\lambda r}]\leq(2\log d)^{b}d^{2\lambda}+1=\mathcal{O}(d^{2\lambda}(\log d)^{b}).

∎

Now we present intermediate steps.

Lemma E.4

(Auxiliary bounds for expectation of
s_{K_{\min}}
)
.

Let
M
be a Hadamard manifold.
Fix
z\in M
. Choose prior as
X_{0}\sim p_{0}
where

p_{0}(x)\propto\exp\big(-d\,d(x,z)^{2}\big).

Assume Assumption
1
holds. Let
\kappa=\sqrt{-K_{\min}}
and
\lambda_{0}=a\max\{1,\kappa\}
, where
0\leq a\leq a_{0}
is some constant.
Assume

\displaystyle\max\big\{\mathbb{E}[d(X_{1},z)^{4}e^{\lambda_{1}d(X_{1},z)}],\mathbb{E}[e^{\lambda_{1}d(X_{1},z)}]\big\}\leq M,\qquad\text{where}\qquad\lambda_{1}=a_{1}\max\{1,\kappa\}.

Then we have, for
b\in\{0,1,2,3,4\}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[d(X_{0},X_{1})^{b}\,s_{K_{\min}}\big(d(X_{0},X_{1})\big)^{a}\Big]

\displaystyle\lesssim M\mathbb{E}_{X_{0}\sim p_{0}}\Big[d(X_{0},z)^{b}e^{\lambda_{0}d(X_{0},z)}\Big]\lesssim d^{2\lambda_{0}}(\log d)^{b}M,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[d(X_{0},z)^{b}\,s_{K_{\min}}\big(d(X_{0},X_{1})\big)^{a}\Big]

\displaystyle\lesssim M\mathbb{E}_{X_{0}\sim p_{0}}\Big[d(X_{0},z)^{b}e^{\lambda_{0}d(X_{0},z)}\Big]\lesssim d^{2\lambda_{0}}(\log d)^{b}M.

Proof.
Observe that

\displaystyle s_{K_{\min}}(d(x_{0},x_{1}))^{a}=\frac{\sinh^{a}(\kappa d(x_{0},x_{1}))}{\kappa^{a}}\leq(e^{\max\{1,\kappa\}d(x_{0},x_{1})})^{a}.

Hence

\displaystyle d(x_{0},x_{1})^{b}\,s_{K_{\min}}\big(d(x_{0},x_{1})\big)^{a}\leq d(x_{0},x_{1})^{b}(e^{\max\{1,\kappa\}d(x_{0},x_{1})})^{a}.

(24)

Taking expectation, it suffices to bound

\mathbb{E}\big[d(x_{0},x_{1})^{b}e^{\lambda_{0}d(x_{0},x_{1})}\big]
with
\lambda_{0}=a\max\{1,\kappa\}
.

The triangle inequality gives

\displaystyle d(x_{0},x_{1})\leq d(x_{0},z)+d(x_{1},z).

Therefore,

\displaystyle d(x_{0},x_{1})^{b}e^{\lambda_{0}d(x_{0},x_{1})}\leq

\displaystyle\big(d(x_{0},z)+d(x_{1},z)\big)^{b}\exp\big(\lambda_{0}(d(x_{0},z)+d(x_{1},z))\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle(d(x_{0},z)^{b}+d(x_{1},z)^{b})\exp(\lambda_{0}d(x_{0},z))\exp(\lambda_{0}d(x_{1},z)).

Take expectation and use independence of
X_{0}
and
X_{1}
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[d(X_{0},X_{1})^{b}e^{\lambda_{0}d(X_{0},X_{1})}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq 2\,\mathbb{E}\Big[e^{\lambda_{0}d(X_{1},z)}\Big]\,\mathbb{E}_{X_{0}\sim p_{0}}\Big[d(X_{0},z)^{b}e^{\lambda_{0}d(X_{0},z)}\Big]

\displaystyle\quad+2\,\mathbb{E}\Big[d(X_{1},z)^{b}e^{\lambda_{0}d(X_{1},z)}\Big]\,\mathbb{E}_{X_{0}\sim p_{0}}\Big[e^{\lambda_{0}d(X_{0},z)}\Big].

(25)

By Assumption, both
\mathbb{E}\Big[e^{\lambda_{0}d(X_{1},z)}\Big]
and
\mathbb{E}\Big[d(X_{1},z)^{b}e^{\lambda_{0}d(X_{1},z)}\Big]
are controlled by
M
.
It hence follows that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[d(X_{0},X_{1})^{b}e^{\lambda_{0}d(X_{0},X_{1})}\Big]

\displaystyle\lesssim M\mathbb{E}_{X_{0}\sim p_{0}}\Big[d(X_{0},z)^{b}e^{\lambda_{0}d(X_{0},z)}\Big]\lesssim d^{2\lambda_{0}}(\log d)^{b}M,

where we applied Lemma
E.3
.

∎

E.2.2
Regularity Control

The following lemma summarizes the building blocks needed to establish regularity, serving as the same purpose as Lemma
D.6
,
D.7
,
D.8
, and
D.9
.

Lemma E.5
.

On
\SPD(n)
, with prior distribution being
p_{0}\propto\exp(-dd(x_{0},z)^{2})
, assume Assumption
1
, we have the following bounds.

\displaystyle\|\grad_{x}\log J_{t}(x\mid x_{1})\|\lesssim

\displaystyle d\,\frac{1}{1-t}L_{R}s_{K_{\min}}(d(x_{0},x_{1}))^{3},

\displaystyle\Big\|\grad_{x}\log p_{0}(\Psi_{t,x_{1}}(x))\Big\|\lesssim

\displaystyle d\frac{1}{1-t}s_{K_{\min}}(d(x_{0},x_{1}))d(x_{0},z),

\displaystyle|\partial_{t}\log J_{t}(x\mid x_{1})|\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{d}{1-t}d(x_{0},x_{1})s_{K_{\min}}(d(x_{0},x_{1}))^{3}L_{R},

\displaystyle|\partial_{t}\log p_{0}(\Psi_{t,x_{1}}(x))|\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{d}{1-t}d(x_{0},z)s_{K_{\min}}(d(x_{0},x_{1})),

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{x}^{2}\log p_{0}(\Psi_{t,x_{1}}(x))\|_{\op}\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{d}{(1-t)^{2}}s_{K_{\min}}(d(x_{0},x_{1}))^{4}L_{R}d(x_{0},z),

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{x}^{2}\log J_{t}(x\mid x_{1})\|_{\op}\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{d}{(1-t)^{2}}L_{R}^{3}s_{K_{\min}}(d(x_{0},x_{1}))^{6},

\displaystyle\Big\|\partial_{t}\grad_{x}\log p_{0}(\Psi_{t,x_{1}}(x))\Big\|\lesssim

\displaystyle d(x_{0},z)\frac{d}{(1-t)^{2}}d(x_{0},x_{1})L_{R}s_{K_{\min}}(d(x_{0},x_{1}))^{3},

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}\grad_{x}\log J_{t}(x\mid x_{1})\|\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{d}{(1-t)^{2}}L_{R}^{3}d(x_{1},x_{0})s_{K_{\min}}(d(x_{0},x_{1}))^{6},

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{x}\Log_{x}(x_{1})\|_{\op}^{2}\lesssim

\displaystyle d(x_{0},x_{1})^{2},

\displaystyle|\Div_{x}\Log_{x}(x_{1})|^{2}\lesssim

\displaystyle d^{2}d(x_{0},x_{1})^{2},

\displaystyle\|\grad\Div_{x}\Log_{x}(x_{1})\|\lesssim

\displaystyle dd(x_{0},x_{1})^{\frac{3}{2}}.

Here we emphasize that
\Log_{x}(x_{1})
is viewed as a vector field, for fixed
x_{1}
, so
\nabla\Log_{x}(x_{1})
is covariant derivative of the vector field
\Log_{x}(x_{1})
.
Consequently,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|\grad_{x}\log p_{t}(x_{1}\mid x)\|^{2}\mid X_{t}=x]\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{d^{2}}{(1-t)^{2}}L_{R}^{2}\mathbb{E}[s_{K_{\min}}(d(x_{0},x_{1}))^{6}],

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|\grad_{x}\log p_{t}(x_{1}\mid x)\|^{4}\mid X_{t}=x]\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{d^{4}}{(1-t)^{4}}L_{R}^{4}\mathbb{E}[s_{K_{\min}}(d(x_{0},x_{1}))^{12}],

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[|\partial_{t}\log p_{t}(x_{1}\mid x)|^{2}\mid X_{t}=x]\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{d^{2}}{(1-t)^{2}}L_{R}^{2}\mathbb{E}[d(x_{0},x_{1})^{2}s_{K_{\min}}(d(x_{0},x_{1}))^{6}],

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[|\partial_{t}\log p_{t}(x_{1}\mid x)|^{4}\mid X_{t}=x]\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{d^{4}}{(1-t)^{4}}L_{R}^{4}\mathbb{E}[d(x_{0},x_{1})^{4}s_{K_{\min}}(d(x_{0},x_{1}))^{12}],

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|\nabla_{x}^{2}\log p_{t}(x_{1}\mid x)\|_{\op}^{2}\mid X_{t}=x]\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{d^{2}}{(1-t)^{4}}L_{R}^{6}\mathbb{E}[s_{K_{\min}}(d(x_{0},x_{1}))^{12}]

\displaystyle+\frac{d^{4}}{(1-t)^{4}}L_{R}^{4}\mathbb{E}[s_{K_{\min}}(d(x_{0},x_{1}))^{6}]^{2},

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|\nabla_{x}^{2}\log p_{t}(x_{1}\mid x)\|_{\op}^{4}\mid X_{t}=x]\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{d^{4}}{(1-t)^{8}}L_{R}^{12}\mathbb{E}[s_{K_{\min}}(d(x_{0},x_{1}))^{24}]

\displaystyle+\frac{d^{8}}{(1-t)^{8}}L_{R}^{8}\mathbb{E}[s_{K_{\min}}(d(x_{0},x_{1}))^{12}]^{2},

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|\partial_{t}\grad_{x}\log p_{t}(x_{1}\mid x)\|^{2}\mid X_{t}=x]\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{d^{2}}{(1-t)^{4}}L_{R}^{6}\mathbb{E}[d(x_{1},x_{0})^{2}s_{K_{\min}}(d(x_{0},x_{1}))^{12}]

\displaystyle+\frac{d^{4}}{(1-t)^{4}}L_{R}^{4}\mathbb{E}[s_{K_{\min}}(d(x_{0},x_{1}))^{6}]\mathbb{E}[d(x_{0},x_{1})^{2}s_{K_{\min}}(d(x_{0},x_{1}))^{6}].

Proof.

Throughout the proof, we use
r(x)
to denote the radial distance function
d(x,z)
, where
z
is the center of
p_{0}\propto\exp(-\beta d(x_{0},z)^{2})
.
We show the following in Appendix
E.3
(Lemma
E.7
and Lemma
E.8
):

\displaystyle\|\grad_{x}\log J_{t}(x\mid x_{1})\|\leq

\displaystyle d\,\|(d\Log_{x_{1}})_{x}\|\Bigg(\frac{1}{1-t}\,\|\nabla(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\frac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\|+\|\nabla(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\|\Bigg),

\displaystyle\Big\|\grad_{x}\log p_{0}(\Psi_{t,X_{1}}(X_{t}))\Big\|\leq

\displaystyle 2dd_{g}(x_{0},z)\Big\|(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\tfrac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\Big\|_{\op}\;\frac{1}{1-t}\;\big\|(d\Log_{x_{1}})_{x}\big\|_{\op}.

We also prove the following in (Lemma
E.10
and Lemma
E.12
:

\displaystyle\|(d\Log_{x_{1}})_{x}\|

\displaystyle\leq 1,

\displaystyle\big\|\big(\nabla(d\Log_{x})\big)_{y}\big\|

\displaystyle\leq\big\|\big(\nabla(d\Exp_{x})\big)_{\Log_{x}(y)}\big\|,

\displaystyle\Big\|(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\tfrac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\Big\|_{\op}

\displaystyle\leq s_{K_{\min}}(\|\tfrac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)\|)/\|\tfrac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)\|\lesssim s_{K_{\min}}(d(x_{0},x_{1})),

\displaystyle\|\nabla(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\frac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{16}{3}s_{K_{\min}}(d(x_{0},x_{1})/2)^{2}L_{R}s_{K_{\min}}(d(x_{0},x_{1}))\lesssim L_{R}s_{K_{\min}}(d(x_{0},x_{1}))^{3},

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{2}(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\frac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\|

\displaystyle\lesssim L_{R}^{3}s_{K_{\min}}(d(x_{0},x_{1}))^{5}.

We estimate the terms as follows.
For gradient and time derivative, we have

\displaystyle\|\grad_{x}\log J_{t}(x\mid x_{1})\|\lesssim

\displaystyle d\,\frac{1}{1-t}L_{R}s_{K_{\min}}(d(x_{0},x_{1}))^{3},

\displaystyle\Big\|\grad_{x}\log p_{0}(\Psi_{t,X_{1}}(X_{t}))\Big\|\lesssim

\displaystyle d\frac{1}{1-t}s_{K_{\min}}(d(x_{0},x_{1}))d(x_{0},z),

and

\displaystyle|\partial_{t}\log J_{t}(x\mid x_{1})|\leq

\displaystyle\frac{d}{1-t}+\frac{d}{(1-t)^{2}}\,\|\Log_{x_{1}}(x)\|\;\|\nabla(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\frac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{d}{1-t}d(x_{0},x_{1})s_{K_{\min}}(d(x_{0},x_{1}))^{3}L_{R},

\displaystyle|\partial_{t}\log p_{0}(\Psi_{t,x_{1}}(x))|\leq

\displaystyle 2\beta r\Big\|(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\tfrac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\Big\|_{\op}\;\frac{1}{(1-t)^{2}}\;\big\|\Log_{x_{1}}(x)\big\|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{d}{1-t}d(x_{0},z)s_{K_{\min}}(d(x_{0},x_{1})).

For second covariant derivative,

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{x}^{2}\log p_{0}(\Psi_{t,x_{1}}(x))\|_{\op}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\Big(\Big\|(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\tfrac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\Big\|_{\op}\;\frac{1}{1-t}\;\big\|(d\Log_{x_{1}})_{x}\big\|_{\op}\Big)^{2}\Big(2\beta+2\beta r\|\nabla^{2}r\|_{\op}\Big|_{\Psi_{t,x_{1}}(x)}\Big)

\displaystyle+\Big\|\nabla(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\tfrac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\Big\|\,\Big(\frac{1}{1-t}\big\|(d\Log_{x_{1}})_{x}\big\|_{\op}\Big)^{2}\,2\beta r

\displaystyle+\Big\|(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\tfrac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\Big\|_{\op}\,\frac{1}{1-t}\,\big\|\nabla(d\Log_{x_{1}})_{x}\big\|\,2\beta r

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(1-t)^{2}}s_{K_{\min}}(d(x_{0},x_{1}))^{2}(dd(x_{0},z)\kappa\coth(\kappa d(x_{0},z)))

\displaystyle+\frac{d}{(1-t)^{2}}s_{K_{\min}}(d(x_{0},x_{1}))^{3}L_{R}d(x_{0},z)+\frac{d}{1-t}d(x_{0},z)s_{K_{\min}}(d(x_{0},x_{1}))^{4}L_{R}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{d}{(1-t)^{2}}s_{K_{\min}}(d(x_{0},x_{1}))^{4}L_{R}d(x_{0},z),

where notice that
x\coth(x)
is of order
x
.
And we have

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{x}^{2}\log J_{t}(x\mid x_{1})\|_{\op}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle d\,\|(d\Log_{x_{1}})_{x}\|^{2}\Bigg[\frac{1}{(1-t)^{2}}\Big(\|\nabla(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\frac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\|^{2}+\|\nabla^{2}(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\frac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\|\Big)

\displaystyle+\Big(\|\nabla(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\|^{2}+\|\nabla^{2}(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\|\Big)\Bigg]

\displaystyle+d\,\|\nabla(d\Log_{x_{1}})_{x}\|\Bigg(\frac{1}{1-t}\,\|\nabla(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\frac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\|+\|\nabla(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\|\Bigg)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{d}{(1-t)^{2}}(L_{R}^{2}s_{K_{\min}}(d(x_{0},x_{1}))^{6}+L_{R}^{3}s_{K_{\min}}(d(x_{0},x_{1}))^{5})+\frac{d}{1-t}L_{R}^{2}s_{K_{\min}}(d(x_{0},x_{1}))^{6}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{d}{(1-t)^{2}}L_{R}^{3}s_{K_{\min}}(d(x_{0},x_{1}))^{6}.

For time derivative of gradient,

\displaystyle\Big\|\partial_{t}\grad_{x}\log p_{0}(\Psi_{t,x_{1}}(x))\Big\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\Big(\Big\|(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\tfrac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\Big\|_{\op}\;\frac{1}{1-t}\;\big\|(d\Log_{x_{1}})_{x}\big\|_{\op}\Big)\times\Big(2\beta+2\beta r\|\nabla^{2}r\|_{\op}\Big|_{\Psi_{t,x_{1}}(x)}\Big)

\displaystyle\times\Big\|(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\tfrac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\Big\|_{\op}\;\frac{1}{(1-t)^{2}}\;\big\|\Log_{x_{1}}(x)\big\|

\displaystyle+\Bigg[\frac{1}{(1-t)^{2}}\Big\|(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\tfrac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\Big\|_{\op}\,\big\|(d\Log_{x_{1}})_{x}\big\|_{\op}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{(1-t)^{3}}\,\big\|\Log_{x_{1}}(x)\big\|\,\Big\|\nabla(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\tfrac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\Big\|\,\big\|(d\Log_{x_{1}})_{x}\big\|_{\op}\Bigg]2\beta r

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\Big(d+dd(x_{0},z)\Big)s_{K_{\min}}(d(x_{0},x_{1}))^{2}\frac{1}{(1-t)^{2}}\;

\displaystyle+dd(x_{0},z)\Big(\frac{1}{(1-t)^{2}}s_{K_{\min}}(d(x_{0},x_{1}))+\frac{1}{(1-t)^{2}}d(x_{0},x_{1})L_{R}s_{K_{\min}}(d(x_{0},x_{1}))^{3}\Big)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle d(x_{0},z)\frac{d}{(1-t)^{2}}d(x_{0},x_{1})L_{R}s_{K_{\min}}(d(x_{0},x_{1}))^{3}.

And we have

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}\grad_{x}\log J_{t}(x\mid x_{1})\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|(d\Log_{x_{1}})_{x}\|\Bigg[\frac{d}{(1-t)^{2}}\|\nabla(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\frac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\|

\displaystyle+\frac{d}{(1-t)^{3}}\,\|\Log_{x_{1}}(x)\|\Big(\|\nabla(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\frac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\|^{2}+\|\nabla^{2}(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\frac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\|\Big)\Bigg]

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{d}{(1-t)^{2}}L_{R}s_{K_{\min}}(d(x_{0},x_{1}))^{3}+\frac{d}{(1-t)^{2}}d(x_{1},x_{0})\Big(L_{R}^{2}s_{K_{\min}}(d(x_{0},x_{1}))^{6}+L_{R}^{3}s_{K_{\min}}(d(x_{0},x_{1}))^{5}\Big)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{d}{(1-t)^{2}}L_{R}^{3}d(x_{1},x_{0})s_{K_{\min}}(d(x_{0},x_{1}))^{6}.

The three inequalities on
\Log
follow from Appendix
E.5
:

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{x}\Log_{x}(y)\|_{\op}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2+2\,d(x,y)^{2}\Big(\frac{s_{K_{\min}}^{\prime}(d(x,y))}{s_{K_{\min}}(d(x,y))}\Big)^{2},

\displaystyle|\Div_{x}\Log_{x}(y)|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2+2(d-1)^{2}\,d(x,y)^{2}\Big(\frac{s_{K_{\min}}^{\prime}(d(x,y))}{s_{K_{\min}}(d(x,y))}\Big)^{2},

\displaystyle\|\grad\Div_{x}\Log_{x}(y)\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sqrt{2}d}{2}(2\bigl(1+d(x,y)\,\frac{s_{K_{\min}}^{\prime}(d(x,y))}{s_{K_{\min}}(d(x,y))}\bigr))^{\frac{3}{2}}.

Now we prove the inequalities on derivatives of
\log p_{t}
.
We remark that due to Lemma
E.4
, comparing point-wise upper bounds for derivative of
\log J_{t}
and that of
\log p_{0}
, since the
d(x_{0},z)
term doesn’t increase the order of expectation compared with
d(x_{0},x_{1})
term, we can treat
d(x_{0},z)
as
d(x_{0},x_{1})
and see that derivatives of
\log J_{t}
dominate over the same derivative of
\log p_{0}
. Thus in computing derivatives of
\log p_{t}=\log p_{0}(\Psi_{t,x_{1}}(x))+\log J_{t}(x|x_{1})+\text{const}
, it suffices to consider derivatives of
\log J_{t}(x|x_{1})
terms.

The first four inequalities are straightforward by applying the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, and note that they are dominated by the
\log J_{t}
terms.
Following Lemma
D.8

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{x}^{2}\log p_{t}(x_{1}\mid x)\|_{\op}^{2}\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{2}\log J_{t}(x\mid X_{1})\|_{\op}^{2}+\mathbb{E}\bigl[\|\nabla^{2}\log J_{t}(x\mid X_{1})\|_{\op}^{2}\mid X_{t}=x\bigr]

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}\bigl[\|\grad_{x}\log J_{t}(x\mid X_{1})\|^{2}\mid X_{t}=x\bigr]\mathbb{E}\bigl[\|\grad\log p_{t}(x_{1}\mid x)\|^{2}\mid X_{t}=x\bigr],

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{x}^{2}\log p_{t}(x_{1}\mid x)\|_{\op}^{4}\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{2}\log J_{t}(x\mid X_{1})\|_{\op}^{4}

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}\bigl[\|\grad_{x}\log J_{t}(x\mid X_{1})\|^{4}\mid X_{t}=x\bigr]\mathbb{E}\bigl[\|\grad\log p_{t}(x_{1}\mid x)\|^{4}\mid X_{t}=x\bigr],

and plug in the corresponding expression (using Cauch-Schwarz), we obtain the bounds for the Hessian of
\log p_{t}
, and following Lemma
D.9
,

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}\grad_{x}\log p_{t}(x_{1}\mid x)\|

\displaystyle\leq\|\partial_{t}\grad_{x}\log J_{t}(x\mid x_{1})\|+\mathbb{E}\bigl[\|\partial_{t}\grad_{x}\log J_{t}(x\mid X_{1})\|\mid X_{t}=x\bigr]

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}[\|\grad_{x}\log J_{t}(x\mid X_{1})\|\,|\partial_{t}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x)|\mid X_{t}=x],

we plug in the corresponding expressions (using Cauch-Schwarz), we obtain the last inequality.
∎

We summarize the required bound in the following Lemma, which is similar to Lemma
D.10
,
D.11
,
D.12
and
D.13
.

Lemma E.6
.

On
\SPD(n)
, with prior distribution being
p_{0}\propto\exp(-dd(x_{0},z)^{2})
, under the conditions in Assumption
1
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|v(t,x)\|^{2}]\lesssim

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[d(x_{0},x_{1})^{2}],

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|\nabla v(t,x)\|]\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{d}{1-t}L_{R}\mathbb{E}[d(x_{0},x_{1})^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}[s_{K_{\min}}(d(x_{0},x_{1}))^{6}]^{\frac{1}{2}},

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[|\frac{d}{dt}v(t,x)|]\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{d}{1-t}L_{R}\mathbb{E}[d(x_{0},x_{1})^{2}s_{K_{\min}}(d(x_{0},x_{1}))^{6}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}[d(x_{0},x_{1})^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}},

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|\grad_{x}\Div v(t,x)\|]\lesssim

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[d(x_{0},x_{1})s_{K_{\min}}(d(x_{0},x_{1}))^{6}]\frac{d^{2}}{(1-t)^{2}}L_{R}^{3},

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[|\frac{d}{dt}\Div v(t,x)|]\lesssim

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[d(x_{0},x_{1})^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{d^{2}}{(1-t)^{2}}L_{R}^{3}\mathbb{E}[d(x_{1},x_{0})^{2}s_{K_{\min}}(d(x_{0},x_{1}))^{12}]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Furthermore,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|\nabla v(t,x)\|^{2}]\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{d^{2}}{(1-t)^{2}}L_{R}^{2}\mathbb{E}[d(x_{0},x_{1})^{4}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}[s_{K_{\min}}(d(x_{0},x_{1}))^{12}]^{\frac{1}{2}},

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[|\frac{d}{dt}v(t,x)|^{2}]\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{d^{2}}{(1-t)^{2}}L_{R}^{2}\mathbb{E}[d(x_{0},x_{1})^{4}s_{K_{\min}}(d(x_{0},x_{1}))^{12}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}[d(x_{0},x_{1})^{4}]^{\frac{1}{2}},

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|\grad_{x}\Div v(t,x)\|^{2}]\lesssim

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[d(x_{0},x_{1})^{2}s_{K_{\min}}(d(x_{0},x_{1}))^{12}]\frac{d^{4}}{(1-t)^{4}}L_{R}^{6}.

Proof.

Throughout the proof, we will use the bounds in Lemma
E.5
.We first control the vector field regularity. This is similar to Lemma
D.10
and Lemma
D.11
. We have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|\nabla v(t,x)\|]\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1-t}\mathbb{E}\bigl[\|\Log_{x}(X_{1})\|\,\|\grad_{x}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x)\|\mid X_{t}=x\bigr]

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{1-t}\mathbb{E}[\|\nabla\Log_{x}(x_{1})\|\mid X_{t}=x]

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{d}{1-t}L_{R}\mathbb{E}[d(x_{0},x_{1})^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}[s_{K_{\min}}(d(x_{0},x_{1}))^{6}]^{\frac{1}{2}},

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[|\frac{d}{dt}v(t,x)|]=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(1-t)^{2}}\mathbb{E}[\Log_{x}(x_{1})\mid X_{t}=x]+\frac{1}{1-t}\mathbb{E}[\Log_{x}(x_{1})\,\partial_{t}\log p_{t}(x_{1}\mid x)\mid X_{t}=x]

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{d}{1-t}L_{R}\mathbb{E}[d(x_{0},x_{1})^{2}s_{K_{\min}}(d(x_{0},x_{1}))^{6}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}[d(x_{0},x_{1})^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Now we control the divergence regularity.
Similar to Lemma
D.12
and , we have

\displaystyle\|\grad_{x}\Div v(t,x)\|\leq\frac{1}{1-t}\Bigl(T_{1}+T_{2}+T_{3}+T_{4}\Bigr),

where

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[T_{1}]=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|\grad_{x}\Div_{x}\Log_{x}(X_{1})\|]

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle d\mathbb{E}[d(x_{0},x_{1})^{\frac{3}{2}}],

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[T_{2}]=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[|\Div_{x}\Log_{x}(X_{1})|\,\|\grad_{x}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x)\|]

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[|\Div_{x}\Log_{x}(X_{1})|^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}[\|\grad_{x}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x)\|^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{d^{2}}{1-t}L_{R}\mathbb{E}[d(x_{0},x_{1})^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}[s_{K_{\min}}(d(x_{0},x_{1}))^{6}]^{\frac{1}{2}},

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[T_{3}]=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|\grad_{x}\langle\grad_{x}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x),\Log_{x}(X_{1})\rangle\|]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|\nabla\Log_{x}(X_{1})\|\,\|\grad_{x}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x)\|]

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathbb{E}[\|\nabla^{2}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x)\|\,\|\Log_{x}(X_{1})\|]

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\Big(\mathbb{E}[d(x,x_{1})^{2}]\frac{d^{2}}{(1-t)^{2}}L_{R}^{2}\mathbb{E}[s_{K_{\min}}(d(x_{0},x_{1}))^{6}]\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle+\Big(\frac{d^{2}}{(1-t)^{4}}L_{R}^{6}\mathbb{E}[s_{K_{\min}}(d(x_{0},x_{1}))^{12}]\mathbb{E}[d(x,x_{1})^{2}]\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{d}{1-t}L_{R}^{3}\mathbb{E}[s_{K_{\min}}(d(x_{0},x_{1}))^{12}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}[d(x_{0},x_{1})^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}},

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[T_{4}]=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[|\langle\grad_{x}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x),\Log_{x}(X_{1})\rangle|\,\|\grad_{x}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x)\|]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|\Log_{x}(X_{1})\|\,\|\grad_{x}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x)\|^{2}]

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[d(x,x_{1})s_{K_{\min}}(d(x_{0},x_{1}))^{6}]\frac{d^{2}}{(1-t)^{2}}L_{R}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[d(x_{0},x_{1})s_{K_{\min}}(d(x_{0},x_{1}))^{6}]\frac{d^{2}}{1-t}L_{R}^{2}.

Similar to Lemma
D.13
,

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\Div v(t,x)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(1-t)^{2}}\mathbb{E}\bigl[\Div_{x}\Log_{x}(X_{1})+\langle\grad_{x}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x),\Log_{x}(X_{1})\rangle\mid X_{t}=x\bigr]

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{1-t}\mathbb{E}[\Div_{x}\Log_{x}(X_{1})\,\partial_{t}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x)\mid X_{t}=x]

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{1-t}\mathbb{E}[\partial_{t}\langle\grad_{x}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x),\Log_{x}(X_{1})\rangle\mid X_{t}=x]

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{1-t}\mathbb{E}[\langle\grad_{x}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x),\Log_{x}(X_{1})\rangle\,\partial_{t}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x)\mid X_{t}=x].

We bound

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[T_{1}]=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(1-t)^{2}}\mathbb{E}\bigl[\Div_{x}\Log_{x}(X_{1})+\langle\grad_{x}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x),\Log_{x}(X_{1})\rangle\bigr]

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{d}{(1-t)^{2}}\mathbb{E}[d(x,x_{1})]+\frac{1}{(1-t)^{2}}\mathbb{E}[d(x,x_{1})^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}[\frac{d^{2}}{(1-t)^{2}}L_{R}^{2}s_{K_{\min}}(d(x_{0},x_{1}))^{6}]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{d}{1-t}\mathbb{E}[d(x_{0},x_{1})]+\frac{d}{(1-t)^{2}}L_{R}\mathbb{E}[d(x_{0},x_{1})^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}[s_{K_{\min}}(d(x_{0},x_{1}))^{6}]]^{\frac{1}{2}},

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[T_{2}]=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1-t}\mathbb{E}[\Div_{x}\Log_{x}(X_{1})\,\partial_{t}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x)]

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1-t}d\mathbb{E}[d(x,x_{1})^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{d}{1-t}L_{R}\mathbb{E}[d(x_{0},x_{1})^{2}s_{K_{\min}}(d(x_{0},x_{1}))^{6}]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{d^{2}}{1-t}L_{R}\mathbb{E}[d(x_{0},x_{1})^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}[d(x_{0},x_{1})^{2}s_{K_{\min}}(d(x_{0},x_{1}))^{6}]^{\frac{1}{2}},

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[T_{3}]=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1-t}\mathbb{E}[\partial_{t}\langle\grad_{x}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x),\Log_{x}(X_{1})\rangle]

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[d(x_{0},x_{1})^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{d}{(1-t)^{2}}L_{R}^{3}\mathbb{E}[d(x_{1},x_{0})^{2}s_{K_{\min}}(d(x_{0},x_{1}))^{12}]^{\frac{1}{2}},

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[T_{4}]=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1-t}\mathbb{E}[\langle\grad_{x}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x),\Log_{x}(X_{1})\rangle\,\partial_{t}\log p_{t}(X_{1}\mid x)]

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1-t}(\frac{d^{3}}{(1-t)^{3}}L_{R}^{3}\mathbb{E}[s_{K_{\min}}(d(x_{0},x_{1}))^{9}])^{\frac{1}{3}}\mathbb{E}[d(x,x_{1})^{3}]^{\frac{1}{3}}(\frac{d^{3}}{(1-t)^{3}}L_{R}^{3}\mathbb{E}[d(x_{0},x_{1})^{3}s_{K_{\min}}(d(x_{0},x_{1}))^{9}])^{\frac{1}{3}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{d^{2}}{(1-t)^{2}}L_{R}^{2}\mathbb{E}[s_{K_{\min}}(d(x_{0},x_{1}))^{9}]^{\frac{1}{3}}\mathbb{E}[d(x_{0},x_{1})^{3}]^{\frac{1}{3}}\mathbb{E}[d(x_{0},x_{1})^{3}s_{K_{\min}}(d(x_{0},x_{1}))^{9}]^{\frac{1}{3}}.

Higher order bounds follow exactly the same procedure.

∎

E.3
Auxiliary Bounds on Derivatives

Recall that we are using the following notation

\displaystyle\Psi_{t,x_{1}}(x)=\Exp_{x_{1}}\Big(\tfrac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)\Big).

Lemma E.7

(Controlling derivatives of
\log p_{0}
)
.

Let
M
be a Hadamard manifold. Fix
\beta>0
,
m=2
, and
z\in M
, and define

p_{0}(x)\propto\exp\big(-\beta\,d(x,z)^{m}\big).

Fix
t\in[0,1)
,
x_{1}\in M
, and
x\in M
. Define
r:=d(\Psi_{t,x_{1}}(x),z)
, so that
r
represent the radial distance between
x_{0}
and
z
. Then the following bounds hold:

\displaystyle\Big\|\grad_{x}\log p_{0}\Psi_{t,x_{1}}(x)\Big\|\leq

\displaystyle 2\beta r\Big\|(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\tfrac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\Big\|_{\op}\;\frac{1}{1-t}\;\big\|(d\Log_{x_{1}})_{x}\big\|_{\op},

\displaystyle|\partial_{t}\log p_{0}(\Psi_{t,x_{1}}(x))|\leq

\displaystyle 2\beta r\Big\|(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\tfrac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\Big\|_{\op}\;\frac{1}{(1-t)^{2}}\;\big\|\Log_{x_{1}}(x)\big\|,

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{x}^{2}\log p_{0}(\Psi_{t,x_{1}}(x))\|_{\op}\leq

\displaystyle\Big(\Big\|(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\tfrac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\Big\|_{\op}\;\frac{1}{1-t}\;\big\|(d\Log_{x_{1}})_{x}\big\|_{\op}\Big)^{2}

\displaystyle\Big(2\beta+2\beta r\|\nabla^{2}r\|_{\op}\Big|_{\Psi_{t,x_{1}}(x)}\Big)

\displaystyle+\Big\|\nabla(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\tfrac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\Big\|\,\Big(\frac{1}{1-t}\big\|(d\Log_{x_{1}})_{x}\big\|_{\op}\Big)^{2}\,2\beta r

\displaystyle+\Big\|(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\tfrac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\Big\|_{\op}\,\frac{1}{1-t}\,\big\|\nabla(d\Log_{x_{1}})_{x}\big\|\,2\beta r,

\displaystyle\Big\|\partial_{t}\grad_{x}\log p_{0}(\Psi_{t,x_{1}}(x))\Big\|\leq

\displaystyle\Big(\Big\|(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\tfrac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\Big\|_{\op}\;\frac{1}{1-t}\;\big\|(d\Log_{x_{1}})_{x}\big\|_{\op}\Big)

\displaystyle\times\Big(2\beta+2\beta r\|\nabla^{2}r\|_{\op}\Big|_{\Psi_{t,x_{1}}(x)}\Big)

\displaystyle\times\Big\|(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\tfrac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\Big\|_{\op}\;\frac{1}{(1-t)^{2}}\;\big\|\Log_{x_{1}}(x)\big\|

\displaystyle+\Bigg[\frac{1}{(1-t)^{2}}\Big\|(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\tfrac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\Big\|_{\op}\,\big\|(d\Log_{x_{1}})_{x}\big\|_{\op}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{(1-t)^{3}}\,

\displaystyle\big\|\Log_{x_{1}}(x)\big\|\,\Big\|\nabla(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\tfrac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\Big\|\,\big\|(d\Log_{x_{1}})_{x}\big\|_{\op}\Bigg]2\beta r.

where

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{2}r\|_{\op}\Big|_{\Psi_{t,x_{1}}(x)}\leq\frac{s_{K_{\min}}^{\prime}(r)}{s_{K_{\min}}(r)}=\sqrt{-K_{\min}}\coth(r\sqrt{-K_{\min}}).

and note that
r\mapsto r\cosh(r)
has no singularity at
r=0
.

Proof.

We start with deriving bounds for
\|\grad\log p_{0}(\Psi_{t,x_{1}}(x))\|
and
\|\nabla^{2}\log p_{0}(\Psi_{t,x_{1}}(x))\|_{\op}
. Since
\log p_{0}(y)=-\beta\,d(y,z)^{m}+\mathrm{const}
,
and
\|\grad d(\,\cdot\,,z)\|=1
, we have

\|\grad\log p_{0}(\Psi_{t,x_{1}}(x))\|=2\beta r.

Moreover, notice that

\nabla^{2}(d(y,z)^{m})=2d(y,z)\nabla^{2}d(y,z)+2\grad d(y,z)\otimes\grad d(y,z).

The above identity, together with
\|\grad d(y,z)\otimes\grad d(y,z)\|_{\op}=1
, yields

\|\nabla^{2}\log p_{0}(\Psi_{t,x_{1}}(x))\|_{\op}\leq 2\beta+2\beta\,r\,\|\nabla^{2}r\|_{\op}\Big|_{\Psi_{t,x_{1}}(x)}.

We next derive the first (spatial) derivative of
\Psi_{t,x_{1}}
. Using the chain rule,

(d\Psi_{t,x_{1}})_{x}[u]=(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\tfrac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\Big[\tfrac{1}{1-t}\,(d\Log_{x_{1}})_{x}[u]\Big],\qquad\forall\,u\in T_{x}M.

Hence

\displaystyle\|(d\Psi_{t,x_{1}})_{x}\|_{\op}\leq\Big\|(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\tfrac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\Big\|_{\op}\,\tfrac{1}{1-t}\,\big\|(d\Log_{x_{1}})_{x}\big\|_{\op}.

(26)

For any
u\in T_{x}M
, the chain rule gives the identity

\displaystyle\big\langle\grad_{x}\log p_{0}(\Psi_{t,x_{1}}(x)),\,u\big\rangle=\big\langle\grad\log p_{0}(\Psi_{t,x_{1}}(x)),\,(d\Psi_{t,x_{1}})_{x}[u]\big\rangle.

Therefore, by Cauchy-Schwarz with supremum over
\|u\|=1
, yields

\displaystyle\big|\langle\grad_{x}\log p_{0}(\Psi_{t,x_{1}}(x)),u\rangle\big|

\displaystyle\leq\|\grad\log p_{0}(\Psi_{t,x_{1}}(x))\|\,\|(d\Psi_{t,x_{1}})_{x}[u]\|

\displaystyle\leq\|\grad\log p_{0}(\Psi_{t,x_{1}}(x))\|\,\|(d\Psi_{t,x_{1}})_{x}\|_{\op}.

Substituting
\|\grad\log p_{0}(\Psi_{t,x_{1}}(x))\|=2\beta r

and using (
26
), we obtain

\displaystyle\Big\|\grad_{x}\log p_{0}\Big(\Exp_{x_{1}}\big(\tfrac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)\big)\Big)\Big\|

\displaystyle\leq~~

\displaystyle 2\beta r\,\Big\|(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\tfrac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\Big\|_{\op}\;\frac{1}{1-t}\;\big\|(d\Log_{x_{1}})_{x}\big\|_{\op}.

Now we work on the first time derivative.
Recall
\Psi_{t,x_{1}}(x)=\Exp_{x_{1}}\Big(\tfrac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)\Big)
.
By chain rule,

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\log p_{0}(\Psi_{t,x_{1}}(x))=\big\langle\grad\log p_{0}(\Psi_{t,x_{1}}(x)),\,\partial_{t}\Psi_{t,x_{1}}(x)\big\rangle,

where
\partial_{t}\Psi_{t,x_{1}}(x)=(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\tfrac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\Big[\tfrac{1}{(1-t)^{2}}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)\Big]
.
By Cauchy-Schwarz,

\displaystyle|\partial_{t}\log p_{0}(\Psi_{t,x_{1}}(x))|

\displaystyle\leq\|\grad\log p_{0}(\Psi_{t,x_{1}}(x))\|\,\|\partial_{t}\Psi_{t,x_{1}}(x)\|

\displaystyle\leq\|\grad\log p_{0}(\Psi_{t,x_{1}}(x))\|\,\Big\|(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\tfrac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\Big\|_{\op}\,\tfrac{1}{(1-t)^{2}}\,\|\Log_{x_{1}}(x)\|

\displaystyle\leq 2\beta r\Big\|(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\tfrac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\Big\|_{\op}\;\frac{1}{(1-t)^{2}}\;\big\|\Log_{x_{1}}(x)\big\|,

where recall
\|\grad\log p_{0}(\Psi_{t,x_{1}}(x))\|=2\beta r
.

Now we compute the Hessian.
For any
u,w\in T_{x}M
, using chain rule, we obtain

\displaystyle\nabla_{x}^{2}\log p_{0}(\Psi_{t,x_{1}}(x))[u,w]=

\displaystyle\nabla^{2}\log p_{0}(\Psi_{t,x_{1}}(x))\big[(\Psi_{t,x_{1}})_{x}[u],(\Psi_{t,x_{1}})_{x}[w]\big]

\displaystyle+\big\langle\grad\log p_{0}(\Psi_{t,x_{1}}(x)),\,\nabla_{u}\big((\Psi_{t,x_{1}})_{x}[w]\big)\big\rangle.

By Cauchy-Schwarz and taking the supremum over
\|u\|=\|w\|=1
, we obtain

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{x}^{2}\log p_{0}(\Psi_{t,x_{1}}(x))\|_{\op}=\sup_{\|u\|=\|w\|=1}\big|\nabla_{x}^{2}\log p_{0}(\Psi_{t,x_{1}}(x))[u,w]\big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{\|u\|=\|w\|=1}\|\nabla^{2}\log p_{0}(\Psi_{t,x_{1}}(x))\|_{\op}\,\|(\Psi_{t,x_{1}})_{x}[u]\|\,\|(\Psi_{t,x_{1}})_{x}[w]\|

\displaystyle\qquad+\|\grad\log p_{0}(\Psi_{t,x_{1}}(x))\|\,\big\|\nabla_{u}\big((\Psi_{t,x_{1}})_{x}[w]\big)\big\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{2}\log p_{0}(\Psi_{t,x_{1}}(x))\|_{\op}\,\|(\Psi_{t,x_{1}})_{x}\|_{\op}^{2}+\|\grad\log p_{0}(\Psi_{t,x_{1}}(x))\|\,\|\nabla(\Psi_{t,x_{1}})_{x}\|.

It remains to bound
\|\nabla(\Psi_{t,x_{1}})_{x}\|
. Recall that

(\Psi_{t,x_{1}})_{x}[w]=(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\tfrac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\Big[\tfrac{1}{1-t}(d\Log_{x_{1}})_{x}[w]\Big].

Recall the Leibniz rule with
v=\tfrac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)
being a
x
-dependent tangent vector at
T_{x_{1}}M
.

\displaystyle\nabla_{u}\big((\Psi_{t,x_{1}})_{x}[w]\big)

\displaystyle=\nabla_{u}\Big((d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{v}\Big[\tfrac{1}{1-t}(d\Log_{x_{1}})_{x}[w]\Big]\Big)

\displaystyle=\Big(\nabla_{\nabla_{u}v}(d\Exp_{x_{1}})\Big)_{v}\Big[\tfrac{1}{1-t}(d\Log_{x_{1}})_{x}[w]\Big]\;+\;(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{v}\Big[\tfrac{1}{1-t}\,\nabla_{u}\big((d\Log_{x_{1}})_{x}[w]\big)\Big].

where note that
v
depends on
x
, so using chain rule,

\displaystyle\nabla_{u}((d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{v})

\displaystyle=\nabla_{u}((d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\tfrac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)})

\displaystyle=\bigl(\nabla(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\tfrac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\bigr)(\nabla_{u}\tfrac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x))

\displaystyle=\Big(\nabla_{\nabla_{u}\tfrac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}(d\Exp_{x_{1}})\Big)_{\tfrac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}.

Notice that for
v=\tfrac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)
, its first derivative is a directional derivative.
We have

\displaystyle\nabla_{u}v=\tfrac{1}{1-t}(d\Log_{x_{1}})_{x}[u],\qquad\text{and}\qquad\nabla_{u}\big((d\Log_{x_{1}})_{x}[w]\big)=\nabla(d\Log_{x_{1}})_{x}[u,w].

Taking norms and applying the definitions of the operator norms gives

\displaystyle\big\|\nabla_{u}\big((\Psi_{t,x_{1}})_{x}[w]\big)\big\|

\displaystyle\leq\Big\|\nabla(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{v}\Big\|\;\big\|\nabla_{u}v\big\|\;\Big\|\tfrac{1}{1-t}(d\Log_{x_{1}})_{x}[w]\Big\|\;

\displaystyle\qquad+\;\Big\|(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{v}\Big\|_{\op}\;\tfrac{1}{1-t}\;\big\|\nabla(d\Log_{x_{1}})_{x}[u,w]\big\|

\displaystyle=\Big\|\nabla(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{v}\Big\|\;\Big\|\tfrac{1}{1-t}(d\Log_{x_{1}})_{x}[u]\Big\|\;\Big\|\tfrac{1}{1-t}(d\Log_{x_{1}})_{x}[w]\Big\|

\displaystyle\qquad+\Big\|(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{v}\Big\|_{\op}\;\tfrac{1}{1-t}\;\big\|\nabla(d\Log_{x_{1}})_{x}[u,w]\big\|.

Taking supremum over
\|u\|=\|w\|=1
, we obtain

\displaystyle\big\|\nabla\big((d\Psi_{t,x_{1}})_{x}\big)\big\|\leq\Big\|\nabla(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{v}\Big\|\,\Big(\tfrac{1}{1-t}\big\|(d\Log_{x_{1}})_{x}\big\|_{\op}\Big)^{2}+\Big\|(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{v}\Big\|_{\op}\,\tfrac{1}{1-t}\,\big\|\nabla(d\Log_{x_{1}})_{x}\big\|.

Finally using the fact that

\displaystyle\|\grad\log p_{0}(\Psi_{t,x_{1}}(x))\|

\displaystyle=2\beta r,

\displaystyle\quad\|\nabla^{2}\log p_{0}(\Psi_{t,x_{1}}(x))\|_{\op}

\displaystyle\leq 2\beta+2\beta r\|\nabla^{2}r\|_{\op}\big|_{\Psi_{t,x_{1}}(x)},

and using (
26
), we have

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{x}^{2}\log p_{0}(\Psi_{t,x_{1}}(x))\|_{\op}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{2}\log p_{0}(\Psi_{t,x_{1}}(x))\|_{\op}\,\|(\Psi_{t,x_{1}})_{x}\|_{\op}^{2}+\|\grad\log p_{0}(\Psi_{t,x_{1}}(x))\|\,\|\nabla(\Psi_{t,x_{1}})_{x}\|

\displaystyle\leq\;

\displaystyle\Big(\Big\|(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\tfrac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\Big\|_{\op}\;\frac{1}{1-t}\;\big\|(d\Log_{x_{1}})_{x}\big\|_{\op}\Big)^{2}\Big(2\beta+2\beta r\|\nabla^{2}r\|_{\op}\Big|_{\Psi_{t,x_{1}}(x)}\Big)

\displaystyle+\Big\|\nabla(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\tfrac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\Big\|\,\Big(\frac{1}{1-t}\big\|(d\Log_{x_{1}})_{x}\big\|_{\op}\Big)^{2}\,2\beta r

\displaystyle+\Big\|(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\tfrac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\Big\|_{\op}\,\frac{1}{1-t}\,\big\|\nabla(d\Log_{x_{1}})_{x}\big\|\,2\beta r.

It remains to consider the mixed derivative term.
For any
u\in T_{x}M
, recall we have

\displaystyle\big\langle\grad_{x}\log p_{0}(\Psi_{t,x_{1}}(x)),u\big\rangle=\big\langle\grad\log p_{0}(\Psi_{t,x_{1}}(x)),(\Psi_{t,x_{1}})_{x}[u]\big\rangle.

Differentiate both sides in
t
(with
x_{1},x,u
fixed):

\displaystyle\big\langle\partial_{t}\grad_{x}\log p_{0}(\Psi_{t,x_{1}}(x)),u\big\rangle=

\displaystyle\Big\langle\nabla^{2}\log p_{0}(\Psi_{t,x_{1}}(x))\big[\partial_{t}\Psi_{t,x_{1}}(x)\big],\,(\Psi_{t,x_{1}})_{x}[u]\Big\rangle

\displaystyle+\big\langle\grad\log p_{0}(\Psi_{t,x_{1}}(x)),\,\partial_{t}\big((\Psi_{t,x_{1}})_{x}[u]\big)\big\rangle.

By Cauchy-Schwarz,

\displaystyle\big|\langle\partial_{t}\grad_{x}\log p_{0}(\Psi_{t,x_{1}}(x)),u\rangle\big|

\displaystyle\leq\|\nabla^{2}\log p_{0}(\Psi_{t,x_{1}}(x))\|_{\op}\,\|\partial_{t}\Psi_{t,x_{1}}(x)\|\,\|(\Psi_{t,x_{1}})_{x}\|_{\op}\,\|u\|

\displaystyle\quad+\|\grad\log p_{0}(\Psi_{t,x_{1}}(x))\|\,\Big\|\partial_{t}\big((\Psi_{t,x_{1}})_{x}[u]\big)\Big\|.

We already have
\|\partial_{t}\Psi_{t,x_{1}}(x)\|
and
\|(\Psi_{t,x_{1}})_{x}\|_{\op}
from previous steps.

It remains to bound
\|\partial_{t}((\Psi_{t,x_{1}})_{x}[u])\|
.
Recall for any
u\in T_{x}M
,

(\Psi_{t,x_{1}})_{x}[u]=(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{v}\Big[\tfrac{1}{1-t}(d\Log_{x_{1}})_{x}[u]\Big],\qquad v=\tfrac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x).

Fix
x_{1},x,u
and differentiate with respect to
t
.
Since
x
is fixed, the tensor
(d\Log_{x_{1}})_{x}[u]
does not depend on
t
; only the scalar factor

\tfrac{1}{1-t}
and the point
v
depend on
t
.
By the Leibniz rule (product rule for a
t
-dependent linear map applied to a
t
-dependent vector),

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\big((\Psi_{t,x_{1}})_{x}[u]\big)

\displaystyle=\partial_{t}\Big((d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{v}\Big)\Big[\tfrac{1}{1-t}(d\Log_{x_{1}})_{x}[u]\Big]+(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{v}\Big[\partial_{t}\Big(\tfrac{1}{1-t}(d\Log_{x_{1}})_{x}[u]\Big)\Big].

For the first term,
(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{v}
depends on
t
only through
v(t)
, hence the chain rule gives

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\Big((d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{v}\Big)=\Big(\nabla(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{v}\Big)(\partial_{t}v)=\Big(\nabla_{\partial_{t}v}(d\Exp_{x_{1}})\Big)_{v}.

For the second term, since
(d\Log_{x_{1}})_{x}[u]
is
t
-independent,

\partial_{t}\Big(\tfrac{1}{1-t}(d\Log_{x_{1}})_{x}[u]\Big)=\tfrac{1}{(1-t)^{2}}(d\Log_{x_{1}})_{x}[u].

Combining these identities yields the formula

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\big((\Psi_{t,x_{1}})_{x}[u]\big)

\displaystyle=\Big(\nabla_{\partial_{t}v}(d\Exp_{x_{1}})\Big)_{v}\Big[\tfrac{1}{1-t}(d\Log_{x_{1}})_{x}[u]\Big]+(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{v}\Big[\tfrac{1}{(1-t)^{2}}(d\Log_{x_{1}})_{x}[u]\Big].

Noting
\partial_{t}v=\tfrac{1}{(1-t)^{2}}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)
,
we take norms and obtain (for
\|u\|=1
)

\displaystyle\Big\|\partial_{t}\big((\Psi_{t,x_{1}})_{x}[u]\big)\Big\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\Big\|\nabla(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{v}\Big\|\;\|\partial_{t}v\|\;\Big\|\tfrac{1}{1-t}(d\Log_{x_{1}})_{x}[u]\Big\|

\displaystyle\qquad+\Big\|(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{v}\Big\|_{\op}\;\tfrac{1}{(1-t)^{2}}\;\big\|(d\Log_{x_{1}})_{x}\big\|_{\op}\,\|u\|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big\|\nabla(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{v}\Big\|\;\tfrac{1}{(1-t)^{2}}\|\Log_{x_{1}}(x)\|\;\tfrac{1}{1-t}\big\|(d\Log_{x_{1}})_{x}\big\|_{\op}\,

\displaystyle\qquad+\Big\|(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{v}\Big\|_{\op}\;\tfrac{1}{(1-t)^{2}}\;\big\|(d\Log_{x_{1}})_{x}\big\|_{\op}\,

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{(1-t)^{3}}\|\Log_{x_{1}}(x)\|\,\Big\|\nabla(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{v}\Big\|\,\big\|(d\Log_{x_{1}})_{x}\big\|_{\op}

\displaystyle\qquad+\tfrac{1}{(1-t)^{2}}\Big\|(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{v}\Big\|_{\op}\,\big\|(d\Log_{x_{1}})_{x}\big\|_{\op}.

Substituting the expressions for

\|\grad\log p_{0}(\Psi_{t,x_{1}}(x))\|
and
\|\nabla^{2}\log p_{0}(\Psi_{t,x_{1}}(x))\|_{\op}
,
we get

\displaystyle\Big\|\partial_{t}\grad_{x}\log p_{0}\Big(\Exp_{x_{1}}\big(\tfrac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)\big)\Big)\Big\|

\displaystyle\leq\;

\displaystyle\Big(\Big\|(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\tfrac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\Big\|_{\op}\;\frac{1}{1-t}\;\big\|(d\Log_{x_{1}})_{x}\big\|_{\op}\Big)

\displaystyle\times\Big(2\beta+2\beta r\|\nabla^{2}r\|_{\op}\Big|_{\Psi_{t,x_{1}}(x)}\Big)

\displaystyle\times\Big\|(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\tfrac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\Big\|_{\op}\;\frac{1}{(1-t)^{2}}\;\big\|\Log_{x_{1}}(x)\big\|

\displaystyle+\Bigg[\frac{1}{(1-t)^{2}}\Big\|(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\tfrac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\Big\|_{\op}\,\big\|(d\Log_{x_{1}})_{x}\big\|_{\op}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{(1-t)^{3}}\,\big\|\Log_{x_{1}}(x)\big\|\,\Big\|\nabla(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\tfrac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\Big\|\,\big\|(d\Log_{x_{1}})_{x}\big\|_{\op}\Bigg]2\beta r.

Finally, we remark that when
K_{\min}<0
, the Hessian comparison theorem gives

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{2}r\|_{\op}\Big|_{\Psi_{t,x_{1}}(x)}\leq\frac{s_{K_{\min}}^{\prime}(r)}{s_{K_{\min}}(r)}=\sqrt{-K_{\min}}\coth(r\sqrt{-K_{\min}}).

∎

Lemma E.8

(Controlling derivatives of
J_{t}
-terms)
.

Assume
M
is a Hadamard manifold. Fix
x_{1}\in M
,
x\in M
, and
t\in(0,1)
.
Recall

\displaystyle J_{t}(x\mid x_{1})

\displaystyle=(1-t)^{-d}\,\frac{\big|\det(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\frac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\big|}{\big|\det(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\big|},

\displaystyle\log J_{t}(x\mid x_{1})

\displaystyle=-d\log(1-t)+\log\det(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\frac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}-\log\det(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}.

Then the following pointwise bounds hold.

\displaystyle\|\grad_{x}\log J_{t}(x\mid x_{1})\|\leq

\displaystyle d\,\|(d\Log_{x_{1}})_{x}\|\Bigg(\frac{1}{1-t}\,\|\nabla(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\frac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\|+\|\nabla(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\|\Bigg),

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{x}^{2}\log J_{t}(x\mid x_{1})\|_{\op}\leq

\displaystyle d\,\|(d\Log_{x_{1}})_{x}\|^{2}\Bigg[\frac{1}{(1-t)^{2}}\Big(\|\nabla(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\frac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\|^{2}

\displaystyle+\|\nabla^{2}(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\frac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\|\Big)

\displaystyle+\Big(\|\nabla(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\|^{2}+\|\nabla^{2}(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\|\Big)\Bigg]

\displaystyle+d\,\|\nabla(d\Log_{x_{1}})_{x}\|\Bigg(\frac{1}{1-t}\,\|\nabla(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\frac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\|+\|\nabla(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\|\Bigg),

\displaystyle|\partial_{t}\log J_{t}(x\mid x_{1})|\leq

\displaystyle\frac{d}{1-t}+\frac{d}{(1-t)^{2}}\,\|\Log_{x_{1}}(x)\|\;\|\nabla(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\frac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\|,

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}\grad_{x}\log J_{t}(x\mid x_{1})\|\leq

\displaystyle\|(d\Log_{x_{1}})_{x}\|\Bigg[\frac{d}{(1-t)^{2}}\|\nabla(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\frac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\|

\displaystyle+\frac{d}{(1-t)^{3}}\,\|\Log_{x_{1}}(x)\|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\Big(\|\nabla(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\frac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\|^{2}+\|\nabla^{2}(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\frac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\|\Big)\Bigg].

Proof.

We repeatedly use Lemma
E.16
with
A(\cdot)
chosen to be

\xi\mapsto(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\xi}
, and the chain rule through
\Log_{x_{1}}
.

We first estimate the gradient.
From

\log J_{t}(x\mid x_{1})=-d\log(1-t)+\log\det(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\frac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}-\log\det(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\Log_{x_{1}}(x)},

the constant
-d\log(1-t)
has zero
x
-gradient, hence

\grad_{x}\log J_{t}(x\mid x_{1})=\grad_{x}\log\det(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\frac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}-\grad_{x}\log\det(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}.

Consider the first term.
Differentiating the map
x\mapsto\frac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)
yields a factor
\frac{1}{1-t}(d\Log_{x_{1}})_{x}
.
Thus,

\displaystyle\big\|\grad_{x}\log\det(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\frac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\big\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1-t}\,\|(d\Log_{x_{1}})_{x}\|\;\Big\|\grad_{\xi}\log\det(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\xi}\Big\|\Bigg|_{\xi=\frac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}.

Now apply Lemma
E.16
(specifically, the gradient bound in (
33
)) at

A(\xi)=(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\xi}
to get

\Big\|\grad_{\xi}\log\det(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\xi}\Big\|\leq d\,\|(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\xi}^{-1}\|\;\|\nabla(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\xi}\|.

Combining the above, we obtain

\displaystyle\big\|\grad_{x}\log\det(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\frac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\big\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{d}{1-t}\,\|(d\Log_{x_{1}})_{x}\|\,\|(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\frac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}^{-1}\|\;\|\nabla(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\frac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\|.

The same argument (without the factor
\frac{1}{1-t}
) yields

\big\|\grad_{x}\log\det(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\big\|\leq d\,\|(d\Log_{x_{1}})_{x}\|\,\|(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}^{-1}\|\;\|\nabla(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\|.

Finally, apply the triangle inequality,
we get

\displaystyle\|\grad_{x}\log J_{t}(x\mid x_{1})\|\leq d\,\|(d\Log_{x_{1}})_{x}\|\Bigg(\frac{1}{1-t}\,\|(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\frac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}^{-1}\|\;\|\nabla(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\frac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\|

\displaystyle\hskip 160.75789pt+\|(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}^{-1}\|\;\|\nabla(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\|\Bigg).

Now we estimate the Hessian.
Write
f(x):=\log\det(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\frac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}
.
Taking second derivative (with product rule) we have

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{x}^{2}f(x)\|_{\op}\leq

\displaystyle\Big\|\nabla_{\xi}^{2}\log\det(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\xi}\Big\|_{\op}\Bigg|_{\xi=\frac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\cdot\Big\|\frac{1}{1-t}(d\Log_{x_{1}})_{x}\Big\|^{2}

\displaystyle+\Big\|\grad_{\xi}\log\det(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\xi}\Big\|\Bigg|_{\xi=\frac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\cdot\Big\|\nabla\Big(\frac{1}{1-t}(d\Log_{x_{1}})_{x}\Big)\Big\|.

Thus we have (here
\nabla_{\xi}^{2}
represent taking derivative w.r.t.
\xi
)

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{x}^{2}\log J_{t}(x\mid x_{1})\|_{\op}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|(d\Log_{x_{1}})_{x}\|^{2}\Bigg(\frac{1}{(1-t)^{2}}\,\Big\|\nabla_{\xi}^{2}\log\det(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\xi}\Big\|_{\op}\Bigg|_{\xi=\frac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}+\Big\|\nabla_{\xi}^{2}\log\det(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\xi}\Big\|_{\op}\Bigg|_{\xi=\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\Bigg)

\displaystyle+\|\nabla(d\Log_{x_{1}})_{x}\|\,\Bigg(\frac{1}{1-t}\,\Big\|\grad_{\xi}\log\det(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\xi}\Big\|\Bigg|_{\xi=\frac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}+\Big\|\grad_{\xi}\log\det(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\xi}\Big\|\Bigg|_{\xi=\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle d\,\|(d\Log_{x_{1}})_{x}\|^{2}\Bigg[\frac{1}{(1-t)^{2}}\Big(\|(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\frac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}^{-1}\|^{2}\;\|\nabla(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\frac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\|^{2}

\displaystyle+\|(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\frac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}^{-1}\|\;\|\nabla^{2}(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\frac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\|\Big)

\displaystyle+\Big(\|(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}^{-1}\|^{2}\;\|\nabla(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\|^{2}+\|(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}^{-1}\|\;\|\nabla^{2}(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\|\Big)\Bigg]

\displaystyle\quad+d\,\|\nabla(d\Log_{x_{1}})_{x}\|\Bigg(\frac{1}{1-t}\,\|(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\frac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}^{-1}\|\;\|\nabla(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\frac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\|

\displaystyle+\|(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}^{-1}\|\;\|\nabla(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\|\Bigg),

where in the first inequality we used triangle inequality,
and in the second inequality we applied Lemma
E.16
.

For time derivative, since
x
is fixed and only
\frac{1}{1-t}
depends on
t
,

\partial_{t}\log J_{t}(x\mid x_{1})=\frac{d}{1-t}+\partial_{t}\Big(\log\det(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\frac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\Big).

By the chain rule,

\partial_{t}\Big(\log\det(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\frac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\Big)=D\log\det(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\xi}\Big|_{\xi=\frac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\Big[\partial_{t}\Big(\frac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)\Big)\Big].

But
\partial_{t}\big(\frac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)\big)=\frac{1}{(1-t)^{2}}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)
, hence

\Big|\partial_{t}\Big(\log\det(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\frac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\Big)\Big|\leq\frac{1}{(1-t)^{2}}\,\|\Log_{x_{1}}(x)\|\;\Big\|\grad_{\xi}\log\det(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\xi}\Big\|\Bigg|_{\xi=\frac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}.

Apply Lemma
E.16
(gradient bound (
33
)) at

\xi=\frac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)
, we obtain

\displaystyle|\partial_{t}\log J_{t}(x\mid x_{1})|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{d}{1-t}+\frac{d}{(1-t)^{2}}\,\|\Log_{x_{1}}(x)\|\;\|(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\frac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}^{-1}\|\;\|\nabla(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\frac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\|.

For mixed derivative, recall

\grad_{x}\log J_{t}(x\mid x_{1})=\grad_{x}\log\det(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\frac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}-\grad_{x}\log\det(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}.

Only the first term depends on
t
.
Differentiate the first term: there are two contributions,
one from differentiating the prefactor
\frac{1}{1-t}
and one from differentiating

\grad_{\xi}\log\det(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\xi}
at
\xi=\frac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)
.
This yields

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}\grad_{x}\log\det(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\frac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|(d\Log_{x_{1}})_{x}\|\Bigg[\frac{1}{(1-t)^{2}}\,\Big\|\grad_{\xi}\log\det(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\xi}\Big\|\Bigg|_{\xi=\frac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{1-t}\,\Big\|\partial_{t}\Big(\grad_{\xi}\log\det(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\xi}\Big)\Big\|\Bigg|_{\xi=\frac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\Bigg].

Next, note that

\displaystyle\partial_{t}(\grad_{\xi}\log\det(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\xi})=\nabla_{\xi}^{2}\log\det(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\xi}\,[\partial_{t}\xi]

with

\partial_{t}\xi=\frac{1}{(1-t)^{2}}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)
. Hence

\displaystyle\Big\|\partial_{t}\Big(\grad_{\xi}\log\det(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\xi}\Big)\Big\|\leq\frac{1}{(1-t)^{2}}\,\|\Log_{x_{1}}(x)\|\;\Big\|\nabla_{\xi}^{2}\log\det(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\xi}\Big\|_{\op}.

By Lemma
E.16
,
we obtain

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}\grad_{x}\log J_{t}(x\mid x_{1})\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|(d\Log_{x_{1}})_{x}\|\Bigg[\frac{1}{(1-t)^{2}}\,d\,\|(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\frac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}^{-1}\|\;\|\nabla(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\frac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\|

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{(1-t)^{3}}\,\|\Log_{x_{1}}(x)\|\,d\Big(\|(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\frac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}^{-1}\|^{2}\;\|\nabla(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\frac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\|^{2}

\displaystyle+\|(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\frac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}^{-1}\|\;\|\nabla^{2}(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\frac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\|\Big)\Bigg].

Finally, by
Lezcano-Casado 2020
with
r=\frac{\|\Log_{x_{1}}(x)\|}{1-t}
,
and noting that on
\SPD(n)
sectional curvature is upper bounded by zero, we have

\displaystyle\|(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\frac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}^{-1}\|\leq 1.

Hence we get

\displaystyle\|\grad_{x}\log J_{t}(x\mid x_{1})\|\leq

\displaystyle d\,\|(d\Log_{x_{1}})_{x}\|\Bigg(\frac{1}{1-t}\,\|\nabla(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\frac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\|+\|\nabla(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\|\Bigg),

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{x}^{2}\log J_{t}(x\mid x_{1})\|_{\op}\leq

\displaystyle d\,\|(d\Log_{x_{1}})_{x}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\Bigg[\frac{1}{(1-t)^{2}}\Big(\|\nabla(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\frac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\|^{2}+\|\nabla^{2}(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\frac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\|\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad+\Big(\|\nabla(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\|^{2}+\|\nabla^{2}(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\|\Big)\Bigg]

\displaystyle+d\,\|\nabla(d\Log_{x_{1}})_{x}\|\Bigg(\frac{1}{1-t}\,\|\nabla(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\frac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\|+\|\nabla(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\|\Bigg),

\displaystyle|\partial_{t}\log J_{t}(x\mid x_{1})|\leq

\displaystyle\frac{d}{1-t}+\frac{d}{(1-t)^{2}}\,\|\Log_{x_{1}}(x)\|\;\|\nabla(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\frac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\|,

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}\grad_{x}\log J_{t}(x\mid x_{1})\|\leq

\displaystyle\|(d\Log_{x_{1}})_{x}\|\Bigg[\frac{d}{(1-t)^{2}}\|\nabla(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\frac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\|

\displaystyle+\frac{d}{(1-t)^{3}}\,\|\Log_{x_{1}}(x)\|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\Big(\|\nabla(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\frac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\|^{2}+\|\nabla^{2}(d\Exp_{x_{1}})_{\frac{1}{1-t}\Log_{x_{1}}(x)}\|\Big)\Bigg],

completing the proof.
∎

E.4
Bounding
\bigl\|\bigl(\nabla^{2}d\Exp_{p}\bigr)_{rv}\bigr\|_{\op}

In this section, we bound the third derivative of
\Exp
. We first recall some notation in
Lezcano-Casado 2020
, which allows us to write the third derivative as some ODE. Then we apply some comparison theorem to derive the upper bound.
We remark that on a symmetric space, the (covariant) derivative of the curvature tensor is identically zero, see for example
[
Lee 2018
, Theorem 10.19]
. The techniques in this section works for non-symmetric spaces, but as we are working on
\SPD(n)
, we assume
M
is a symmetric space, which would simplify our computation.

Recall that
\nabla
denotes the (Levi–Civita) connection on
TM
over
M
.
Let
\nabla^{\text{flat}}
be the flat connection on the vector space
T_{p}M
.
Set
\Exp_{p}:U\subset T_{p}M\to M
on a normal neighborhood of
p
.

We first introduce pullback connection.
Instead of differentiating along a vector field on
M
, we can also differentiate along a vector field
X
on
T_{p}M
.
Define the corresponding connection as

\displaystyle\bigl(\nabla^{\text{pullback}}_{X}Y\bigr)(u)=\nabla_{\,(d\Exp_{p})_{u}(X(u))}\,Y,

In other words, given
u\in T_{p}M
, and we have
q:=\Exp_{p}(u)
.
Then
differentiate along
X
in the tangent space via the pullback connection, evaluated at
u\in T_{p}M
,
is equivalent to
differentiate along
d\Exp_{p}(X)
(which can be viewed as a vector field on
M
) evaluated at
q
.

Recall the pullback bundle
\Exp_{p}^{*}(TM)
is a vector bundle over the base
T_{p}M
.
Concretely,

\displaystyle\Exp_{p}^{*}(TM):=\{(u,v):u\in T_{p}M,v\in TM\text{ with base point }\Exp_{p}(u)\}.

For example, given an element of the tensor product space
(\alpha,(u,v))\in T_{p}^{*}M\otimes\Exp_{p}^{*}(TM)
, we have

(\alpha,(u,v))(w)=\alpha(w)v
, where
\alpha\in T_{p}^{*}M
is a covector, so that
\alpha(w)\in\mathbb{R}
.

Let
\{e_{i}\}
be an orthonormal basis for
T_{p}M
,
and
\{e^{i}\}
be the corresponding basis in the cotangent space
T_{p}^{*}M
.
By definition,

\displaystyle\big(\sum_{i}e^{i}\otimes(u,(d\Exp_{p})_{u}[e_{i}])\big)(w):=\sum_{i}e^{i}(w)(d\Exp_{p})_{u}[e_{i}],

and by linearity

\displaystyle\sum_{i}e^{i}(w)(d\Exp_{p})_{u}[e_{i}]=(d\Exp_{p})_{u}[\sum_{i}e^{i}(w)e_{i}]=(d\Exp_{p})_{u}(w).

Thus we see that
d\Exp_{p}
can be viewed as an element of the tensor product space:

\displaystyle(d\Exp_{p})_{u}\sum_{i}e^{i}\otimes(u,(d\Exp_{p})_{u}[e_{i}])\in T_{p}^{*}M\otimes\Exp_{p}^{*}(TM).

We equip
T_{p}^{*}M
with the connection induced by
\nabla^{\text{flat}}
and
\Exp_{p}^{*}(TM)

with
\nabla^{\text{pullback}}
. These induce a connection on the tensor product bundle, denoted by

\nabla^{\text{induced}}
, characterized by the Leibniz rule.
Now consider
\overline{W}_{1},\overline{W}_{2}
being vector fields in the tangent space
T_{p}M
,
and
d\Exp_{p}(\overline{W}_{2})
can be viewed as a section, i.e.,

d\Exp_{p}(\overline{W}_{2}):u\mapsto(d\Exp_{p})_{u}(\overline{W}_{2}(u))
.
We differentiate

\displaystyle\nabla^{\text{pullback}}_{\overline{W}_{1}}(d\Exp_{p}(\overline{W}_{2}))=\nabla^{\text{pullback}}_{\overline{W}_{1}}(\sum_{i}e^{i}(\overline{W_{2}})(d\Exp_{p})[e_{i}])

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i}\overline{W}_{1}e^{i}(\overline{W_{2}})(d\Exp_{p})[e_{i}]+\sum_{i}e^{i}(\overline{W_{2}})\nabla^{\text{pullback}}_{\overline{W}_{1}}(d\Exp_{p})[e_{i}].

where we emphasize that
(d\Exp_{p})[e_{i}]
is a function of
u
, similar as before:
(d\Exp_{p})[e_{i}]:u\mapsto(d\Exp_{p})_{u}[e_{i}]
.

By linearity of
(d\Exp_{p})
, and definition of flat connection, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i}\overline{W}_{1}e^{i}(\overline{W_{2}})(d\Exp_{p})[e_{i}]=(d\Exp_{p})[\sum_{i}\overline{W}_{1}e^{i}(\overline{W_{2}})e_{i}]=(d\Exp_{p})[\nabla^{\text{flat}}_{\overline{W}_{1}}\overline{W}_{2}].

On the other hand, since
e^{i}
are constants, we know
\nabla^{\text{flat}}_{\overline{W}_{1}}e^{i}=0
