, so that

\displaystyle e^{i}\otimes\nabla^{\text{pullback}}_{\overline{W}_{1}}(d\Exp_{p})[e_{i}]=

\displaystyle e^{i}\otimes\nabla^{\text{pullback}}_{\overline{W}_{1}}(d\Exp_{p})[e_{i}]+\nabla^{\text{flat}}_{\overline{W}_{1}}e^{i}\otimes(d\Exp_{p})[e_{i}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\nabla^{\text{induced}}_{\overline{W}_{1}}(e^{i}\otimes(d\Exp_{p})[e_{i}]),

hence

\displaystyle\sum_{i}e^{i}(\overline{W}_{2})\otimes\nabla^{\text{pullback}}_{\overline{W}_{1}}((d\Exp_{p})[e_{i}])=\Big(\sum_{i}e^{i}\otimes\nabla^{\text{pullback}}_{\overline{W}_{1}}((d\Exp_{p})[e_{i}])\Big)(\overline{W}_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big(\sum_{i}\nabla^{\text{induced}}_{\overline{W}_{1}}(e^{i}\otimes(d\Exp_{p})[e_{i}])\Big)(\overline{W}_{2})=\sum_{i}\Big(\nabla^{\text{induced}}_{\overline{W}_{1}}(e^{i}\otimes(d\Exp_{p})[e_{i}])\Big)(\overline{W}_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\nabla^{\text{induced}}_{\overline{W}_{1}}(\sum_{i}e^{i}\otimes(d\Exp_{p})[e_{i}]))(\overline{W}_{2})=(\nabla^{\text{induced}}_{\overline{W}_{1}}d\Exp_{p})(\overline{W}_{2}).

Substitute into the previous expression, we obtain

\displaystyle\nabla^{\text{pullback}}_{\overline{W}_{1}}(d\Exp_{p}(\overline{W}_{2}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i}\overline{W}_{1}e^{i}(\overline{W_{2}})(d\Exp_{p})[e_{i}]+\sum_{i}e^{i}(\overline{W_{2}})\nabla^{\text{pullback}}_{\overline{W}_{1}}(d\Exp_{p})[e_{i}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(d\Exp_{p})[\nabla^{\text{flat}}_{\overline{W}_{1}}\overline{W}_{2}]+\nabla^{\text{induced}}_{\overline{W}_{1}}(d\Exp_{p})(\overline{W}_{2})

Thus, for vector fields
\overline{W}_{1},\overline{W}_{2}
on
U\subset T_{p}M
we have

\nabla^{\text{pullback}}_{\overline{W}_{1}}\bigl(d\Exp_{p}(\overline{W}_{2})\bigr)=\bigl(\nabla^{\text{induced}}_{\overline{W}_{1}}d\Exp_{p}\bigr)(\overline{W}_{2})+d\Exp_{p}\bigl(\nabla^{\text{flat}}_{\overline{W}_{1}}\overline{W}_{2}\bigr).

(27)

Define
c(t,s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})=\Exp_{p}(t(v+s_{1}w_{1}+s_{2}w_{2}+s_{3}w_{3}))
, and
\gamma(t)=\Exp_{p}(tv)
. We briefly recall what
Lezcano-Casado 2020
did, to control the second derivative of
\Exp
map.
Define
J_{1}(t)=(d\Exp_{p})_{tv}(tw_{1})
to be the Jacobi field along
\gamma
with initial condition
w_{1}
.
We can define the extension of
J_{1}
in the
w_{2}
-direction by
\widetilde{J}_{1}(t,s):=(d\Exp_{p})_{t(v+sw_{2})}(tw_{1})
, where note that
\widetilde{J}_{1}(t,0)=J_{1}(t)
.
For fixed
t
, the tangent space vector field
u\mapsto tw_{1}
is constant on
T_{p}M
, hence

\nabla^{\text{flat}}_{tw_{2}}(tw_{1})=0.

(28)

Applying (
27
) with
\overline{W}_{1}\equiv tw_{2}
and
\overline{W}_{2}\equiv tw_{1}
, and evaluated at
t(v+sw_{2})
, we have

\bigl(\nabla^{\text{pullback}}_{tw_{2}}\widetilde{J}_{1}(t,s)\bigr)(t(v+sw_{2}))=\bigl(\nabla^{\text{induced}}_{tw_{2}}d\Exp_{p}\bigr)_{t(v+sw_{2})}(tw_{1})+(d\Exp_{p})_{t(v+sw_{2})}\bigl(\nabla^{\text{flat}}_{tw_{2}}(tw_{1})\bigr).

By (
28
) the last term vanishes.
Using the fact
\bigl(\nabla^{\text{pullback}}_{X}Y\bigr)(u)=\nabla_{\,(d\Exp_{p})_{u}(X(u))}Y
with
X=tw_{2}
,
Y=\widetilde{J}_{1}
,
u=tv
, we have (restricted to
s=0
)

\displaystyle\bigl(\nabla^{\text{pullback}}_{tw_{2}}\widetilde{J}_{1}\bigr)(tv)=\nabla_{\,(d\Exp_{p})_{tv}(tw_{2})}\widetilde{J}_{1}.

Together, with the previously defined notation
J_{2}=(d\Exp_{p})_{tv}(tw_{2})
, we have

\displaystyle\nabla_{J_{2}}J_{1}=\nabla_{J_{2}}\widetilde{J}_{1}\big|_{s=0}=\nabla^{\text{pullback}}_{tw_{2}}\widetilde{J}_{1}\big|_{s=0}=\bigl(\nabla^{\text{induced}}d\Exp_{p}\bigr)_{tv}(tw_{1},tw_{2}).

We therefore define the second-order variation field along
\gamma
by

K_{12}(t):=\bigl(\nabla^{\text{induced}}d\Exp_{p}\bigr)_{tv}(tw_{1},tw_{2})=\nabla_{J_{2}}J_{1}.

Then
[
Lezcano-Casado 2020
, Proposition 4.1]
shows that
K_{12}
satisfies

\ddot{K}_{12}+R(K_{12},\dot{\gamma})\dot{\gamma}+Y_{12}=0,\qquad K_{12}(0)=0,\ \dot{K}_{12}(0)=0,

(29)

with
Y_{12}
given explicitly therein.
Using the same technique, we can define

\displaystyle K_{13}(t):=\bigl(\nabla^{\text{induced}}d\Exp_{p}\bigr)_{tv}(tw_{1},tw_{3})=\nabla_{J_{3}}J_{1},K_{23}(t):=\bigl(\nabla^{\text{induced}}d\Exp_{p}\bigr)_{tv}(tw_{2},tw_{3})=\nabla_{J_{3}}J_{2},

satisfying the corresponding ODE as (
29
).

Now we study the third derivative
L_{123}=\bigl((\nabla^{\text{induced}})^{2}d\Exp_{p}\bigr)(tw_{1},tw_{2},tw_{3})
.
Define
J_{i}(t)
to be the Jacobi field along
\gamma
with initial condition
w_{i}
for
i=2,3
, in the same way as we did for
J_{1}
.
Extend
K_{12}(t)=\bigl(\nabla^{\text{induced}}d\Exp_{p}\bigr)_{tv}(tw_{1},tw_{2})=\nabla_{J_{2}}J_{1}
off
\gamma
by

\displaystyle\widetilde{K}_{12}(t,s_{3}):=\bigl(\nabla^{\text{induced}}d\Exp_{p}\bigr)_{t(v+s_{3}w_{3})}(tw_{1},tw_{2}),

so that
\widetilde{K}_{12}(t,0)=K_{12}(t)
.
We then define the third-order variation field along
\gamma
by

L_{123}(t):=\nabla_{J_{3}}K_{12}(t):=\nabla_{J_{3}}\widetilde{K}_{12}(t,s_{3})\Big|_{s_{3}=0}.

(30)

Apply
\nabla^{\text{pullback}}_{\overline{W}_{3}}
to the first-order Leibniz rule
(
27
). For vector fields
\overline{W}_{1},\overline{W}_{2},\overline{W}_{3}
on
T_{p}M
we obtain,
at any
u\in T_{p}M
,

\displaystyle\bigl(\nabla^{\text{pullback}}_{\overline{W}_{3}}\nabla^{\text{pullback}}_{\overline{W}_{1}}(d\Exp_{p}(\overline{W}_{2}))\bigr)(u)

\displaystyle=\bigl((\nabla^{\text{induced}}_{\overline{W}_{3}}\nabla^{\text{induced}}_{\overline{W}_{1}}d\Exp_{p})_{u}\bigr)(\overline{W}_{2}(u))

\displaystyle\quad+\bigl(\nabla^{\text{induced}}_{\overline{W}_{1}}d\Exp_{p}\bigr)_{u}\bigl((\nabla^{\text{flat}}_{\overline{W}_{3}}\overline{W}_{2})(u)\bigr)

\displaystyle\quad+\bigl(\nabla^{\text{induced}}_{\overline{W}_{3}}d\Exp_{p}\bigr)_{u}\bigl((\nabla^{\text{flat}}_{\overline{W}_{1}}\overline{W}_{2})(u)\bigr)

\displaystyle\quad+(d\Exp_{p})_{u}\bigl((\nabla^{\text{flat}}_{\overline{W}_{3}}\nabla^{\text{flat}}_{\overline{W}_{1}}\overline{W}_{2})(u)\bigr).

(31)

Now specialize (
E.4
) to the constant vector fields

\overline{W}_{1}\equiv tw_{2}
,
\overline{W}_{2}\equiv tw_{1}
,
\overline{W}_{3}\equiv tw_{3}
.
Since
\nabla^{\text{flat}}_{tw_{i}}(tw_{j})=0
for all
i,j
, the last three terms in
(
E.4
) vanish. Evaluating at
u=tv
yields

\displaystyle L_{123}(t)=\bigl(\nabla^{\text{pullback}}_{tw_{3}}\widetilde{K}_{12}\bigr)(tv)=\bigl((\nabla^{\text{induced}}_{tw_{3}}\nabla^{\text{induced}}_{tw_{2}}d\Exp_{p})_{tv}\bigr)(tw_{1}),

where note that
(d\Exp_{p})_{tv}(tw_{3})=J_{3}(t)
.

By definition of the second covariant derivative tensor
(\nabla^{\text{induced}})^{2}d\Exp_{p}
, this is precisely

L_{123}(t)=\bigl((\nabla^{\text{induced}})^{2}d\Exp_{p}\bigr)_{tv}(tw_{1},tw_{2},tw_{3}).

(32)

Throughout this section, we consider covariant derivative along
J_{i}
, for example
\nabla_{J_{3}}J_{2}
, as abbreviation of
\nabla_{J_{3}}\tilde{J}_{2}
. Here, the
\tilde{\cdot}
represent the corresponding extension along suitable direction, as constructed above.

Lemma E.9
.

For
L_{123}
defined above, we have

\displaystyle\ddot{L}_{123}+R(L_{123},\dot{\gamma})\dot{\gamma}+Y_{123}=0,\qquad L_{123}(0)=0,\ \dot{L}_{123}(0)=0.

When
M
is a symmetric space,

\displaystyle Y_{123}

\displaystyle=R(\dot{J}_{3},\dot{\gamma})K_{12}+2R(J_{3},\dot{\gamma})\dot{K}_{12}+R(K_{12},\dot{J}_{3})\dot{\gamma}+R(K_{12},\dot{\gamma})\dot{J}_{3}

\displaystyle+2R(\nabla_{J_{3}}J_{1},\dot{\gamma})\dot{J}_{2}+2R(J_{1},\dot{J}_{3})\dot{J}_{2}+2R(J_{1},\dot{\gamma})\nabla_{J_{3}}\dot{J}_{2}

\displaystyle+2R(\nabla_{J_{3}}J_{2},\dot{\gamma})\dot{J}_{1}+2R(J_{2},\dot{J}_{3})\dot{J}_{1}+2R(J_{2},\dot{\gamma})\nabla_{J_{3}}\dot{J}_{1}.

Proof.

Now recall the following ODE is staisfied by
K

[
Lezcano-Casado 2020
, Proposition 4.1.]
.

\displaystyle\ddot{K}_{12}+R(K_{12},\dot{\gamma})\dot{\gamma}+Y_{12}=0.

By differentiating the ODE (note that the vector field to which we take covariant derivative, is viewed as the corresponding extension, i.e.,
\tilde{K}_{12}
for
K_{12}
,
\grad r
for
\dot{\gamma}
and
\nabla_{\grad r}\nabla_{\grad r}\tilde{K}_{12}
for
\ddot{K}_{12}
), we obtain

\displaystyle\nabla_{J_{3}}\ddot{K}_{12}+\nabla_{J_{3}}R(K_{12},\dot{\gamma})\dot{\gamma}+\nabla_{J_{3}}Y_{12}=0,

We first compute
\nabla_{J_{3}}\ddot{K}_{12}
.
Recall
\dot{X}:=\nabla_{\dot{\gamma}}X
and
\ddot{X}:=\nabla_{\dot{\gamma}}\nabla_{\dot{\gamma}}X
.
Let
L_{123}:=\nabla_{J_{3}}K_{12}
.
We compute
\nabla_{J_{3}}\ddot{K}_{12}
step by step.

Recall by definition of curvature tensor

\displaystyle\nabla_{J_{3}}\nabla_{\dot{\gamma}}X=\nabla_{\dot{\gamma}}\nabla_{J_{3}}X+\nabla_{[J_{3},\dot{\gamma}]}X+R(J_{3},\dot{\gamma})X,

and the fact that
[J_{3},\dot{\gamma}]=0
(for the same reason as in
Lezcano-Casado 2020
),
we have

\displaystyle\nabla_{J_{3}}\ddot{K}_{12}

\displaystyle=\nabla_{J_{3}}\nabla_{\dot{\gamma}}\dot{K}_{12}=\nabla_{\dot{\gamma}}\nabla_{J_{3}}\dot{K}_{12}+R(J_{3},\dot{\gamma})\dot{K}_{12}

\displaystyle=\nabla_{\dot{\gamma}}\nabla_{J_{3}}\nabla_{\dot{\gamma}}K_{12}+R(J_{3},\dot{\gamma})\dot{K}_{12}

\displaystyle=\nabla_{\dot{\gamma}}(\nabla_{\dot{\gamma}}\nabla_{J_{3}}K_{12}+R(J_{3},\dot{\gamma})K_{12})+R(J_{3},\dot{\gamma})\dot{K}_{12}=\nabla_{\dot{\gamma}}\nabla_{\dot{\gamma}}L_{123}+\nabla_{\dot{\gamma}}(R(J_{3},\dot{\gamma})K_{12})+R(J_{3},\dot{\gamma})\dot{K}_{12}.

Notice that (by Leibniz rule)

\displaystyle\nabla_{\dot{\gamma}}(R(J_{3},\dot{\gamma})K_{12})

\displaystyle=(\nabla_{\dot{\gamma}}R)(J_{3},\dot{\gamma})K_{12}+R(\nabla_{\dot{\gamma}}J_{3},\dot{\gamma})K_{12}+R(J_{3},\nabla_{\dot{\gamma}}\dot{\gamma})K_{12}+R(J_{3},\dot{\gamma})\nabla_{\dot{\gamma}}K_{12}

\displaystyle=(\nabla_{\dot{\gamma}}R)(J_{3},\dot{\gamma})K_{12}+R(\dot{J}_{3},\dot{\gamma})K_{12}+R(J_{3},\dot{\gamma})\dot{K}_{12}.

Hence we have

\displaystyle\nabla_{J_{3}}\ddot{K}_{12}=\ddot{L}_{123}+(\nabla_{\dot{\gamma}}R)(J_{3},\dot{\gamma})K_{12}+R(\dot{J}_{3},\dot{\gamma})K_{12}+2R(J_{3},\dot{\gamma})\dot{K}_{12}.

Now we compute
\nabla_{J_{3}}\bigl(R(K_{12},\dot{\gamma})\dot{\gamma}\bigr)
. Note that
\nabla_{J_{3}}\dot{\gamma}=\nabla_{\dot{\gamma}}J_{3}=\dot{J}_{3}
, by Leibniz rule,

\displaystyle\nabla_{J_{3}}\bigl(R(K_{12},\dot{\gamma})\dot{\gamma}\bigr)

\displaystyle=(\nabla_{J_{3}}R)(K_{12},\dot{\gamma})\dot{\gamma}+R(\nabla_{J_{3}}K_{12},\dot{\gamma})\dot{\gamma}+R(K_{12},\nabla_{J_{3}}\dot{\gamma})\dot{\gamma}+R(K_{12},\dot{\gamma})\nabla_{J_{3}}\dot{\gamma}

\displaystyle=(\nabla_{J_{3}}R)(K_{12},\dot{\gamma})\dot{\gamma}+R(L_{123},\dot{\gamma})\dot{\gamma}+R(K_{12},\dot{J}_{3})\dot{\gamma}+R(K_{12},\dot{\gamma})\dot{J}_{3}.

Recall

\displaystyle Y:=2R(J_{1},\dot{\gamma})\dot{J}_{2}+2R(J_{2},\dot{\gamma})\dot{J}_{1}+(\nabla_{\dot{\gamma}}R)(J_{2},\dot{\gamma})J_{1}+(\nabla_{J_{2}}R)(J_{1},\dot{\gamma})\dot{\gamma}.

Also recall
[J_{3},\dot{\gamma}]=0
and
\nabla_{J_{3}}\dot{\gamma}=\nabla_{\dot{\gamma}}J_{3}=\dot{J}_{3}
.
Then, by the Leibniz rule for tensor fields, we have

\displaystyle\nabla_{J_{3}}Y

\displaystyle=2\,\nabla_{J_{3}}\big(R(J_{1},\dot{\gamma})\dot{J}_{2}\big)+2\,\nabla_{J_{3}}\big(R(J_{2},\dot{\gamma})\dot{J}_{1}\big)

\displaystyle\quad+\nabla_{J_{3}}\Big((\nabla_{\dot{\gamma}}R)(J_{2},\dot{\gamma})J_{1}\Big)+\nabla_{J_{3}}\Big((\nabla_{J_{2}}R)(J_{1},\dot{\gamma})\dot{\gamma}\Big),

where each term expands as follows.
For the first term,

\displaystyle\nabla_{J_{3}}\big(R(J_{1},\dot{\gamma})\dot{J}_{2}\big)

\displaystyle=(\nabla_{J_{3}}R)(J_{1},\dot{\gamma})\dot{J}_{2}+R(\nabla_{J_{3}}J_{1},\dot{\gamma})\dot{J}_{2}+R(J_{1},\nabla_{J_{3}}\dot{\gamma})\dot{J}_{2}+R(J_{1},\dot{\gamma})\nabla_{J_{3}}\dot{J}_{2}

\displaystyle=(\nabla_{J_{3}}R)(J_{1},\dot{\gamma})\dot{J}_{2}+R(\nabla_{J_{3}}J_{1},\dot{\gamma})\dot{J}_{2}+R(J_{1},\dot{J}_{3})\dot{J}_{2}+R(J_{1},\dot{\gamma})\nabla_{J_{3}}\dot{J}_{2}.

For the second term,

\displaystyle\nabla_{J_{3}}\big(R(J_{2},\dot{\gamma})\dot{J}_{1}\big)

\displaystyle=(\nabla_{J_{3}}R)(J_{2},\dot{\gamma})\dot{J}_{1}+R(\nabla_{J_{3}}J_{2},\dot{\gamma})\dot{J}_{1}+R(J_{2},\nabla_{J_{3}}\dot{\gamma})\dot{J}_{1}+R(J_{2},\dot{\gamma})\nabla_{J_{3}}\dot{J}_{1}

\displaystyle=(\nabla_{J_{3}}R)(J_{2},\dot{\gamma})\dot{J}_{1}+R(\nabla_{J_{3}}J_{2},\dot{\gamma})\dot{J}_{1}+R(J_{2},\dot{J}_{3})\dot{J}_{1}+R(J_{2},\dot{\gamma})\nabla_{J_{3}}\dot{J}_{1}.

For the third term,

\displaystyle\nabla_{J_{3}}\Big((\nabla_{\dot{\gamma}}R)(J_{2},\dot{\gamma})J_{1}\Big)

\displaystyle=(\nabla_{J_{3}}\nabla_{\dot{\gamma}}R)(J_{2},\dot{\gamma})J_{1}+(\nabla_{\dot{\gamma}}R)(\nabla_{J_{3}}J_{2},\dot{\gamma})J_{1}

\displaystyle\qquad+(\nabla_{\dot{\gamma}}R)(J_{2},\nabla_{J_{3}}\dot{\gamma})J_{1}+(\nabla_{\dot{\gamma}}R)(J_{2},\dot{\gamma})\nabla_{J_{3}}J_{1}

\displaystyle=(\nabla_{J_{3}}\nabla_{\dot{\gamma}}R)(J_{2},\dot{\gamma})J_{1}+(\nabla_{\dot{\gamma}}R)(\nabla_{J_{3}}J_{2},\dot{\gamma})J_{1}

\displaystyle\qquad+(\nabla_{\dot{\gamma}}R)(J_{2},\dot{J}_{3})J_{1}+(\nabla_{\dot{\gamma}}R)(J_{2},\dot{\gamma})\nabla_{J_{3}}J_{1}.

For the last term,

\displaystyle\nabla_{J_{3}}\Big((\nabla_{J_{2}}R)(J_{1},\dot{\gamma})\dot{\gamma}\Big)

\displaystyle=(\nabla_{J_{3}}\nabla_{J_{2}}R)(J_{1},\dot{\gamma})\dot{\gamma}+(\nabla_{J_{2}}R)(\nabla_{J_{3}}J_{1},\dot{\gamma})\dot{\gamma}

\displaystyle\qquad+(\nabla_{J_{2}}R)(J_{1},\nabla_{J_{3}}\dot{\gamma})\dot{\gamma}+(\nabla_{J_{2}}R)(J_{1},\dot{\gamma})\nabla_{J_{3}}\dot{\gamma}

\displaystyle=(\nabla_{J_{3}}\nabla_{J_{2}}R)(J_{1},\dot{\gamma})\dot{\gamma}+(\nabla_{J_{2}}R)(\nabla_{J_{3}}J_{1},\dot{\gamma})\dot{\gamma}

\displaystyle\qquad+(\nabla_{J_{2}}R)(J_{1},\dot{J}_{3})\dot{\gamma}+(\nabla_{J_{2}}R)(J_{1},\dot{\gamma})\dot{J}_{3}.

Plug in the expressions, we have

\displaystyle\ddot{L}_{123}+R(L_{123},\dot{\gamma})\dot{\gamma}+Y_{123}=0,

where

\displaystyle Y_{123}

\displaystyle=(\nabla_{\dot{\gamma}}R)(J_{3},\dot{\gamma})K_{12}+(\nabla_{J_{3}}R)(K_{12},\dot{\gamma})\dot{\gamma}+R(\dot{J}_{3},\dot{\gamma})K_{12}+2R(J_{3},\dot{\gamma})\dot{K}_{12}

\displaystyle+R(K_{12},\dot{J}_{3})\dot{\gamma}+R(K_{12},\dot{\gamma})\dot{J}_{3}

\displaystyle+2(\nabla_{J_{3}}R)(J_{1},\dot{\gamma})\dot{J}_{2}+2R(\nabla_{J_{3}}J_{1},\dot{\gamma})\dot{J}_{2}+2R(J_{1},\dot{J}_{3})\dot{J}_{2}+2R(J_{1},\dot{\gamma})\nabla_{J_{3}}\dot{J}_{2}

\displaystyle+2(\nabla_{J_{3}}R)(J_{2},\dot{\gamma})\dot{J}_{1}+2R(\nabla_{J_{3}}J_{2},\dot{\gamma})\dot{J}_{1}+2R(J_{2},\dot{J}_{3})\dot{J}_{1}+2R(J_{2},\dot{\gamma})\nabla_{J_{3}}\dot{J}_{1}

\displaystyle+(\nabla_{J_{3}}\nabla_{\dot{\gamma}}R)(J_{2},\dot{\gamma})J_{1}+(\nabla_{\dot{\gamma}}R)(\nabla_{J_{3}}J_{2},\dot{\gamma})J_{1}+(\nabla_{\dot{\gamma}}R)(J_{2},\dot{J}_{3})J_{1}+(\nabla_{\dot{\gamma}}R)(J_{2},\dot{\gamma})\nabla_{J_{3}}J_{1}

\displaystyle+(\nabla_{J_{3}}\nabla_{J_{2}}R)(J_{1},\dot{\gamma})\dot{\gamma}+(\nabla_{J_{2}}R)(\nabla_{J_{3}}J_{1},\dot{\gamma})\dot{\gamma}+(\nabla_{J_{2}}R)(J_{1},\dot{J}_{3})\dot{\gamma}+(\nabla_{J_{2}}R)(J_{1},\dot{\gamma})\dot{J}_{3}.

On a symmetric space,

\displaystyle Y_{123}

\displaystyle=R(\dot{J}_{3},\dot{\gamma})K_{12}+2R(J_{3},\dot{\gamma})\dot{K}_{12}+R(K_{12},\dot{J}_{3})\dot{\gamma}+R(K_{12},\dot{\gamma})\dot{J}_{3}

\displaystyle+2R(\nabla_{J_{3}}J_{1},\dot{\gamma})\dot{J}_{2}+2R(J_{1},\dot{J}_{3})\dot{J}_{2}+2R(J_{1},\dot{\gamma})\nabla_{J_{3}}\dot{J}_{2}

\displaystyle+2R(\nabla_{J_{3}}J_{2},\dot{\gamma})\dot{J}_{1}+2R(J_{2},\dot{J}_{3})\dot{J}_{1}+2R(J_{2},\dot{\gamma})\nabla_{J_{3}}\dot{J}_{1}.

∎

Now we can apply comparison theory
[
Lezcano-Casado 2020
, Proposition 4.9]
to control
\|L\|
.

Lemma E.10
.

Let
(M,g)
be a Riemannian
symmetric space
, i.e.
\nabla R\equiv 0
.
Fix a unit-speed geodesic
\gamma:[0,r]\to M
with
\|\dot{\gamma}\|\equiv 1
.
Under Assumption
1
, we have

\displaystyle\|L(t)\|\leq\frac{7L_{R}^{2}}{3}s_{K_{\min}}(s)^{5}+16L_{R}^{3}t^{2}s_{K_{\min}}(t)^{5}+20L_{R}^{2}ts_{K_{\min}}(t)^{4}.

Consequently,

\displaystyle\bigl\|\bigl((\nabla)^{2}d\Exp_{p}\bigr)_{rv}\bigr\|_{\op}=\sup_{\|w_{1}\|,\|w_{2}\|,\|w_{3}\|\leq 1}\bigl\|\bigl((\nabla^{\text{induced}})^{2}d\Exp_{p}\bigr)_{rv}(w_{1},w_{2},w_{3})\bigr\|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sup_{\|w_{1}\|,\|w_{2}\|,\|w_{3}\|\leq 1}\frac{1}{r^{3}}\|L_{123}(r)\|\leq\frac{7L_{R}^{2}}{3r^{3}}s_{K_{\min}}(r)^{5}+16L_{R}^{3}\frac{1}{r}s_{K_{\min}}(r)^{5}+20L_{R}^{2}\frac{1}{r^{2}}s_{K_{\min}}(r)^{4}.

Proof.

Recall

\displaystyle Y_{123}

\displaystyle=R(\dot{J}_{3},\dot{\gamma})K_{12}+2R(J_{3},\dot{\gamma})\dot{K}_{12}+R(K_{12},\dot{J}_{3})\dot{\gamma}+R(K_{12},\dot{\gamma})\dot{J}_{3}

\displaystyle\quad+2R(\nabla_{J_{3}}J_{1},\dot{\gamma})\dot{J}_{2}+2R(J_{1},\dot{J}_{3})\dot{J}_{2}+2R(J_{1},\dot{\gamma})\nabla_{J_{3}}\dot{J}_{2}

\displaystyle\quad+2R(\nabla_{J_{3}}J_{2},\dot{\gamma})\dot{J}_{1}+2R(J_{2},\dot{J}_{3})\dot{J}_{1}+2R(J_{2},\dot{\gamma})\nabla_{J_{3}}\dot{J}_{1}.

By definition of
K_{13},K_{23}
, we have
\nabla_{J_{3}}J_{1}=K_{13}
and
\nabla_{J_{3}}J_{2}=K_{23}
.
Next we express
\nabla_{J_{3}}\dot{J}_{i}
in terms of
\dot{K}_{i3}
and curvature.
By definition of
R
and
[J_{3},\dot{\gamma}]=0
,

\displaystyle\nabla_{J_{3}}\dot{J}_{i}=\nabla_{J_{3}}\nabla_{\dot{\gamma}}J_{i}

\displaystyle=\nabla_{\dot{\gamma}}\nabla_{J_{3}}J_{i}+R(J_{3},\dot{\gamma})J_{i}

\displaystyle=\dot{K}_{i3}+R(J_{3},\dot{\gamma})J_{i}.

Therefore

\displaystyle 2R(J_{1},\dot{\gamma})\nabla_{J_{3}}\dot{J}_{2}

\displaystyle=2R(J_{1},\dot{\gamma})\dot{K}_{23}+2R(J_{1},\dot{\gamma})\big(R(J_{3},\dot{\gamma})J_{2}\big),

\displaystyle 2R(J_{2},\dot{\gamma})\nabla_{J_{3}}\dot{J}_{1}

\displaystyle=2R(J_{2},\dot{\gamma})\dot{K}_{13}+2R(J_{2},\dot{\gamma})\big(R(J_{3},\dot{\gamma})J_{1}\big).

Substituting these and using
R(x,y)z\leq L_{R}\|x\|\|y\|\|z\|
, we have

\displaystyle\|Y_{123}\|

\displaystyle\leq 3L_{R}\|K_{12}\|\|\dot{J}_{3}\|+2L_{R}\|J_{3}\|\|\dot{K}_{12}\|+2L_{R}\|K_{13}\|\|\dot{J}_{2}\|+2L_{R}\|K_{23}\|\|\dot{J}_{1}\|

\displaystyle\quad+2L_{R}\|J_{1}\|\|\dot{K}_{23}\|+2L_{R}\|J_{2}\|\|\dot{K}_{13}\|+2L_{R}\|J_{1}\|\|\dot{J}_{2}\|\|\dot{J}_{3}\|+2L_{R}\|J_{2}\|\|\dot{J}_{1}\|\|\dot{J}_{3}\|

\displaystyle\quad+4L_{R}^{2}\|J_{1}\|\|J_{2}\|\|J_{3}\|

\displaystyle\leq 7L_{R}\|K\|\|\dot{J}\|+6L_{R}\|J\|\|\dot{K}\|+4L_{R}\|J\|\|\dot{J}\|^{2}+4L_{R}^{2}\|J\|^{3}.

Using Lemma
E.11
,
[
Lezcano-Casado 2020
, Theorem 3.12, Theorem 4.11]
we have

\displaystyle\|K(t)\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{16}{3}s_{K_{\min}}(t/2)^{2}L_{R}s_{K_{\min}}(t),

\displaystyle\|\dot{K}(t)\|

\displaystyle\leq L_{R}^{2}\frac{16}{3}ts_{K_{\min}}(t)^{3}+2L_{R}s_{K_{\min}}(t)^{2},

\displaystyle\|J(t)\|

\displaystyle\leq s_{K_{\min}}(t),

\displaystyle\|\dot{J}(t)\|

\displaystyle\leq s_{K_{\min}}^{\prime}(t),

hence we can bound

\displaystyle\|Y_{123}\|\leq

\displaystyle 7L_{R}\frac{16}{3}s_{K_{\min}}(t/2)^{2}L_{R}s_{K_{\min}}(t)s_{K_{\min}}^{\prime}(t)+6L_{R}s_{K_{\min}}(t)(L_{R}^{2}\frac{16}{3}ts_{K_{\min}}(t)^{3}+2L_{R}s_{K_{\min}}(t)^{2})

\displaystyle+4L_{R}s_{K_{\min}}(t)(s_{K_{\min}}^{\prime}(t))^{2}+4L_{R}^{2}(s_{K_{\min}}(t))^{3}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{112L_{R}^{2}}{3}s_{K_{\min}}(t/2)^{2}s_{K_{\min}}(t)s_{K_{\min}}^{\prime}(t)+32L_{R}^{3}ts_{K_{\min}}(t)^{4}+20L_{R}^{2}s_{K_{\min}}(t)^{3}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{28L_{R}^{2}}{3}s_{K_{\min}}(t)^{3}s_{K_{\min}}^{\prime}(t)+32L_{R}^{3}ts_{K_{\min}}(t)^{4}+20L_{R}^{2}s_{K_{\min}}(t)^{3}.

We apply
[
Lezcano-Casado 2020
, Proposition 4.9]
.
Consider ODE

\displaystyle\rho^{\prime\prime}(t)+K_{\min}\rho(t)=\eta(t).

Define
y(t)=s_{K_{\min}}(t)
.
Note that
y
solves
y^{\prime\prime}(t)+K_{\min}y(t)=0
.
Then the function
\rho(t)=\int_{0}^{t}y(t-s)\eta(s)ds
satisfies
\rho^{\prime\prime}(t)+K_{\min}\rho(t)=\eta(t)
.
We apply this to each part of the bound on
Y_{123}
:

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}s_{K_{\min}}(t-s)\frac{28L_{R}^{2}}{3}s_{K_{\min}}(s)^{3}s_{K_{\min}}^{\prime}(s)ds

\displaystyle\leq\frac{7L_{R}^{2}}{3}s_{K_{\min}}(t)\int_{0}^{t}4s_{K_{\min}}(s)^{3}s_{K_{\min}}^{\prime}(s)ds=\frac{7L_{R}^{2}}{3}s_{K_{\min}}(s)^{5},

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}s_{K_{\min}}(t-s)32L_{R}^{3}s_{K_{\min}}(s)^{4}sds

\displaystyle\leq 32L_{R}^{3}s_{K_{\min}}(t)\int_{0}^{t}s_{K_{\min}}(s)^{4}sds

\displaystyle\leq 32L_{R}^{3}s_{K_{\min}}(t)^{5}\int_{0}^{t}sds=16L_{R}^{3}t^{2}s_{K_{\min}}(t)^{5},

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}s_{K_{\min}}(t-s)20L_{R}^{2}s_{K_{\min}}(s)^{3}ds

\displaystyle\leq 20L_{R}^{2}ts_{K_{\min}}(t)^{4}.

Hence we can use the following
\tilde{\rho}
as upper bound for
\rho
:

\displaystyle\rho(t)\leq\tilde{\rho}(t):=\frac{7L_{R}^{2}}{3}s_{K_{\min}}(s)^{5}+16L_{R}^{3}t^{2}s_{K_{\min}}(t)^{5}+20L_{R}^{2}ts_{K_{\min}}(t)^{4}.

In other words,

\displaystyle\|L(t)\|\leq\frac{7L_{R}^{2}}{3}s_{K_{\min}}(s)^{5}+16L_{R}^{3}t^{2}s_{K_{\min}}(t)^{5}+20L_{R}^{2}ts_{K_{\min}}(t)^{4}.

∎

Lemma E.11
.

When
M
is a symmetric space, we have

\displaystyle\|\dot{K}(t)\|\leq L_{R}^{2}\frac{16}{3}ts_{K_{\min}}(t)^{3}+2L_{R}s_{K_{\min}}(t)^{2}.

Proof.

Since
K
solves
\ddot{K}+R(K,\dot{\gamma})\dot{\gamma}+Y=0
, we have

\displaystyle\ddot{K}=-R(K,\dot{\gamma})\dot{\gamma}-Y.

Integrate over
[0,t]
, we obtain

\displaystyle\dot{K}(t)-\dot{K}(0)=\int_{0}^{t}\ddot{K}(s)ds=\int_{0}^{t}-R(K,\dot{\gamma})\dot{\gamma}(s)-Y(s)ds.

Using
\|\dot{\gamma}\|=1
and
\dot{K}(0)=0
,
we obtain

\displaystyle\|\dot{K}(t)\|=\|\int_{0}^{t}\ddot{K}(s)ds\|\leq\int_{0}^{t}\|R(K,\dot{\gamma})\dot{\gamma}(s)\|+\|Y(s)\|ds\leq\int_{0}^{t}L_{R}\|K(s)\|+\|Y(s)\|ds.

It suffices to obtain bound for
\|Y\|
and
\|K\|
.
Recall

\displaystyle Y:=2R(J_{1},\dot{\gamma})\dot{J}_{2}+2R(J_{2},\dot{\gamma})\dot{J}_{1}+(\nabla_{\dot{\gamma}}R)(J_{2},\dot{\gamma})J_{1}+(\nabla_{J_{2}}R)(J_{1},\dot{\gamma})\dot{\gamma},

and that for a symmetric space, we have
Recall

\displaystyle Y=2R(J_{1},\dot{\gamma})\dot{J}_{2}+2R(J_{2},\dot{\gamma})\dot{J}_{1}.

Hence

\displaystyle\|Y(s)\|\leq 2L_{R}(\|J_{1}\|\|\dot{J}_{2}\|+\|\dot{J}_{1}\|\|J_{2}\|)\leq 4L_{R}s_{K_{\min}}^{\prime}(s)s_{K_{\min}}(s)=2L_{R}s_{K_{\min}}(2s),

where by
Lezcano-Casado 2020
we have for unit tangent vector
v
,

\|d(\Exp_{p})_{rv}(rw)\|\leq\max\{1,\frac{s_{K_{\min}}(r)}{r}\}r\|w\|,

so that
\|J(s)\|\leq s_{K_{\min}}(s)
.
Also,
Lezcano-Casado 2020
gives

\|\Hess r\|\leq\frac{s_{K_{\min}}^{\prime}(r)}{s_{K_{\min}}(r)},

so that

\|\dot{J}(s)\|\leq\|J\|\|\Hess r\|\leq\frac{s_{K_{\min}}^{\prime}(s)}{s_{K_{\min}}(s)}s_{K_{\min}}(s)\leq s_{K_{\min}}^{\prime}(s)
.

Furthermore,
Lezcano-Casado 2020
implies (with
w
replaced by
rw
)

\displaystyle K(s)\leq\frac{16}{3}s_{K_{\min}}(s/2)^{2}L_{R}s_{K_{\min}}(s).

Hence, we have

\displaystyle\|\dot{K}(t)\|\leq\int_{0}^{t}L_{R}(\frac{16}{3}s_{K_{\min}}(s/2)^{2}L_{R}s_{K_{\min}}(s))+2(L_{R}s_{K_{\min}}(2s))ds.

Note that in our case,
s_{K_{\min}}(t)=\frac{\sinh(\sqrt{|K_{\min}|}t)}{\sqrt{|K_{\min}|}}
,
so we have
s_{K_{\min}}(s/2)\leq s_{K_{\min}}(s)
.

For the first term, we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}L_{R}(\frac{16}{3}s_{K_{\min}}(s/2)^{2}L_{R}s_{K_{\min}}(s))ds

\displaystyle\leq L_{R}^{2}\frac{16}{3}\int_{0}^{t}s_{K_{\min}}(s)^{3}ds\leq L_{R}^{2}\frac{16}{3}\int_{0}^{t}s_{K_{\min}}(t)^{3}ds

\displaystyle=L_{R}^{2}\frac{16}{3}ts_{K_{\min}}(t)^{3}.

For the second term,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}2(L_{R}s_{K_{\min}}(2s))ds\leq 2L_{R}s_{K_{\min}}(t)^{2}.

Put together, we obtain

\displaystyle\|\dot{K}(t)\|\leq L_{R}^{2}\frac{16}{3}ts_{K_{\min}}(t)^{3}+2L_{R}s_{K_{\min}}(t)^{2}.

∎

E.5
Bounds involving Riemannian Log

Here, we obtain bounds on
\|(d\Log_{x})_{y}\|
,
\big\|\big(\nabla(d\Log_{x})\big)_{y}\big\|
,
\|\nabla_{x}\Log_{x}(y)\|_{\op}
,
\|\nabla_{x}\Log_{x}(y)\|_{\op}^{2}
,
|\Div_{x}\Log_{x}(y)|
, and
|\Div_{x}\Log_{x}(y)|^{2}
. We also obtain a bound
\|\grad\Div_{x}\Log_{x}(y)\|
when the manifold is
\SPD(n)
. In this section, for different differential operators
d
and
\nabla
, note that

\nabla\Log_{x}(x_{1})
is covariant derivative (viewing
\Log_{x}(x_{1})
as a vector field, with
x_{1}
being fixed. On the other hand,
d\Log_{x_{1}}
is considering fixed base point.

Before presenting our results, we also recall some facts. Let
M
be a Hadamard manifold of dimension
d
. Fix
x\in M
and let
y\in M
,
y\neq x
.
Then:

(i)

\|\Log_{x}(y)\|=d(x,y)
.

(ii)

\grad_{x}\Big(\frac{1}{2}d(x,y)^{2}\Big)=-\Log_{x}(y)
.

(iii)

\Div_{x}\Log_{x}(y)=-\Delta_{x}\Big(\frac{1}{2}d(x,y)^{2}\Big)
.

Lemma E.12

(Bounds for
d\Log_{x}
and
\nabla(d\Log_{x})
)
.

Let
M
be a Hadamard manifold of dimension
d
. Fix
x\in M
.
Then, we have

\displaystyle\|(d\Log_{x})_{y}\|

\displaystyle\leq 1,

\displaystyle\big\|\big(\nabla(d\Log_{x})\big)_{y}\big\|

\displaystyle\leq\big\|\big(\nabla(d\Exp_{x})\big)_{\Log_{x}(y)}\big\|.

Proof.
On a Hadamard manifold,
\Exp_{x}:T_{x}M\to M
is a global diffeomorphism, hence
\Log_{x}=\Exp_{x}^{-1}
is smooth on
M
.
Let
u=\Log_{x}(y)
. Then
\Exp_{x}(u)=y
and the inverse function theorem gives

(d\Log_{x})_{y}=\big((d\Exp_{x})_{u}\big)^{-1}=\big((d\Exp_{x})_{\Log_{x}(y)}\big)^{-1}.

Take norm on both sides and recall
\big\|\big((d\Exp_{x})_{\Log_{x}(y)}\big)^{-1}\big\|\leq 1
, we get the first bound.

Now we differentiate the identity

(d\Exp_{x})_{\Log_{x}(\cdot)}\circ(d\Log_{x})_{\cdot}=Id_{T_{\cdot}M}

covariantly at
y
in the direction
w_{1}\in T_{y}M
, and then apply the resulting operator to
w_{2}\in T_{y}M
.
Using Leibniz rule and that
\nabla(Id)=0
, we get

\big(\nabla(d\Exp_{x})\big)_{\Log_{x}(y)}\Big[(d\Log_{x})_{y}w_{1},\,(d\Log_{x})_{y}w_{2}\Big]+(d\Exp_{x})_{\Log_{x}(y)}\Big(\big(\nabla(d\Log_{x})\big)_{y}[w_{1},w_{2}]\Big)=0.

Re-arrange the terms, we get

\displaystyle\big(\nabla(d\Log_{x})\big)_{y}[w_{1},w_{2}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\big((d\Exp_{x})_{\Log_{x}(y)}\big)^{-1}\Big(\big(\nabla(d\Exp_{x})\big)_{\Log_{x}(y)}\Big[\big((d\Exp_{x})_{\Log_{x}(y)}\big)^{-1}w_{1},\,\big((d\Exp_{x})_{\Log_{x}(y)}\big)^{-1}w_{2}\Big]\Big).

Take norms, and recalling that
\|\big((d\Exp_{x})_{\Log_{x}(y)}\big)^{-1}\|\leq 1
,
we obtain

\displaystyle\big\|\big(\nabla(d\Log_{x})\big)_{y}\big\|\leq\big\|\big(\nabla(d\Exp_{x})\big)_{\Log_{x}(y)}\big\|.

∎

Lemma E.13

(Bounds for
\|\nabla_{x}\Log_{x}(y)\|
)
.

Let
M
be a Hadamard manifold of dimension
d
with sectional curvature bounded below by
K_{\min}\leq 0
.
Fix
x\in M
and
y\in M
,
y\neq x
. Then

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{x}\Log_{x}(y)\|_{\op}

\displaystyle\leq 1+d(x,y)\,\frac{s_{K_{\min}}^{\prime}(d(x,y))}{s_{K_{\min}}(d(x,y))},

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{x}\Log_{x}(y)\|_{\op}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2+2\,d(x,y)^{2}\Big(\frac{s_{K_{\min}}^{\prime}(d(x,y))}{s_{K_{\min}}(d(x,y))}\Big)^{2}.

Proof.

Let
r(\cdot):=d(\cdot,y)
, so
r(x)=d(x,y)
.
Since
r\,\grad_{x}r=-\Log_{x}(y)
(since
\grad_{x}(\frac{1}{2}r^{2})=r\grad_{x}r
),
we have

\Log_{x}(y)=-\,r(x)\,\grad_{x}r(x).

Differentiate covariantly in
x
, we obtain

\nabla_{x}\Log_{x}(y)=-\nabla_{x}\big(r(x)\,\grad_{x}r(x)\big)=-\big(\nabla_{x}r(x)\big)\otimes\grad_{x}r(x)\;-\;r(x)\,\nabla_{x}\grad_{x}r(x).

Taking operator norms and using
\|\grad_{x}r(x)\|=1
gives

\|\nabla_{x}\Log_{x}(y)\|_{\op}\leq\|\grad_{x}r(x)\|^{2}+r(x)\,\|\nabla\grad_{x}r(x)\|_{\op}=1+d(x,y)\,\|\nabla\grad_{x}r(x)\|_{\op}.

By Hessian comparison under
\Sec\geq K_{\min}
, we have

\displaystyle\|\nabla\grad_{x}r(x)\|_{\op}\leq\frac{s_{K_{\min}}^{\prime}(d(x,y))}{s_{K_{\min}}(d(x,y))}.

Combining the above estimates yields the first inequality.
The squared bound follows from
(a+b)^{2}\leq 2a^{2}+2b^{2}
with
a=1
and

b=d(x,y)\,\frac{s_{K_{\min}}^{\prime}(d(x,y))}{s_{K_{\min}}(d(x,y))}
.
∎

Lemma E.14

(Bounds for
\Div_{x}\Log_{x}(y)
and
|\Div_{x}\Log_{x}(y)|^{2}
)
.

Let
M
be a Hadamard manifold of dimension
d
with sectional curvature bounded below by
K_{\min}\leq 0
.
Fix
x\in M
and
y\in M
,
y\neq x
. Then

\displaystyle|\Div_{x}\Log_{x}(y)|

\displaystyle\leq 1+(d-1)\,d(x,y)\,\frac{s_{K_{\min}}^{\prime}(d(x,y))}{s_{K_{\min}}(d(x,y))},

\displaystyle|\Div_{x}\Log_{x}(y)|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2+2(d-1)^{2}\,d(x,y)^{2}\Big(\frac{s_{K_{\min}}^{\prime}(d(x,y))}{s_{K_{\min}}(d(x,y))}\Big)^{2}.

Proof.

Let
r(\cdot):=d(\cdot,y)
.
Then we have
\Div_{x}\Log_{x}(y)=-\Delta_{x}\Big(\frac{1}{2}r(x)^{2}\Big)
. Using the product rule for Laplacian, we have

\Delta(\frac{1}{2}r^{2})=\langle\grad r,\grad r\rangle+r\Delta r=1+r\Delta r
.
For the upper bound, Laplacian comparison theorem under
\Sec\geq K_{\min}
gives

\displaystyle\Delta r\leq(d-1)\,\frac{s_{K_{\min}}^{\prime}(d(x,y))}{s_{K_{\min}}(d(x,y))}.

Substitute into
|\Div_{x}\Log_{x}(y)|=\Delta_{x}(\frac{1}{2}r^{2})

we obtain

|\Div_{x}\Log_{x}(y)|\leq 1+(d-1)\,d(x,y)\,\frac{s_{K_{\min}}^{\prime}(d(x,y))}{s_{K_{\min}}(d(x,y))}.

Finally, using
(a+b)^{2}\leq 2a^{2}+2b^{2}
with

a=1
and
b=(d-1)\,d(x,y)\,\frac{s_{K_{\min}}^{\prime}(d(x,y))}{s_{K_{\min}}(d(x,y))}
gives the desired results.
∎

Moreover, on
\SPD(n)
, we have the following results.

Lemma E.15

(
\|\grad\Div\Log\|
bound on
\SPD(n)
)
.

Let
\SPD(n)
be the manifold of real symmetric positive-definite matrices endowed with the
affine-invariant Riemannian metric

\langle U,V\rangle_{P}:=\Tr\big(P^{-1}UP^{-1}V\big),\qquad U,V\in T_{P}\SPD(n)=Sym(n).

Fix
y\in\SPD(n)
and define

r(x):=d(x,y),\qquad f(x):=d(x,y)^{2}=r(x)^{2},\qquad d:=\dim(\SPD(n))=\frac{n(n+1)}{2}.

Then we have

\displaystyle\|\grad\Div\Log\|\leq\frac{\sqrt{2}d}{2}\left(2\Big(1+r\,\frac{s_{K_{\min}}^{\prime}(r)}{s_{K_{\min}}(r)}\Big)\right)^{\frac{3}{2}}.

Proof.
We first show that
\SPD(n)
is a totally geodesic submanifold of
PD(n,\mathbb{C})
under the affine-invariant metric.
Let
\PD(n,\mathbb{C})
denote the manifold of complex Hermitian positive-definite matrices endowed with the
same affine-invariant metric

\langle U,V\rangle_{P}=\Tr(P^{-1}UP^{-1}V)
.
Define a smooth isometry by

\displaystyle\sigma:\PD(n,\mathbb{C})\to\PD(n,\mathbb{C}),\qquad\sigma(P):=\overline{P}.

Notice that
\sigma
is an isometry because for any
P\in\PD(n,\mathbb{C})
and
U,V\in Herm(n)
,

\langle d\sigma_{P}[U],d\sigma_{P}[V]\rangle_{\sigma(P)}=\Tr\big(\overline{P}^{-1}\,\overline{U}\,\overline{P}^{-1}\,\overline{V}\big)=\overline{\Tr(P^{-1}UP^{-1}V)}=\Tr(P^{-1}UP^{-1}V)=\langle U,V\rangle_{P},

where we used that
\Tr(P^{-1}UP^{-1}V)\in\mathbb{R}
for Hermitian
P,U,V
.
Thus we see that the fixed-point set of
\sigma
is exactly
\SPD(n)
. According to
Kobayashi 1972
we have that the fixed-point set of an isometry is a (embedded) totally geodesic submanifold.
Hence
\SPD(n)
is totally geodesic in
\PD(n,\mathbb{C})
.

Let
F(P):=d_{\PD(n,\mathbb{C})}(P,y)^{2}
be the squared distance to
y\in\SPD(n)\subset PD(n,\mathbb{C})
,
considered as a function on
PD(n,\mathbb{C})
.
By
Hirai et al. 2023
,
F
is
2
-self-concordant on
\PD(n,\mathbb{C})
: we have that for all
P\in\PD(n,\mathbb{C})
and
u,v,w\in T_{P}\PD(n,\mathbb{C})
,

|(\nabla^{3}F)_{P}(u,v,w)|\leq\sqrt{2}\,\sqrt{(\nabla^{2}F)_{P}(u,u)}\,\sqrt{(\nabla^{2}F)_{P}(v,v)}\,\sqrt{(\nabla^{2}F)_{P}(w,w)}.

Now restrict
F
to the totally geodesic submanifold
\SPD(n)
. By definition, for a totally geodesic submanifold, the second fundamental form vanishes, and Gauss formula
[
Lee 2018
, Theorem 8.2]
implies that the connection
\nabla
on
\SPD(n)
is induced by that of
\PD(n,\mathbb{C})
.
Moreover, the geodesic distance function on
\SPD(n)
is induced by that on
\PD(n,\mathbb{C})
, so we know self-concordance of distance squared also holds on
\SPD(n)
.

Now we make use of the self-concordance property to bound
\|\grad\Div\Log\|
. Let
f(x)=d_{\SPD}(x,y)^{2}
and note that on the Hadamard manifold
\SPD(n)
,

\|\grad_{x}\Div_{x}\Log_{x}(y)\|=\tfrac{1}{2}\|\grad_{x}\Delta_{x}f(x)\|.

Let
\{e_{i}\}_{i=1}^{d}
be an orthonormal basis of
T_{x}\SPD(n)
. Such an orthonormal basis can be extended to a neighborhood of
x
, and we obtain an orthonormal frame:
\nabla e_{i}|_{x}=0,\forall i

[
Lee 2018
, Exercise 5-21]
. Thus we can differentiate
\Delta f
as follows.
For any unit
w\in T_{x}\SPD(n)
,

\langle\grad\Delta f,w\rangle=\sum_{i=1}^{d}(\nabla^{3}f)(w,e_{i},e_{i})+2\nabla^{2}f(\nabla_{w}e_{i},e_{i})=\sum_{i=1}^{d}(\nabla^{3}f)(w,e_{i},e_{i}).

By self-concordance, we have

|(\nabla^{3}f)(w,e_{i},e_{i})|\leq\sqrt{2}\,\sqrt{(\nabla^{2}f)(w,w)}\,(\nabla^{2}f)(e_{i},e_{i})
.
Therefore

\displaystyle\|\grad\Div\Log\|\leq\frac{\sqrt{2}d}{2}\,\sqrt{\|\nabla^{2}f\|_{\op}^{3}}.

Writing
f=r^{2}
, we have

\nabla^{2}f=2\,dr\otimes dr+2r\,\nabla^{2}r,\qquad\Delta f=2+2r\,\Delta r,

using
\|\grad r\|=1
.
Under
\Sec\geq K_{\min}
,
Hessian comparison gives
\|\nabla^{2}r\|_{\op}\leq\frac{s_{K_{\min}}^{\prime}(r)}{s_{K_{\min}}(r)}
, hence

\|\nabla^{2}f\|_{\op}\leq 2+2r\,\|\nabla^{2}r\|_{\op}\leq 2\Big(1+r\,\frac{s_{K_{\min}}^{\prime}(r)}{s_{K_{\min}}(r)}\Bigr).

Substituting this in the previous estimate yields

\displaystyle\|\grad\Div\Log\|\leq\frac{\sqrt{2}d}{2}\left(2\Big(1+r\,\frac{s_{K_{\min}}^{\prime}(r)}{s_{K_{\min}}(r)}\Big)\right)^{1.5}.

∎

E.6
Auxiliary results

Given a matrix
A(x)
, the directional derivative in
v
can be computed through

\displaystyle D\log\det(A(x))[v]=\tr(A^{-1}DA(x)[v]).

Hence

\displaystyle\|\nabla\log\det(A(x))\|_{\op}=\sup_{\|v\|=1}|\tr(A^{-1}DA(x)[v])|\leq d\|A^{-1}\|\times\|DA(x)\|_{\op}.

Furthermore, using the product rule,
and noting that
DA^{-1}(x)=-A^{-1}(x)DA(x)A^{-1}(x)
, we obtain

\displaystyle D^{2}\log\det(A(x))[v,v]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tr(DA^{-1}(x)[v]DA(x)[v])+\tr(A^{-1}(x)D^{2}A(x)[v,v])

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\tr(A^{-1}(x)DA(x)[v]A^{-1}(x)DA(x)[v])+\tr(A^{-1}(x)D^{2}A(x)[v,v]).

Hence, we have

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{2}\log\det(A(x))\|_{op}\leq d\|A^{-1}(x)\|^{2}\times\|\nabla A(x)\|^{2}+d\|A^{-1}(x)\|\times\|\nabla^{2}A(x)\|.

This is summarized as the following result, and is used in Lemma
E.8
.

Lemma E.16

(Derivatives of
\log\det
)
.

Let
A(x)
be a smooth family of invertible linear maps on a
d
-dimensional inner product space.
Then for any unit vector
v
,

D\log\det(A(x))[v]=\tr\big(A(x)^{-1}DA(x)[v]\big).

Consequently,

\displaystyle\|\nabla\log\det(A(x))\|

\displaystyle=\sup_{\|v\|=1}\big|\tr\big(A(x)^{-1}DA(x)[v]\big)\big|\leq d\,\|A(x)^{-1}\|\,\|\nabla A(x)\|,

(33)

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{2}\log\det(A(x))\|_{\op}

\displaystyle\leq d\,\|A(x)^{-1}\|^{2}\,\|\nabla A(x)\|^{2}+d\,\|A(x)^{-1}\|\,\|\nabla^{2}A(x)\|.

(34)
